# Tidewatch - ledger

A running log of what got done, newest first. The plan lives in [PLAN.md](PLAN.md).

## Status

- **Current milestone:** v0.1.19: tabs named by their work, agents that draw avatars again, YOLO as a new tab default with the reason a level is refused, take-over that groups worktrees under their repo, test-command detection for bun and deno, and a Running elsewhere card that hides sessions already moved.
- **Done:** Phases 0 to 10; v0.1.0 to v0.1.19 released (Homebrew cask, Linux tarball and AppImage); the K mark identity; the showcase page.
- **Also:** six habit workflows are built in, and Build workflow from this session (palette; the agent proposes a workflow that opens as an unsaved draft), 2026-10-16. Restart to update keeps every shell and agent running (live handoff, 2026-10-15). The receiving side of stdusk's "Move tabs to Tidewatch" is built (#49).
- **Next:** the diff widgets go teal (the graph already does on the side layout); commit messages that truncate early in the narrow tree; the divider position saved between runs; the site hero screenshot; the owner's live checks; Linux desktop runs.

## 2026-11-01

- **Plan limits in the status bar (branch `feat/plan-limits`).** The owner picked limits A on the agents and limits board ("artifact looks solid"). Claude Code hands its plan limits only to a status line command, so every Claude tab's `--settings` file now names `tidewatch --statusline <tab state dir>` (`statusLine` with the owner's `padding` kept). That command saves `rate_limits.five_hour` and `rate_limits.seven_day` (`used_percentage` 0 to 100, `resets_at` unix seconds) and `cost.total_cost_usd` to `<tab state>/plan-limits.json`, then runs the owner's own status line command (found in the user, project and local Claude settings, the most specific key by key, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` honoured) with the same input, folder and environment and passes its output and exit code through; no owner command prints nothing. Checked on claude 2.1.316: the field names come from the binary and from the input of a live session (`cost` is there from the first screen, `rate_limits` only after the first answer, as its own docs say); a real `claude --settings` run showed the owner's starship footer through the pass-through, so `--settings` replaces the owner's `statusLine` command. The app reads the newest `plan-limits.json` of its agent tabs on a `Jobs` thread at most every 5 s on frames that happen anyway and feeds save mode, which now acts on real readings; the status bar shows `5h` and `week` meters at the right (teal, orange from 80 percent, tooltips `5-hour limit 31%, resets in 2h 10m` and `Week limit 84%, resets Thu 14:00`, none for a window with no reading or a reset in the past, the fullest alone on a narrow bar), the left mark is now the active tab's tokens, and a tab's tooltip adds `$20.51 this session` once the cost is a cent. `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_LIMITS` stays for captures and `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_TOKENS` also fakes the cost. Numbers: workspace tests 5621 -> 5670 passing, clippy and fmt clean; `--statusline` takes a median 8.4 ms in a release build (1.8 ms is the cost of starting any process here; 14.7 ms with a trivial owner command, most of it the owner's `sh`) and 11.1 ms in a debug build. Not done: a capture of a meter's tooltip (`TIDEWATCH_DEV_HOVER` does not open tooltips in a background run), a plan name (`Claude Max`) is not read.
- **A live list of the agents a session runs, above the reply box (branch `feat/running-agents`).** Owner: "Can tidewatch show [Claude Code's footer with main and four subagents] running agents in any way?", board pick "agents A". `tidewatch_core::subagents` reads `<session id>/subagents/agent-<id>.meta.json` and the agent's own transcript (checked on disk first: only a running agent lacks `finished`, but 2,200 older metas never had the key, so the last assistant `stop_reason` and ten silent minutes decide too; foreground agents get a meta file as well, only `requestShape` differs). A look runs on a `Jobs` thread when the transcript is first known, every 2 s while the tab's agent works or a listed agent runs, and never otherwise; a transcript is read from where the last look stopped. The card shows what each agent does now, `sonnet · high`, time and tokens (`token_usage`, once per message id, so cache reads count as in the tab's own total), `done 4m 01s` for a minute after it ends, Hide per tab, six rows then `+N more`. The tab tooltip and the Overview card say `4 agents running`. Rows are not clickable: the Reader's parser drops `isSidechain` lines and its view is bound to the tab's rows and reply box, so a read-only subagent view would be a second Reader. Open: a real live session was not captured, only the fake (`TIDEWATCH_FAKE_SUBAGENTS`) and the fixtures cut from a real pair.
- **The question card opens folded to one line (branch `feat/question-fold`).** Owner: "the option select is so big I can't see what the message behind it is ... it should be collapsed by default since I first need to read before I decide", pick "question A". A live question (Claude's menu or an `ask_user` call) now opens as one 44 px line over the reply box: orange dot, `<agent> asks:` and the question cut with an ellipsis, `3 options` (and ` · picks in 0:45` in a tab that runs on its own, the countdown keeps running) and an Answer button; the whole question is its tooltip. Answer, a click on the line or Enter while the reply box is empty opens today's full card, which got a chevron Fold button in its header. A folded line takes no keys (1-9, G, Space, arrows, Cmd+Enter), the next question opens folded again, and the chat gets the room the card no longer takes and stays pinned to the bottom when it was following. `Live` carries the fold state (`question_view.rs`), `reader_view.rs` is untouched except one test. Dev: `TIDEWATCH_DEV_ASK=1` (stand-in question) and `open` / `fold` steps for `TIDEWATCH_DEV_QUESTION`. Tests: 12 new (3 of them in `dev_reader.rs` and `reader_view.rs`), the 6 old card tests now open the card first. Checked with the screenshot switch on a stand-in claude with a hand-written transcript: folded and open in a 1280 and a 600 point window. Not changed, seen in the narrow capture: the open card's footer hint spills past a column under about 330 points (it did before this change).

- **The buttons on your own messages are one icon toolbar under the bubble (branch `feat/bubble-actions`).** Owner: "These floating actions are also shitty ... we should sexify them", pick "Merge both, actions A". Send again, Copy and Edit and send were three loose bordered buttons drawn over the bubble's top edge; now one small card under the bubble, right-aligned with it, holds three 28 px icon buttons (pencil, copy, circular arrow) with a tooltip each, and Copy shows a teal check for 1.5 s after a click. The card floats on its own layer in the gap that is already there, so the bubble and its `You` line do not change. Every own message gets 16 px of room under it (`bubble_toolbar::ROOM`, also in `reader_scroll::USER_CHROME_H`), so the hovered toolbar never covers the next row's `You · time` line or the reply box's top. Moving from the bubble down to the card keeps it shown. A message the agent never showed keeps its `Not seen by the agent` words and gets Send again and Dismiss (an x) in the same card, always shown. The meta line no longer hides itself in a narrow column. New module `bubble_toolbar.rs`; tests 5528 -> 5538 (the old label-based tests now find the icons by id, plus placement, clear of the next `You` line and the reply box, hover bridge, tooltip and Copy tests). Checked with the screenshot switch on a stand-in claude with a hand-written transcript: hovered message, the Copy tooltip and a not-seen message. Not tried by hand: a real click on Copy in the built app (the headless test proves the clipboard text, the check state is not captured).

- **`set_avatar` works whenever it is called (branch `fix/avatar-always`).** The owner: "Is the icon thing fixed? I noticed even running it manually does nothing..." and "I want everything to be possible." Cause: since 17b0e0c7 the tool was listed but refused every call with "The owner has not asked for an icon for this tab. Wait until they click Draw avatar." unless the tab had been armed by Draw avatar, Redraw, a pending redraw ask or the hook's icon ask, so telling an agent in chat to set its icon did nothing. The refusal, `ToolCtx.avatar`, the listener's per-tab arming (`Entry.avatar`, `allow_avatar`, the store in the hook ask) and `avatar_redraw::Waiting::held` (only used to arm) are gone; the once-per-tab icon ask, Draw avatar and Redraw are unchanged. `Credential.avatar` is removed too: serde ignores unknown fields here, and a test loads an old credential JSON with `"avatar": true`. Tests replaced one for one (a fresh tab with no click saves, in `mcp.rs` and `listener_http.rs`; the hook ask goes once after a goal and to no other tab) plus the old-credential test: 5563 passing, 0 failed (5562 before). Not verified: a real agent told in chat to set its icon (no signed-in model here).
- **Kebab-case tab names (branch `feat/kebab-tab-names`).** `set_name` (module `tab_name`, event `TabName`) is offered to every agent, not only a Meeseeks; the name is kept on the tab (`AgentTab.chosen_name`), saved in `session.toml` (`chosen_name`, old files load with none) and carried through a restart handover. `tab_title::pick` reads the owner's `/rename` first, as written (the transcript parser now tells `custom-title` from `ai-title`: `custom_title`, a new `Message::OwnTitle` from the feed), then the chosen name, a Meeseeks task slug, a `branch_name::slug` of the ledger goal, and only then the generated session title, terminal title and first message; an empty slug falls through. A goal such as "Soundtrack backfill blind spots" reads `soundtrack-backfill-blind` (the slug cap of 30 characters drops the last word). The launch text stays short, so the ask for a name rides the next `UserPromptSubmit` answer: once a Claude tab's saved report has a goal and the tab has no name, one line asks for `set_name` (`name_ask.rs`, a `name-asked` marker written first and also when the tab names itself). Numbers: workspace tests 5527 -> 5546, clippy 1.99.0 and fmt clean. Captured with `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_TAB_NAMES=1` plus `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_PARKED=visible` (strip) and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_OVERVIEW=1`. Not done: a Meeseeks saved with a name before this version has no `name-asked` marker and is asked once more.
- **Icons from a bundled set (branch `feat/icon-set`).** The owner picked "icons A": agents drew icons before they knew the task and most did not match it, so `set_avatar` now takes `icon`, a name from the bundled Lucide set (lucide-static 1.59.0, ISC), and `color`, one of teal, blue, green, amber, orange, red, pink or purple, and Tidewatch draws it; `svg` stays for a task no icon fits. The pack is `crates/tidewatch-core/assets/lucide/icons.pack` (2159 names with 268 older aliases sharing bytes, 389 kB, with Lucide's LICENSE beside it; `scripts/make-icons.py` rebuilds it). The picture goes through the same `avatar::clean` and `avatar_check::check` as a drawn one, so every screen draws it unchanged; the view box is padded (`-4 -4 32 32`) because Lucide's square icons otherwise spill past the circle the check measures (the app's fitted drawing does not depend on it). An unknown name is a tool error with up to 8 close names, and icon with svg is an error. The tool text and the Draw avatar ask say to pick the one object the goal is about and name it in the reason. The About card credits Lucide beside Noto Emoji. Captures: `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_AVATARS=icons` (music, shield, refresh-cw, database, bug, rocket, book-open). Numbers: 5527 tests before, see the hand-back for after (0 failed). New tabs ask for their icon again (owner: new agents no longer had avatars drawn automatically; the quiet pass had dropped the ask and `set_avatar` was refused until Draw avatar): the first `UserPromptSubmit` answer after a Claude tab's saved report has a goal carries one line (`avatar_ask::CONTEXT`: the Lucide icon of the one object the goal is about, as icon with a color, the object named in the reason), once per tab (`avatar-asked` marker, written first), never for a Meeseeks, a tab with a picture or a host that writes nothing, and the ask turns `set_avatar` on for that tab; nothing is typed into the input box and the house style has no avatar line. Not done: no live agent called the new tool (the tests drive `set` and the RPC path); no keyword (tags) search of the icon names beyond prefix and contains; `signal-zero` and `wifi-zero` are refused as nearly empty.
- **A `!` shell command no longer stays Queued, and its output is no longer raw tags (branch `fix/bash-mode-queue`).** Cause checked against the real transcript: Claude Code writes a `!` command as a user line `<bash-input> CMD</bash-input>` and its result as `<bash-stdout>...</bash-stdout><bash-stderr>...</bash-stderr>`. The queued record held `! CMD`, which never matched that line, so it waited for the 15-minute cutoff, and `user_markup::classify` did not know the bash tags, so the output drew as raw text in a user bubble. Now `classify` reads the input as a `Command` `! CMD` (the existing slash-command row) and the output as a `CommandOutput` (colors removed, both empty hidden), and `Rows::settle_sent` counts a `! ` command row as a user line, so the queued message is claimed when it arrives. Numbers: tests 3364 -> 3365 in the app crate (core stays 1883, its two table tests gained rows). Checked in the built app with a stand-in `claude` that writes the three lines after 4 s: with the line 60 s away the `! pscale ...` message shows Queued, with the line in the Reader shows the command row and its output and nothing queued. Not changed: a queued slash command such as `/clear` has the same gap (its row is not a user line), not verified in a real run and left alone.

## 2026-10-31

- **The showcase page and README for 0.1.49 (branch `docs/site-0.1.49`).** The page still said v0.1.22 and described Workflows, a Delivery step where Tidewatch pushes, and "agent tabs block git push"; the README said the same about pushing and about four fixed stages. Both now match main: no workflows (the new-tab kinds are Agent, Meeseeks, Shell; `restore.rs` retires saved workflow tabs), the agent pushes and opens its own pull request when asked (`pr_ask.rs`, `delivery_card.rs`), the push guard refuses only the default branch and tags, the Progress panel in the order of phase 9 with stages the agent names, and the done check running the repo's tests. Added: the one send line, files in the sentence, live links with chips, tokens per tab, Meeseeks, the profile editor and the repo's way of working. Save mode is in the README only, with the note that it has no plan limit to read yet. Shots retaken in a throwaway home with stand-in `claude` and `gh` scripts and a neutral demo repo: hero, new tab, progress, palette, and a new Links shot; `shot-workflows.png` is gone. Checked headless at 1280 and 390 wide: no horizontal scroll, all 12 images and every local link resolve. Not retaken: the overview, needs, changes and search shots (older look, nothing on the page contradicts them).

- **Phase 11: Live links (branch `feat/live-links`).** The Links card of the Progress panel now follows GitHub as it happens. The forwarder also asks for `issues` events, and every webhook that names a pull request or issue of a repo the window holds brings one `gh` lookup, shared by every tab that holds a link to it, two seconds after the news (a burst costs one lookup per link per 2 s); links to other repos are polled every 5 minutes while their tab is in front and every 30 otherwise, and a repo `gh` cannot read or a deleted pull request stops the looking and reads `no access` or `gone`. A link keeps more than its state: draft, checks (pass, failing, running), review (approved, changes requested), conflicts, an issue's close reason, comment count and GitHub's update time; each row shows pills (what wants you first, then how it stands, `live` last), a meta line with the repo, `3 comments`, `updated 2m ago` and `checked 12m ago` where no forwarder delivers, and the tab's own pull request takes the watch's status. A board of 4 links or more has the decided chips: type (All, PRs, Issues, Artifacts, Docs and web) and state (Open, Needs action, Done), each with a count that follows the other chips, and a search box; rows sort needs action first, then open by last change, then done, so the merged and closed fold is gone; a filter that keeps nothing says `No open PRs · Show all`. A link that begins to need action (checks fail, changes requested, conflicts) lights the orange dot on the Progress switch and says nothing in the chat. "Links from all tabs" waits for a later build, as decided. Numbers: 45 more test functions than at the base (core 2,092 to 2,106, app 3,207 to 3,238); the workspace passes (3,211 in the app, 1,847 in the core library), clippy 1.99.0 and fmt clean. Captures with `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_LINKS=1` and the new `TIDEWATCH_DEV_LINKS=<type>,<state>,<search>,all`. Not verified: the `gh` error texts that sort a failure into no access or gone come from the GraphQL messages as documented, not from a live call.

- **Phase 8 of the voice review: files in the sentence (branch `feat/files-in-sentence`).** A picture pasted with Cmd+V, a file dropped and an `@` pick each land at the text cursor as a chip inside the sentence, never at the end or in a chip row above the box, and the sent message carries each path at that same place (`The numbers in /tmp/q3.pdf don't match '/tmp/My Shot.png', can you check?`). The box text holds each file as a token of private-use marks around the hex of its path (`tokens.rs`), so a draft, a recalled message and a held one keep them with no second list; the layout leaves a chip-wide gap and `token_view.rs` paints the chip (a thumbnail for a picture, a glyph for a file or folder, a hover preview, click to open in a sent bubble). Backspace and Delete take a token whole, the cursor never rests inside one, a solid caret shows where a dragged file will go, and a drop with no keyboard in the box goes at the end. `@` opens the repo's files (git's list, fuzzy match, name before folders) and a pick becomes a token (`at_files.rs`, `at_files_view.rs`, `files_glue.rs`); the same box in the new-tab form works the same. The sent bubble turns each path of something on the disk, and each `[Image #N]` of a transcript picture, back into its chip in place; a message sent by an older version shows its trailing paths the same way. Picked from the board with the owner away: drops outside the box still go at the end (the cursor when the box has the keyboard), the `Attached:` line and the chip row are gone, and a Meeseeks task gets the files put in its own line, not every task. Dev switches for captures: `TIDEWATCH_DEV_BOX=<text with |>`, `_ENTER=<s>`, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_CLIPBOARD_PNG=<png>` (a stand-in clipboard, with `_IMAGE_PASTE`). Numbers: 3250 tidewatch tests on the base, 3277 after (0 failed). Not done: no real Cmd+V and no real Finder drag were tried (both need a focused window); the captures run the same job, stage and insert code from a stand-in clipboard and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_DROP`. A message the agent shows with the path kept (no marker) is turned back into a chip only while the file still exists.

- **Phase 10: Token budget (branch `feat/token-budget`).** Each agent tab's tokens come from its transcript: Claude's `message.usage` counted once per message id (a multi-block message repeats on every line with the same usage, identical on all 1433 lines of a real 2.1.316 transcript) and Codex's running `token_count` total differenced; Gemini and Copilot fixtures carry no counts, so they show none. The tab tooltip and the Overview card say `1.2M tokens · 96% cached · 18 turns` and `Tidewatch cost this tab 14k tokens (4 messages)` (the turns that typed Tidewatch messages started, matched by text and time); the card shows the short form at the right of its second line when it has 150 px of room, and the status bar shows the active tab's tokens. Save mode (Settings, General, Token budget, on by default as decided: "Save mode on by default at 80%, telling you once") holds Tidewatch's own automatic sends, reminders and new Meeseeks while a plan limit reading is at 80 percent, tells once per window, and never touches a hand send, an owner's review or a pick for a waiting agent. Plan limits: not done. Claude Code gives `rate_limits.five_hour.used_percentage` (found in the 2.1.316 binary) to its status line command only; no file on disk holds it (`~/.claude` has none), and the other route is the OAuth usage endpoint with the owner's token. So no real run has a limit reading: the status bar shows limits and save mode starts only with `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_LIMITS=62@2h,85@3d`, and the Settings card says save mode has nothing to watch. Numbers: 5192 tests before, 5236 after (0 failed), clippy 1.99.0 and fmt clean. Not verified: the tab tooltip's tokens lines were checked by screenshot only (no headless test of the strip's tooltip), and a toast that long was not seen on screen (it fades before the capture).
- **Phase 9: the Progress panel in the board's order (branch `feat/progress-panel-order`).** Top to bottom: the goal, Waiting on you (an amber card with the question and "Answer above the reply box"; the chips stay in the chat), Now (state word, the agent's note or its current step, `3 of 9 steps · updated 6m ago · 3 answers since`, the lead job's bar; never hidden), Waiting for (the job, PR, date or event on its own line, also while a job runs), one row of stage bars, the steps by stage (evidence on its own line under a done step, `checked` when Tidewatch ticked it or ran what it names, `claimed` otherwise; a blocked reason in amber and wrapped in full), Coming up (the report's scheduled items by date, drawn for the first time), Decisions, Not verified and Verified (first four, then `Show N more`; Verified folded), the done check (the chat's Done, Next and Needs you lines are no longer copied into the panel) and Links last. A new question puts the orange dot on the Progress switch even while History shows. The walk fix: a question the agent asked with its question tool and the owner answered is a `QuestionAnswered` fact (from the transcript result or the card or chip that answered), counted since the report as "N answers since", and `step_tick::matching` ticks the open step it is about (marked by Tidewatch, a blocked step too); the question card is the live state because Waiting on you reads the laid-over question, not the last report. Fixes: an answer clears the question and the wait together; a report older than the running turn shows dimmed under "From the last turn" and its old note leaves Now; no heading without content; every fold opens and closes and resets when its list shrinks; Meeseeks tabs have one row of stage bars with the checks as a stage; below 360 px labels stack over values, text wraps, bars keep a whole label, and the job, link and Meeseeks rows have a minimum text width (a headless test draws every block at 200, 280 and 360 px). Numbers: 5195 tests on the base, 5327 after the rebase onto phase 5 (0 failed), clippy 1.99.0 and fmt clean; a long goal wraps (the out of date mark goes under it when it has no room). Not done: nothing writes `Fact::Check` for a command Tidewatch ran except the done check, so `checked` is mostly a Tidewatch tick until the Proof phase's runs log theirs.
- **Phase 7 of the voice review: one send line (branch `feat/one-send-line`).** Step 13 of the signed-off board, option A, without the `@` files row (phase 8). A reply you type no longer goes straight into the terminal: `reader_glue::reply` asks the one gate (`Outbox::offer` with `Send::reply`), and while the agent works Tidewatch holds the message in `ViewState.line` and types it when the turn ends, one per idle moment, never merged with the one before. The held messages sit in a card at the bottom of the chat (`held_view.rs`) with Edit (inline field, Enter saves, Esc cancels), Delete, Send now (Esc, then the message) and Up and Down; Up in an empty box edits the newest one. Also: history (Up and Down in an empty box, Esc returns to the draft, 100 per tab in `reply-history.json`), drafts kept (`reply-draft.json`, written after 0.8 s still), a Stop button beside Send and Esc in an empty box, Send again, Copy and Edit and send on your own bubble, the view follows the newest message after a send, the reason line above a shut box (`Agent is not running · Resume`, also while it holds text; replaces the top bar in the Reader), a queued message the transcript never shows reads `Not seen by the agent` after 2 minutes with Send again and Dismiss (long pastes match now), the card of a Claude question closes when its menu stays off the screen for 2 s, and the outbox never drops the oldest silently, says why a line was cleared, and gives up a wait on a half-typed draft after 120 s. Dev switches for captures: `TIDEWATCH_DEV_HELD`, `_WORKING`, `_ROWS`, `_NOT_SEEN`, `_QUEUED`, `_HELD_EDIT`, `_BLOCKED`. Not done: held messages are not kept across a restart (the draft and history are).

- **Phase 5: What you see (branch `feat/what-you-see`).** Every Tidewatch row in the Reader says why (`Because Auto fixes conflicts on this PR · 1 of 10`) and has its buttons: Change workflow (opens Settings, General) for a message the profile worded, and the switch of its kind (failed checks, reviews, conflicts, push, the done check, the comment guard for this tab). Auto's first conflict, failed check or review fix in a repo waits as a row with the exact words and Send, Change wording and Cancel; one Send and that kind goes on its own (`auto-seen.toml`; a tab that runs on its own is never held up). The reminders of older versions leave no row. A question Tidewatch answered for the owner keeps its card in Needs you with a Review button until the owner opens the tab; a held `ask_user` call gets `Tidewatch picked "X" for you after 5 minutes. The user may change it.` as its tool result (`Reply::PickedForYou`), Claude's own menu gets it as a follow-up through the send gate. Step 10 wording: a review reads `Review from you (3 notes). Fix each one, {fold each fix into its commit}, then answer every note with reply_to_note.` and a skipped question tells the agent to go with its recommended answer and say which one. Step 9: when the owner's own comment-guard hook is in the Claude settings, the tab's settings file leaves Tidewatch's comment hooks out and the strip says so. Numbers: 5154 tests on the phase 3 base, see the hand-back for the count on the final base.

- **Unify round, part 3: the profile editor is one kind of control (branch `feat/unify-profiles`).** The owner picked row 04 A on the unify board ("Segmented controls, one-line rows as the mock; Agent stays a segmented control") and "I want everything to be possible", so every effort (Default, Low, Medium, High, Extra high, Max), every permission level, Delete, Revert, Save and the Advanced disclosure stay. Agent, Effort and Permission level are `segmented::inline_or_chips` controls under their field labels (chips in a narrow card, YOLO in red ink), the four tall level cards are gone, and under Effort and Permission one muted 13 px line says what the selected value does. The header is the face, the name and `opus · medium · Auto edit`, with a `...` button at the right edge that holds Delete in danger ink, so the title no longer meets a button at 900x640. Two overflows the new 900x640 test found are fixed in the shared pieces: the save bar's message ran under Revert and the Advanced label ran past the card; both now cut or wrap to the room. Numbers: tidewatch tests 3179 -> 3184 (the level-card and option-card tests went, the segmented, header menu, description and layout tests came), clippy and fmt clean. `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PROFILE_MENU=1` opens the header menu for captures.

- **Unify round, part 2: a selected row is a tint, lists are one line, chips are outlines (branch `feat/unify-rows`).** The owner picked A on the Unify board and, on the selected look: "Just one question can we rethink this selection status it's a very AI pattern" then "A: tint and teal name". `selection_row` is now a `SEGMENT_ON` tint with no bar, and `selected_ink` gives the name its teal; the palette, emoji and slash lists, font picker, Settings sidebar, repo menu, service cards, diff file list and commit files follow. The new-tab lane, Change this tab (every profile, scrolling past 8) and Recently closed are one line each (`paint_face_line`: round 20 px face, name, muted detail at the right, key cap at the far right; the lane drops a detail that has under 48 px left, Recently closed keeps it), and the CLI badge is round everywhere. Strip chips (update, leftovers, comment guard) are `status_chip`s: Compact outline, status dot, no fill; "Update stuck" and "Fix" lose the dot separator. The comment guard popover has a 40 px header (`N flagged comments`, Turn off for this tab) and calm rows. The Changes tab has 13 px section labels with a count pill (zero muted), shared rows and a closing note under a short list; the commit card lost its teal outline and bar and its files are rows. Paths on Settings pages show with `~` on one line, cut in the middle with the whole path as a tooltip, and a Copy button where one stands alone (House style, page footers); job, Meeseeks and Recently closed metadata is in the regular font. Numbers: 5184 tests before, 5195 after. Not done: the diff gutter and the sideways scrollbar live in kelp-ui's diff body (`ScrollArea::both`), so they stay for a change in `../kelp`. The comment guard strip keeps its own Turn off link beside the new popover button.
- **Unify round, part 1: one label, one dialog frame, a short trust question, leftovers as a summary (branch `feat/unify-dialogs`).** The owner picked A on every row of the unify board. Labels: forms, dialogs and popovers share `screen_kit::field_label` (13 px semibold soft, sentence case); Settings pages keep their section labels, and the menu group labels of the earlier menus round are untouched. Dialogs: 480, 640 or 820 px (`DialogWidth`), one 48 px header, one footer row with the hint as key caps and words with a dot between them; Open a repo's buttons share that row, long paths in a dialog are cut at their start with the whole path as a tooltip. Trust: the first-launch dialog is the question, one line per CLI and a "Show the exact lines added" disclosure, never taller than the window less 48 px (`dialog_pinned`: header and footer stay, the body scrolls); the Folder trust page has the same lines. Leftovers: a summary line, one-line rows (dot, mono name, age or the reason at the right), rules behind "What counts as safe", footer Close and "Clean up N"; a row that is not safe keeps its Discard buttons; `Leftover.modified_ms` carries the folder's age. Fixes with no pick: the profile editor's subtitle and level cards at 900x640, chips that wrap spread evenly (YOLO no longer alone), the new-tab message box needs 72 px and its hint is one sentence, `-0` is muted, the services hint no longer names a Start the dialog lacks, Workflow card title, the no-restore note under the Startup card, the About card's `Panel on time` label, no zero counts in the Overview summary. Numbers: 5184 tests on the base, 5212 after (0 failed), clippy 1.99.0 and fmt clean, each fix shown failing without it except where the headless harness does not reproduce the geometry (the new-tab form at 900x640 was checked by screenshot). Not done: the list rows, profile editor restyle, comment guard, Changes tab, paths on Settings pages and strip chips (the second worker), the `menu_label` group labels, a count in the dialog headers.
- **Phase 4: One way to ask, and the tool texts (branch `feat/one-way-to-ask`).** A question an agent asks with its question tool now fills the tab's `needs_you` itself: a Claude `AskUserQuestion` menu (read from its session file) or a held `ask_user` call becomes Needs you lines (question, the decision as why, each option as an answer, the pick marked) laid over the shown ledger ahead of whatever the agent wrote itself, and answering the question takes them out again. The Overview card shows the real question and its chips for a Claude menu, and a chip click answers the menu through its keys. The report digest the panel log keeps reads only what the agent sent, so Tidewatch never logs a report the agent did not write. The end block stays as it was: nothing compares its Needs you line with the report any more (the chat-question nudge went in phase 1). Step 11: `update_progress` no longer takes the markdown `ledger` field (a tool error that names the typed fields, alone or beside them; old `progress.md` files still load) and its description is the board's three sentences; new `get_workflow` returns the repo's profile (catch-up, fixes, pull requests and the four rule sentences); `ask_user` is hidden from Claude (its `AskUserQuestion` is read; Codex, Gemini and Copilot keep it, since no call of their own question tool is read); a `set_avatar` call is refused until Draw avatar or Redraw armed that tab (it stays in `tools/list`, since a running session never hears of a new tool). Numbers: 5154 tests on the base, 5168 after (0 failed), clippy 1.99.0 and fmt clean. Open: the profile has three settings, so `get_workflow` has no reviews, commits or tests fields; a multi-question call leaves the Overview chips off (they answer one question).

- **Phase 6: Proof (branch `feat/proof`).** The Step 7 checks are five switches in each repo's workflow profile (Settings, General, under the Workflow card; saved in `workflow.toml`). On by default: tests run by Tidewatch, verified means rerunnable, scope. Off by default: a fix comes with a test. When an agent says it is done, the done check runs the repo's test command in the tab's own worktree and the card shows `Ran cargo test --workspace: passed in 134 s` or `2 failed`; a failed run goes to the agent as "Tidewatch ran <command>: N failed (output below). Fix them, <fold from the profile>, and say done again." with the end of the output. Auto waits for a passing run before it asks the agent to push and starts the check itself when none ran. A verified item with no command reads `claimed, not checked`, and files changed outside what the plan named are listed, both in amber with nothing sent. A branch that fixes something and adds no test is sent back when its switch is on. Numbers: 5241 tests pass on the branch rebased onto main 03a691d0 (0 failed; 34 of them new, counted against 5154 on the phase 3 base), clippy 1.99.0 and fmt clean. Not done: the fresh reviewer (needs `report_finding` offered to a Meeseeks in `mcp.rs`, which phase 4 owns, and a link from a builder tab to the reviewer tab it opens), `checked` against `claimed` marks on the panel's Verified list (phase 9).

- **Phase 3: Quiet (branch `feat/quiet`).** The launch text of a Claude agent tab is 340 words with the default house style (was 1,645 on the owner's tab; tested at 450 or fewer): the house style, then `# Working in Tidewatch` with a two-paragraph Report, Asking the user, Long jobs and, for a worktree tab, This repo's workflow filled from the workflow profile (`workflow_detect::profile_for`, `Workflow::launch_lines`). A test holds that no built-in text names a method (rebase, merge, fixup, draft, force-with-lease) unless the profile filled it. Dropped, each with a test that it is no longer sent: the missing-report and "the user answered" prompt lines, the automatic `set_avatar` ask and its `avatar-asked` marker (Draw avatar still asks), the one-hour stale-report nudge (`progress_nudge.rs` is gone; phase 1 had removed the mid-turn ones), and the two REPORT GUARD messages, now one line at the end of a turn: "Before you stop: your progress report wasn't updated this turn, or still says working. Send it with the final state." The Due today ask no longer says to update the report; a failed Meeseeks test run or hand-off no longer says how to commit; the YOLO and Meeseeks blocks use the approved wording ("You run without supervision", Tidewatch picks the recommended answer after 5 minutes). Numbers: 5154 tests pass (5184 before; the report example, nudge and prompt-line tests went with the text they covered), clippy and fmt clean. Open: an existing `house-style.md` keeps its old text until Reset to default; no new face drawing for a tab without an avatar (it keeps the letter).

- **Phase 1 slice 3: the panel screens read the tab's panel log and show every block's age (branch `feat/panel-screens`).** The Progress panel, the Overview cards and the History card header now read the tab's `PanelView` instead of the old live path (`live_of`, `answered_asks`, `AgentRun::rested_at` and the Shadow/Verdict compare are gone). Every block shows its age (`now`, `2m ago`), a turn whose source went quiet reads `no word since 14:35` in the header and on the Overview card, a report the facts contradict gets an `out of date` tag, and a step Tidewatch marked itself reads `by Tidewatch`. The About card has `Panel on time: 99.96% this week` and the late count per source (`panel_counter.rs`, `.state/panel-ontime.json`). The Quiet and Question nudges are gone: the report is asked for only at a turn's end, per the board. Dev switches `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_PANEL=fresh|outdated|quiet|since` and `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_ONTIME=<percent>`. The owner approved the screenshots. Numbers: 5143 tests on the base, 5184 after (0 failed), clippy 1.99.0 and fmt clean. Not checked: `by Tidewatch` on a real session (nothing writes a Tidewatch step mark yet), Linux; ages refresh only on a frame; the week is UTC.

- **Calm menus and popovers (branch `feat/menus-calm`).** The owner asked to "sexify" the menus and picked "A: Calm (one-line rows, colour square, check)" on the board. The repo chip menu is one line per repo (colour tile, name, tab count, a teal check on the repo on show) with Open repo, Add service and Close under a divider. The Parked, services and Amphetamine popovers share one row look: a section label, a status dot in the reason's colour, actions on the hovered row (services: Restart and `...`; a crashed row keeps Start with a warm tint; Logs and Stop moved into `...`, which opens beside the list so it covers no row), durations as rows with a clock. The command palette has a search header with a count, results under group labels with one icon each, key caps for shortcuts and a hint strip. Overview Working cards give the title the whole first line and a short time (`12m`); a one-button Needs you card puts its button on the title row and keeps its own height. New dev switch `TIDEWATCH_DEV_REPO_MENU=1`. Numbers: tidewatch tests 2988 -> 3022 passed, clippy and fmt clean. Not checked: live hover in a window, Linux.

- **The new-tab form's first message is the Reader's message box (branch `fix/new-tab-composer`).** The owner: "Also here on first message we should have a text input no? Like I should have the same experience as in the reader, else how do I attach images and files." The form's box was a plain text field. It is now the shared `composer` in a new `Mode::Field { height }`: same border, corners, 16 px font and placeholder, pictures and files as chips (Cmd+V of a picture, files copied in Finder, files dropped), `:` emoji and `/` commands (Claude, the repo's skills), no Send, plain Enter a new line (so one task per line still works for a Meeseeks) and Cmd+Enter still starts. On Start the first message is `pictures::message(text, chips)`, the same lines a reply sends; a Meeseeks gives every task the same files. A draft has no tab folder, so its files stage in `.state/drafts/<seed>/pastes` (`TidewatchPaths::draft_dir`), removed when the draft closes or starts as a shell and kept for a started agent. The form's Esc now leaves an open slash or emoji list to the box. Numbers: tests tidewatch 2957 -> 2973 passed, core 1674 -> 1675. Not done: files of a draft still open when the window quits stay in `.state/drafts`.

- **Phase 1 slice 2: the panel's log is fed from every source, and a turn whose hook was lost still ends.** `panel_host.rs` keeps one numbered log per agent tab (`panel-log.jsonl`) and appends hooks, `ask_user` calls, the transcript (`transcript_marks.rs` finds where a turn begins and ends in Claude, Codex, Copilot and, weakly, Gemini; `transcript_facts.rs` turns a read into `Turn`, `Said`, `Tool*`, `Ask*` facts and drops history older than the tab), terminal changes (output coalesced to one a second), lifecycle (`Started`, `Resumed`, `KeptRunning` over a restart), the process probe (`agent_process.rs`, one `ps` every 2 s, only while a turn is believed open), reports, jobs, links, finished checks and pull requests. When the log shows the turn started or over and the hook never came, the status follows (`AgentRun::begin_turn_elsewhere`, `settle_ended_turn`); the transcript is tailed every 2 s at most while an agent works (15 s idle). `TIDEWATCH_DEV_STATUS_LOG=1` prints each append and any live/store tone disagreement over 2 s, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_DROP_HOOKS=<percent>` drops that share of hooks. Numbers: a hidden run with `TIDEWATCH_DEV_DROP_HOOKS=100` and a stand-in `claude` ended its turn 7 ms, 8 ms, 224 ms, 404 ms, 405 ms and 1.3 s after the transcript's end line (7 ms and 8 ms when the agent printed after it and poked the tail; the old path had only the 8 s quiet decay); tests 1969 -> 1984 in core, 2969 -> 3009 in the app. Not yet: git facts (the watcher runs for the active tab only), `Permission*` facts (no transcript holds a prompt), the screens still read the old path (slice 3).

- **A UI alignment pass: edges, gaps, sizes and copy only (branch `fix/ui-alignment-pass`).** The owner: "I want you do do a full UI/UX pass and make sure we have everything properly aligned." No control was added, removed or restyled. Dialog hints (close, leftovers, open repo, take-over, services, trust, fix) now sit on the `footer_row` baseline, hint left and buttons right; a hint the buttons leave no 16 px gap for falls back to the old line under the row, and the close dialog is 580 px so its common two-choice case fits. The service Logs view starts at `layout::EDGE` (header and output were at 10 px). The Agents and profiles page uses the shared header and the same centered 820 px column as the other pages (its title was at 290 px against 426 px). Quiet and finished job lines keep the cards' 16 px inner edges, and a name with no room left is dropped instead of drawn over its value. Meeseeks stage labels sit left-aligned under their own bars. Status bar Cancel is `Size::Tag`. Welcome lost "or a workflow" and its second Cmd+O line (the status bar has it); "N leftovers" and "N tabs" follow the count (`review::plural`); the Menu-bar row lost a stray comma. The repo chip is as wide as the widest repo name of the window, capped at 130 px, the same for every repo. Checked and left alone: the agent tab's left edges (the Ready dot, composer border and status bar dot all start at 24 px; the audit read their halos and glow), the Overview rail inset (egui reserves the bar room only while the body scrolls, now pinned by a test) and the services `...` menu (already anchored right under its button; it is taller than the room left in its own card). Numbers: 2957 tests on the base, 2971 after (0 failed), each fix's test shown failing without it. Open: the Now line of the Progress panel stays flush with the panel edge like the header and stage bars (only the job lines moved).

- **A service you add waits for Start.** Saving the Add service dialog now keeps the new service stopped until Start is pressed, and its Start with the repo switch starts off. An agent's `add_service` call still starts the service it asks for, and services saved before keep their switch. Numbers: 4943 tests passed, 0 failed.

- **Meeseeks: the hand-off follows the pull request the task names.** A Meeseeks summoned to work on an existing PR (a link, `PR 12` or `#12` in its task) now passes its hand-off once that PR is merged, or open with the Meeseeks' commits on its head, instead of demanding a new draft PR from its own branch; the form row and stage bar say `Update PR #N`, and its house style tells it to push to that PR's branch. Numbers: core lib 1674 -> 1683, app 2957 -> 2961, 13 new integration tests over a fake gh.

- **Phase 1 slice 1: the panel's facts as a numbered log, in core.** Every fact the right panel shows can now be written as a numbered event to a tab's `panel-log.jsonl` and folded back into the same view after a restart (`panel_event`, `panel_state`, `panel_log`, `panel_store`, `panel_limits`, `panel_rules` in core; the app's report rules moved there). Nothing is wired into the app yet. Numbers: tidewatch-core lib 1674 -> 1743 tests plus 15 replay tests; a fault harness over 200 seeds (30% of hooks dropped, all hooks dropped, listener gaps, a restart mid-turn) lands on the full script's tone every time. Open: slice 2 feeds the log from the transcript, the process and the terminal.

- **Phase 2: stop the wrong orders.** Each repo gets a workflow profile (rebase or merge, fixups or new commits, draft or ready PRs), detected from the owner's and the repo's rule files and confirmed once on a card under the tab strip or in Settings, General. Every PR message Tidewatch sends (failed checks, review, conflicts, push, open the PR, after a merge, Meeseeks fix notes) now names only the method the profile chose, and the House style page warns about lines that name a tool Tidewatch removed. Numbers: core 1674 -> 1708 tests, app 2957 -> 2980. Open: the launch text still names methods (phase 3).

- **Phase 0 of the voice review: nothing gets stuck (PR #269, branch `agent/zesty-dolphin`).** The owner signed off four rules on the board: every background call has a time limit, no key lock without a visible dialog, every pending state shows itself, expires and can be cancelled, and a finished turn beats an older report. Built by five Sonnet workers and merged here: the close dialog shows while its check runs, with Cancel; the post dialog closes while it posts; a tab change waiting for a turn shows in the status bar with Cancel and expires after an hour; every git and gh call goes through `limited.rs` (60 s, 180 s for worktrees, the process group killed); a hooked CLI's spinner no longer counts as life, a finished turn reads idle over an older working report, a timed out `ask_user` closes its question; the restore saver waits at most 60 s and only for restored tabs; a service restart waits for the old exit (10 s); a repo filter with no agents falls back to All; the window has a 600x480 floor and the terminal a 260 px floor, repo chips cap at three rows; a call past 10 s shows `<label> is taking long · 14 s` with Cancel. A review held for a busy agent goes back to a draft after an hour, with a toast; a restarted Claude tab no hook has spoken for reads Done once its input box is free (any CLI after 10 quiet minutes) instead of Quiet forever. Numbers: 4777 tests on the base, 4872 after (0 failed), each fix's test shown failing without it. Open: screenshots of the new lines, a real hung git, Kelp's own git calls (no limit yet).

## 2026-10-29

- **The Needs you question moves above the reply box (branch `feat/needs-you-reply-box`).** The owner approved the board ("Yes designs look legit"). The card with the agent's ready answers used to sit in the right Progress panel; it now sits in the agent tab's left column, directly above the reply box (the Reader's and the first-message composer), with the same content: amber `NEEDS YOU`, the why, the question at 16 px, numbered ghost chips (recommended one teal, dashed Something else). Digits 1 to 9 pick while the reply box is empty and has the keyboard (or nothing else does); the digit never types into the box. Several questions show one at a time, in order, with `1 of N`; an answer moves the card to the next one even while the agent works, and the last answer clears it. The panel lost the card, the Last turn Answer button and `progress_view::Action::Answer` and `Pick`; the step that waits on you stays as a line with its amber reason. New: `ask_model.rs`, `ask_view.rs`, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PICK`. Proof: headless tests (card above the box, chip click, `1` key, keys ignored with text in the box or a dialog open, no overlap from 260 to 640 px) and screenshots at 1300x800 and a 30 percent left column, before and after.

## 2026-10-28

- **Answers are no longer hidden under the `N updates` chip (branch `fix/reader-answer-flow`).** The owner: "Sometimes answers to my questions are not displayed and are hidden under an 'x updates' chip which is very misleading." Root cause: a turn's answer was only its last text, and agents write the answer, call `update_progress` (the hook line and the house style both push them there), then write a short Done block, so the Done block showed and the answer folded. Now a call to one of Tidewatch's own report tools (`update_progress`, `get_progress`, `report_job`, `set_avatar`, `report_finding`, `reply_to_note`) does not split the answer: the texts after the last real work call show in order as one bubble; only text before a Bash, Edit or Read stays a folded note. The `UserPromptSubmit` line now asks for the report before the final reply, and the house style and progress prompt say to write the reply once, after the last tool call. Table tests in `reader_turns` and `reader_rows`, text tests for the lines.
- **Tidewatch's reminders are one quiet line each (same branch).** The owner: "Tidewatch can be very noisy" (five dashed cards in a row with the same report reminder). Reminders that change nothing about the work (report check, avatar, done check, comment guard, resume, stuck job, unless the owner's own click sent them) draw as one 13 px muted row: the mark, `Tidewatch reminded the agent to update its report`, a count pill when several sit back to back, a chevron and the time; a click opens the list of what was sent. Auto's fixes, a Meeseeks check, a review and the owner's own clicks keep their cards. Fewer reminders too: the `user answered` line goes once per saved report and only on the owner's own message, never on a background task's notice (the hook prompt starts `<task-notification>`; the Reader's rows already file that as command output, not the owner's message). Proof: before and after captures of an agent tab with a stand-in `claude` in a throwaway home.
- **The Reader follows the newest copy of a Claude session (branch `fix/reader-newest-transcript`).** The owner: the Reader showed the latest message as Queued forever and none of the replies after it, while the raw terminal showed them. Root cause: the live session wrote `projects/<its folder>/<id>.jsonl` while an older copy of the same id sat in the tab's own project folder (Change this tab and hand copies make these), and the Reader tailed the older one. `Locator` now takes the copy with the newest modification time among the working directory's folder and every other folder under `projects` (a tie keeps the working directory's), and the feed looks every 5 s (one folder listing and a stat each, on its own thread) for a newer copy: `Tail::follow` moves onto it only when it continues the bytes already read (first 64 bytes and the last 256 before the offset match), so no row is drawn twice; a copy that does not continue them is ignored. Restore's transcript check and level lookup use the same pick. Left alone: Codex, Gemini, Copilot (their files are found by id or start time, no copies), `elsewhere_scan` and `retarget_repo` (they key on a live process's own working directory, whose folder is the live one) and `retarget_copy::find_session` (copies from the folder the caller names). The Reader's locator still reads `~/.claude`, not `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. Numbers: transcript_locate 15 tests (4 new), Tail follow 2 new, feed 2 new including the queued message clearing once the newer file holds it.
- **GitHub tells Tidewatch what happens, polling stays as the fallback (branch `feat/github-events`).** The owner: "Is there no way for tidewatch or the agent to react to webhook events in GH for prs issues, whatever instead of running monitoring tasks?" and picked `gh webhook forward` per repo with polling as the fallback. One forwarder per GitHub repo the window holds (never per tab; a pure state machine in `github_forwarder.rs`, the host in `github_events.rs`): a signed `ping` makes it live, SIGTERM to its process group stops it on repo close, quit and restart, a missing extension is installed once on a thread, our own recorded stale hook is deleted on a 422 and a hook we never recorded is left alone. The listener takes `POST /github/<repo token>` and checks `X-Hub-Signature-256` (HMAC-SHA256 through the `hmac` crate and its constant-time `verify_slice`). A delivery for pull_request, review, review comment, issue_comment, check_suite, check_run, workflow_run or push makes the tabs whose PR number or branch it names run the existing `pr_gh::poll` 2 s later; the payload is never read as PR state. While live the routine looks drop to every 10 minutes (every 2 minutes while checks run, was 20 s); in fallback polling is exactly as before. The only visible change is the tooltip on the PR number: `Live from GitHub` or `Checking every 2 min` with the reason. Checked: 4752 tests, 0 failed (state machine, HMAC RFC 4231 vectors, event table, listener over HTTP, host with a fake gh including SIGTERM reaching the forwarder), fmt, clippy on stable and 1.99.0; live on Hobo-Ware/tidewatch (gh 2.98.0, gh-webhook 0.2.0): signed ping accepted and recorded, SIGTERM to the group removed the hook (count 1 then 0), no process left; a SIGKILL of the forwarder left no hook after 2 s, so the live run never reached the stale-hook path (the fake gh covers it). Not verified: a repo without admin (the 403 text is a guess matched broadly), a live event beyond the ping.

- **The tests gate reads the tab's own worktree (branch `fix/test-command-worktree`).** The owner: "Why is the meeseeks trying to run deno, we still have hardcoded shit in the codebase?" A Meeseeks of a repo that moved from Deno to Bun ran `deno task test` and blocked: detection looked at the repo's main checkout, which sat on a branch 1027 commits behind with a `deno.json`, while the agent's worktree was on current main with `bun.lock`. `meeseeks_check::tests` now calls `test_command::resolve(worktree, repo)`: `.tidewatch.toml` from the worktree (the main checkout's only when the worktree has none), markers from the worktree root, and `timeout_in` from the same file. A lockfile now picks the JavaScript runtime before `deno.json`. A detected command whose program is not on PATH is passed over for the next marker; when none can run the check passes with "skipped: no runnable test command (deno not installed)", so the Meeseeks is never Blocked for a missing tool (a configured `test = "..."` is never passed over). The done check card's "Tests running" text comes from the same worktree resolution (`Host::test_command`), not the main checkout's facts. Tests: table tests for worktree against main checkout, lockfile before deno, fall-through, skip, config fallback, timeout, and a real throwaway repo (main checkout on a branch with `deno.json`, worktree on `bun.lock`, a fake `bun` and no `deno` on PATH: the gate runs bun in the worktree, then skips when bun is removed). Not changed: the new-tab form's "Tests pass" tick and tooltip still detect from the main checkout, since no worktree exists yet. The TestFails, TestPasses and SameBehavior gates the old docs name left with the workflows; the Meeseeks Tests step is the only caller.
- **Change this tab always lands an agent in its own worktree (branch `fix/change-tab-agent-branch`).** The owner's 0.1.41 report: a shell whose Claude session ran in a plain workspace folder (holding many repos) became an agent tab sitting in that folder: "you should've freakin moved it to an agent branch or something, IDK why I need to think about this". Converting into an agent now ends in an agent worktree on a fresh `agent/<name>` branch, made like a new agent tab (the `[new_tab]` start branch, fetched first, a free random name, the repo's setup recipe), unless the session already runs in a Tidewatch agent worktree (kept as before) or in no repo at all (the folder tab, as before). The repo is the one the session folder is in (main checkout or a worktree that is not Tidewatch's); for a plain folder it is the repo the transcript's tool calls touched most (Edit, Write, Read file paths, Bash `cd` and `git -C`, newest weighted first), else the repo with the newest commit one level down; it is added to the window when not open. Claude resumes by id only in its own project folder, so the `.jsonl` and its sidecar folder are copied (never moved, no overwrite, each file compared with its source) to the worktree's project folder, then `claude --resume <id>` runs there; Codex and Copilot resume by id anywhere, Gemini's `--resume latest` is per folder so it starts a new session with a note. The pinned main tab cannot own a worktree, so it keeps its shell and the agent opens in a new tab (id reserved before the job). The popover says it in one sentence with the branch name it will use: `Claude Code stops, then the same conversation resumes as Builder · YOLO on a new branch agent/<name> in <repo>.` plus `3 uncommitted files stay in ~/path.` only when there are some. A worktree that cannot be made leaves the tab in its folder and a conversation that cannot be copied starts a new session, each with a note. Tests: table tests for the repo pick (inside a repo, plain folder with transcript evidence, plain folder without, no repo, linked worktree), the copy (identical bytes, sidecar, no overwrite, source untouched), the sentence, `Home`; a real round trip with a throwaway workspace of two repos, a stand-in `claude` and a throwaway home (a shell tab in the workspace whose session edited repo B becomes an agent tab on a new `agent/*` worktree of B, the copy is byte-identical, the original is still there, the launch is `--resume <id>` in the worktree, repo A untouched, B's uncommitted file untouched). Checked on screen: `TIDEWATCH_DEV_CHANGE_TAB=home` and `home:change` with `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_SHELL_AGENT_CWD` (popover sentence, converted tab with the worktree's History in the right panel). Not verified: a real Claude session resumed from the copy, a folder name over Claude Code's 200 character project name (the copy is refused and a new session starts), Codex and Copilot resuming a session started in another folder.
- **A folder tab's right panel is never empty (branch `fix/folder-tab-panel`).** The owner changed a shell whose Claude session ran in a plain folder of many repos: the tab became a Builder tab in that folder and the right panel was empty. An agent tab with no worktree of its own now draws the normal panel. The folder is looked at on a job thread (every 3 s while the tab shows): inside a git repo it shows that repo; with repos one level down (linked worktrees included, at most 50) the card header gets a repo picker chip (the look of the History scope switch, each repo with its branch) and Changes and History read the picked repo as a repo tab's do; with none it shows the folder's `~` path, `No git repo in this folder` and the files changed in the last hour (at most 200, hidden, `node_modules` and `target` skipped). The default pick is the repo holding the agent's newest Edit or Write call (read from the transcript entries the Reader already holds), else the repo whose index, `HEAD` or top-level files changed last; it follows the agent into another repo until the owner picks one, and that pick is saved in the tab's session. A tab in the Other group gets the panel too. Gates green: 2832 app tests and 1600 core tests (about 40 new). Check: a throwaway folder with three repos and one with none, captured at 1300x800 (picker closed, picker open, no-repo view). Not checked: a live agent editing in two repos in turn (the stand-in agent writes no transcript).
- **Unrecorded leftovers can be discarded (branch `fix/discard-unrecorded`).** The owner: "IDC these were not made by tidewatch I want the ability to remove them". The dialog said "not made by this Tidewatch, remove it with git" and offered nothing. Now `leftovers` reads the repo and branch of any worktree from git (`recorded: false` when the registry and the `agent/<name>` rule do not claim it), the row is inspected like any other and lists what it would lose (or `nothing to lose`), an orange `unrecorded` tag stays as information, a proven-safe one joins Clean up, and any other has one red Discard. Discard goes through `remove_with`, so the removals log gets its line first; with `Terms::spare_unique` the branch is deleted only when no commit on it exists elsewhere, otherwise it stays and the toast says `branch kept: N commits only here`. A folder that is a repository's own checkout (a submodule, `--separate-git-dir`) is never listed; a bare repository's worktree works; a detached HEAD has no branch to delete. A folder git cannot name a repository for is listed as `unreadable`; its Discard logs the removal first, then moves the folder to the Trash (`NSFileManager trashItemAtURL` on macOS, `gio trash` elsewhere), never a delete, and is refused if git can read the folder by then. A recorded row whose gitdir points nowhere ("could not check it") becomes `unreadable` the same way, and its Discard also unlocks and prunes the registry's repo when that still exists, so git forgets the folder; its branch stays. Checked: table tests for the row text, real throwaway repos (2 unpushed commits: folder gone, branch at the same commit, log entry; clean: folder and branch gone), and a `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` of the dialog, and one real trash call on macOS (an ignored test). **Not verified:** a click on the Discard button on a real screen, the owner's two real worktrees, `gio trash` on Linux.
- **Services list: the `...` menu keeps the list open, no port field, one button per action (branch `fix/services-popover`).** The owner on 0.1.41: "Clicking on the ... closes the panel" and "Why does it ask for a port? It's just a random shell command right?" Root cause of the first: egui keeps one memory popup per viewport, so `Popup::menu` on the `...` replaced the list popup and closed it. The menu is now its own `Popup::from_response` with the open card in `Memory.menu`; a click outside both closes both, a click in the list off the menu closes the menu, Escape closes the menu first, Edit and Remove act with the list left open (Remove still confirms). The Add and Edit dialogs lost the Port field: a service is a command, the port is read from its output (`service_port.rs`: the newest line that says where it listens, else the newest local address; read every 0.3 s until found, then every 2 s), `services.toml` still reads an old `port` and never writes one, and `add_service` accepts and ignores `port`. A stopped or gave-up card shows Logs and Start, a running one Logs, Restart and Stop (the Logs header too). Why both cards said "stopped, exit 1": the supervisor state was Stopped, which only a Stop (button or agent tool) produces; a service that gave up is Crashed and reads orange. The "exit 1" is portable-pty reporting any signal death as 1 for the shell the Stop killed, so a hand stop now clears the exit and reads `stopped`, and a gave-up service reads `gave up after 5 crashes, Start to try again`. Restart policy untouched. Tests: headless frames for the `...` click, Edit, Remove, outside click, inner click, toggle, Escape; table tests for the port reader; a real pty shows the kill reports exit 1 and the port is read from a stand-in's output. Not verified: the owner's real services, a real `pscale` proxy.

- **Change this tab follows the session and never refuses (branch `fix/change-tab-follows-session`).** The owner's `calm-walrus` shell tab refused because its Claude session ran in `upbeat-lantern`, another worktree of the same repo: "I don't want to think about why it can't, you need to think about how it can be", then "I want everything to be possible, reasons for not doing shit is bullshit and lazy." The converted tab now lands where the session runs: a worktree Tidewatch made becomes the tab's own (its branch and name; the tab moves to that repo's group, adding the repo to the window when it is not open), any other git folder or plain folder runs as a folder tab, a folder another tab shows is followed without owning it, and the resume always starts in the session's folder. The old folder stays on disk untouched. Every old refusal now works: the pinned main tab changes in place and stays pinned, a Meeseeks becomes a normal agent tab, a busy shell is ended with its command (the sentence says so), another CLI starts a new conversation in the same folder, a missing or unreadable session id starts a new one with a toast, a missing profile falls back to the CLI's defaults, YOLO runs as picked in any folder, and only a level the CLI has no flags for falls back to the nearest lower one, a process that will not exit goes with its terminal, an agent that stopped meanwhile is looked for again, a tab still being set up or removed waits, a form, failure or summary tab is replaced. A missing CLI is a Set up button in the popover. Tests: table tests for `landing` (same folder, subfolder, another worktree, worktree of another open repo, of a repo not open, unknown git repo, not Tidewatch's, plain folder, folder another tab shows, pinned), `fit_level`, the sentence; a real round trip with a throwaway repo, two worktrees, a stand-in `claude` and a fake process table (shell tab in A with a session in B becomes an agent tab in B running `--resume <id>` in B, A still listed by git). Checked: `TIDEWATCH_DEV_CHANGE_TAB=elsewhere:change` captures in a throwaway home with real detection of a stand-in session. Not verified: a real Claude session, a pinned main tab hosting an agent across a restart (the main tab is saved as main, so its agent is not restored). Found, not fixed (stdusk): `PtyTerm::foreground_pgid` panics (`rustix` `raw > 0`) on a pane whose shell has exited.

## 2026-10-27

- **Reopen a tab you closed (branch `feat/reopen-closed`).** The owner: "If I closed a builder agent how do I resume it?" Closing an agent tab now notes it in `.state/closed-tabs.jsonl` (repo, worktree path, branch, commit, HEAD, profile, level, CLI, session id, title, whether the branch was kept; newest 100). The Overview's rail shows a Recently closed section under Running elsewhere (last 14 days, a session id, hidden while that session is open in a tab); Reopen recreates the branch at the logged commit when it is gone, adds the worktree at the old path, runs the repo's setup recipe copies and links, and opens the tab through the restore path with `--resume <id>`. A missing commit or a taken path is refused with a toast; nothing is overwritten. The recipe's generator is not re-run. Numbers: 8 core unit and 8 round-trip tests, 6 state and 5 job tests in the app.

- **Workflows are retired (branch `chore/retire-workflows`).** The owner: "I've decided to retire the workflow feature, remove everything related to it (for now)." The runner, built-in workflows, editor, new-tab Workflow kind, stepper, Build workflow command and the `stage_done` and `propose_workflow` tools are deleted; see the Deviations log in PLAN.md for what still loads (old sessions, handovers, `workflows.toml` left on disk). Tests went from 4604 to 4343, the difference being the removed feature's tests.

- **Change what a tab runs: shell to agent, agent to another profile or level, agent back to shell (branch `feat/shell-to-builder`).** The owner: "I need a mechanism to convert a shell session into a builder session", then asked for one flow for every change of a tab and picked option B of the board. A hand-typed `claude` in a shell tab is found (`scan_in_shells`, a look at most every 4 s on a job, only shell tabs, only while the window shows), and the tab can change in place through the take-over's own `end_session` (SIGTERM to that pid only, never the shell) and the restore path's launch wiring: same tab id, place, worktree and folder, same session id. The "Change this tab" popover opens from the profile chip, the tab menu and the palette. Mid-turn waits for the turn unless "Stop turn and switch now"; Agent to Shell remembers the session id; a plain shell starts a new session in place. Not verified: a real Claude session was not converted.

- **Any file attaches as a chip, not only pictures (branch `feat/file-attachments`).** The owner: "I want attachments for all file types not just images please". A file dragged from Finder onto the Reader or the first-message box, or copied in Finder and pasted with Cmd+V, becomes a chip in the picture chip row: same 48 px height, outline remove button, a page glyph with the extension (`CSV`, `PDF`, `ZIP`) or a folder glyph, the name cut in the middle, the size or `Folder`. A picture and a folder keep their own path. Any other file is copied on a job thread into the tab's `pastes` folder (up to 50 MB; a bigger or uncopyable file keeps its own path and a toast says so), so the agent always gets a path it can read. On send the message is the text, then one line per chip: a picture's bare path as before, anything else as `Attached: <path>`; the sent bubble draws the same chips, and a path merely typed or pasted as text stays text. A click on a file chip reveals it in Finder (never opens it, so a script cannot run); a folder opens. Typing in a terminal is unchanged (paths typed). Tests: table tests for kind, copy-or-reference, size and extension labels, middle cut, copy names; headless frames for a dropped CSV (a chip as tall as a picture chip, nothing in the text, the sent message, the x). Checked: background capture in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_DROP`. Not verified: a real Finder drop or Cmd+V of a file, a real agent reading the path, Linux.

- **Animated emoji, bundled (branch `feat/animated-emoji`, stacked on `feat/color-emoji`).** The owner approved the board "Animated emoji: when they play", then chose bundled copies over fetching ("Bundle shrunk copies instead"). `scripts/make-emoji.py` downloads the 881 Noto animated WebP files (512 px, 305 MB) once, shrinks each to 40 px, keeps every second frame (60 ms steps), re-encodes as lossy animated WebP at quality 40 and writes `moving.pack`: **14.7 MB** (budget 15 MB; a test holds it; sample estimates before the full run were 14.1 to 14.6 MB; quality 70 with every frame would have been 44 MB, quality 70 with every second frame 22.5 MB, lossless 32 to 47 MB). The `image` crate gets its `webp` feature, `image-webp` was already in the lock file, and there is no run-time fetch and no cache folder. A new message plays its emoji once and they rest on the still; pointing at one plays it again; history stays still; a message is new when its transcript stamp is at or after the moment its tab started. Settings, General, Chat turns animation off (default on, `[chat] animate_emoji`, a file without it loads as on) and macOS Reduce motion does the same (`NSWorkspace`, read every 2 s; Linux has no check yet). A playing emoji asks for its next frame (`request_repaint_after`), a resting one asks for nothing, and a clip is decoded only when something is about to play. About now also credits the animated emoji as CC BY 4.0. Tests: 2,711 app tests pass (25 new): the rule table (arrival, history, hover edge, restart on hover, off, no replay on re-enable), clip frames and uv cells, headless frames over a real galley (plays, rests on the still, hover replays, setting off, repaint delay, a flag never moves), a bubble test for fresh and history, the Chat card switch, the settings round trip. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SHOT` runs in a throwaway home: a shot 1 s in shows the face, rocket and party popper mid-animation, the same shot at 12 s shows the stills. **Not verified:** a real agent session, Reduce motion on a real Mac, Linux, the Chat card by eye (the capture scrolled past it), release binary size.

- **Emoji draw in colour in the Reader (branch `feat/color-emoji`).** The owner approved the board "Color emoji in chat": egui fills glyph outlines in one colour, so chat emoji were outlines in the text colour. The real character now stays in the text (copy, select and send give the emoji), is laid out at 1.25 times the text size and drawn transparent, and after the layout the Noto colour picture is painted over its slot: 20 pt square, centred 6 pt above the baseline at 16 pt text, every row keeps its height (all sections get one explicit line height). It covers the bubbles of both speakers (`link_text::Text::show`), the reply box (`composer`, through the one `slot_job` call in `bullets::layout`) and the `:smi` list; a ZWJ sequence, skin tone, flag, keycap or tag sequence is one picture, and an emoji with no picture keeps today's glyph. A bare `\u{2764}` with no variation selector stays text, like a browser. The pictures are Noto Emoji `v2.051` at 72 px, 3,731 files plus 281 flags and newer emoji from Google's CDN, quantised to 96 colours (64 banded: PSNR 38.4 dB on the rocket and party popper, 96 gives 41 dB and above, worst of a 60-file sample 40.6 dB) and `oxipng`ed: 13.1 MB of PNGs became one 5.92 MB `still.pack` in the binary (`include_bytes!`, an index plus the pictures; `scripts/make-emoji.py` rebuilds it, nothing is fetched at run time). Textures upload only when an emoji is drawn, at most 512 are kept, nothing repaints for them. Settings, General, About credits Google Noto Emoji (Apache 2.0). Tests: 2,686 app tests pass (23 new: key table, finder tables, slot job, geometry at 20 pt and 6 pt above the baseline, wrapped rows, equal row heights, one slot per sequence, copy text, texture reuse, the About credit). Checked with a `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND` screenshot in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude`: the face sits 5.5 pt above the baseline at full size (centre of the art, 6 pt for the slot), the reply box and the `:smi` list show colour too. **Not verified:** a real agent session, Linux, the tab strip's title (still a font glyph, out of scope).
- **The Parked pill moves to the left end of the tabs (branch `feat/parked-pill-left`).** The owner: "I think I would prefer the parked tab on the left, it's very easy to \"lose\" and forget it's there..." then "So parked tabs are before all other tabs basically". The pill now sits right after the `/` and before the main entry, one strip gap each side, and takes no room when nothing is parked (a tab dragged with nothing parked still shows the `Drop to park` place, now floating over the strip where the pill will sit, so no tab shifts mid-drag). The tab width and the parking fits are measured after it, so a narrow strip parks tabs and never covers the pill; its list opens below it, left edges aligned. A pill that is gone forgets its rect, so a stale one cannot catch a drop over the tabs.
- **Agents write and open their own pull requests (branch `feat/agent-writes-pr`).** The owner: "The PRs that tidewatch opens are shit the titles are bad and the descriptions are also bad this should be delegated to the agent..." and "Tidewatch should never perform actions it should be a monitor ... decisions should be agent driven". Tidewatch no longer composes, pushes or opens anything. Auto and the card's `Ask <Profile> to open a PR` button send the agent one message (`pr_ask.rs`) through the one send gate: push the branch and run `gh pr create --draft --base <base>`, write a Conventional Commits title and a short body, follow the house rules. The card says `Asked <Profile> to open the pull request`, Tidewatch looks for the PR once when the agent goes idle and once more two minutes later, then shows `No pull request yet` with `Ask again`; Auto never asks twice. A Meeseeks opens its own draft before `meeseeks_done`, and its hand-off check fails with a fix note when no PR is at its head. Push fixes are asks too. Removed: `deliver`, the edit dialog, `auto_deliver`, the proposed title and body. Checked: table tests for the card states and timing, the message, the hand-off failure with the fake gh, a push of `<branch>:<branch>-2` through the guard; captures of the three cards in a throwaway repo with a fake gh.
- **A worktree Tidewatch made is never left out of the registry, and one that was is still cleaned up (branch `fix/unrecorded-leftover`).** The owner's Leftover worktrees dialog listed a worktree under Tidewatch's own folder, on its `agent/<name>` branch, as `unrecorded`, with no Discard and a Clean up that did nothing. Root cause: `registry.rs` read the whole file, changed it and wrote it back with no lock and one shared temp file, and `worktree::add_named` only printed the error when that failed, so two tabs opening at once lost an entry and the worktree existed with no record. Now every registry change runs under a file lock with its own temp file, and a worktree whose entry cannot be written is rolled back and refused. A worktree directly in the repo's folder under the Tidewatch root on `agent/<folder name>` counts as ours even without an entry (`is_made_by_tidewatch`) and gets the same safety check, Clean up when proven safe and Discard otherwise; anything else stays `unrecorded`, and Clean up is then disabled with a tooltip saying why. Checked: a six-thread burst of `add_free` lost an entry before the fix and records all six after; unwritable registry leaves nothing behind; table tests for the ownership rule; real-repo and dialog tests for an unrecorded worktree. Not verified: the owner's real worktree (not touched), a live run of the app.

- **Links the agent shares stay in the Progress panel (branch `feat/links-ledger`).** The owner: "can we have like a ledger of all reported links sticky in the progress panel ... so that we can easily lookup resources later (for example after we've made a decision)", approved as drawn (option A), and "yes catch chat links". `update_progress` takes `links` (`url` http or https, one-line `title` of at most 120 characters, optional `for`, at most 20 per call) and `needs_you.links` (stored with `for` set to the question); a broken rule refuses the report with the usual list of fixes. Links are kept in the tab's `links.json` for the life of the tab, merged by URL (first-seen time kept, a later non-empty title or `for` wins, nothing dropped). Tidewatch adds what it already reads: claude.ai artifact links and GitHub pull and issue links in the agent's own chat messages (markdown link text as the title), and the tab's own pull request. A Links card sits under the title in Progress and above Needs you: the newest 3 rows (kind tag, title that opens the link, `host · for: note · 12m ago`, a copy button), `Show all N` in place, and `Read first:` in the Needs you card for links sent with that question. `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_LINKS=1` gives agent tabs the mockup's five links and writes nothing.

- **A resumed Claude agent comes back at the mode it was last in.** The restore plan reads the last `permissionMode` from the tab's transcript (`permission_mode.rs`) and resumes at that level instead of the launch level, so a Shift+Tab inside Claude survives a restart or a crash; YOLO is only taken for a worktree tab or one saved at YOLO. Running and ended sessions in Running elsewhere read their level the same way, so an ended YOLO session moved here no longer lands at Ask. Other CLIs keep their saved level.
