Tabs already resumed at the wrong level keep it: their transcript now says so.

## 2026-10-26

- **Agents report through a typed contract and the screens show one state (branches `feat/progress-contract` and `feat/progress-panel`).** The owner: "for us to have an aligned overview our MCP should be the contract and we should request that they report certain states etc that we can always map", approved as drawn ("yes build it as drawn looks legit"). `update_progress` now takes `state` (working, needs_you, waiting, blocked, done), `now`, 2 to 5 stages the agent names (each a kind: understand, build, ship, verify, research), steps, `needs_you`, `waiting_on`, typed `scheduled`, verified, not verified and decisions; a report that breaks a rule is refused with each fix, `done` while a step is open too. The report is stored as `progress.json` beside `progress.md`; the old markdown ledger is still taken and mapped onto it, and old `progress.md` files still load. The prompt, the ledger guard and the prompt hook ask for the report, the same for every CLI. Tidewatch nudges an idle agent whose report said working for an hour, and one whose last message asks something the report lacks. The panel, the status line, the tab dot, the Overview and the status bar show the same word and colour (needs you amber, waiting blue, blocked red, done green), the Overview has a Waiting section, and after a merge the bottom bar reads `N commits after the merge` with Push to a new PR. Checked: table tests for every rule, the markdown mapping, the nudges, the panel, the Overview and the bar; each of five rules fails when removed; captures of the season-recap-like tab, a working tab, a done tab and the Overview with a stand-in agent that reports through the real MCP tool. Not checked: a real Claude Code, Codex, Gemini or Copilot run.

- **Auto replaces Auto-fix, and pushing the branch is one of its jobs (branch `feat/auto-mode`).** The owner, after a YOLO tab silently pushed and opened a ready PR with an empty body that its agent never heard of: "It should be an option under the autofix which we can rename to AUTO and have users configure whatever they want auto to be there live anyway. Defaults can be configured in settings." Built as drawn on the approved board. The mode reads Auto everywhere (chip, popover, Settings, cards, the `auto` tag; a saved `auto-fix` still loads). The popover has an AUTO MAY label over four ticks and Settings a fourth switch, Push and open the pull request, off by default (`push` in `[github]`). The silent YOLO delivery is gone: a tab pushes only when its mode is Auto and the push tick is on (any tab kind, the tab's choice beats the Settings default; a Meeseeks keeps its own hand-off). Then the agent is told first through the one send gate (`auto` tag, `Sent because Auto may push and open the pull request on this tab`), and only once that went Tidewatch pushes and opens a DRAFT with no dialog, body from the agent's summary, else the ledger goal, verified items and commits, never empty. A chat card reads `Draft opened`, the branch line and `Mark it ready on GitHub when you're happy with it.` (the watcher reads `isDraft`). One go per stretch of work, a failure waits in the dialog for the owner. After a merge, Auto and the Push and open PR button send only the commits after the merged head to the next free `<branch>-N` and open a new PR; the branch's upstream remembers the name. YOLO and Meeseeks tabs with no choice of their own stay Auto for the fix jobs whatever the Settings mode says ("builder should always auto fix"), with the Settings ticks as before; their push tick is never forced and follows the tab's own choice, else the Settings default (off). Numbers: new tests for the rename and old-value load, push tick off means nothing pushed on a YOLO tab, the message before the push, the draft flag and a non-empty body through a fake `gh`, the new branch name after a merge, the Settings default off; fmt, clippy and `cargo test --workspace` clean. Checked: captures in a throwaway home and repo with a bare remote, a stand-in `claude` that posts hooks and a fake `gh` (popover, Settings card, the message and the Draft opened card, the unchanged bar with push off and nothing pushed). **Not verified:** a real agent run, the real `gh` and a real GitHub draft, a live post-merge push (tests with a bare remote only), a squash-merged branch's new PR may list the already merged commits, later commits on a tab that moved to a `-N` branch push to that name through its upstream, Linux.

- **Two send-path gaps closed (branch `fix/send-path-checks`).** From the send audit (finding 12). Answers and skips typed as a message after a question (a held `ask_user` call that ended, a skipped or key-less Claude menu) pasted and pressed Enter a fixed 0.3 s later, never waiting for the terminal to go quiet; they now queue their Enter through the same Enter queue as every other send. Answers typed into Claude's question menu now look at the terminal first: only when the screen is readable and shows no numbered first option for that question (wraps, a cursor glyph, a cut label all still count as open) is nothing typed and a toast says so, and a tab that runs on its own does not count the pick; an empty or unreadable screen sends as before. The menu screen shape follows PLAN.md's description of real runs; no raw capture of the menu exists in the repo yet.
- **A merged tab's worktree is never offered for removal while its agent still needs it (branch `fix/merged-cleanup-guard`).** The owner: "if I say delete worktree I basically orphan the running agent. so we need to address this to NEVER get into this situation again." The merged card kept offering Remove from a look taken before the agent made more commits, while the agent still worked with an open ledger. Now `Busy` (agent working or asking, an open ledger, a running job) replaces the buttons with one line (`PR merged. Cleanup waits: ...`, with the commits not in the PR and uncommitted files from the check), the Remove click and the removal after its recheck both refuse with a toast, and the check is redone when the agent lets go and every 15 s while it works. Commits not in the PR and a dirty tree were already refused by the recheck. A ledger counts as open until its state says done or every step is closed (`Ledger::is_open`); an agent that exited counts for nothing.
  Checked: table tests for each reason in the model, the watch and a real git worktree (the second check), and the offer coming back; with the guard forced off 8 of them fail. Captures in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude`, a fake `gh` and a bare remote: blocked (working, open ledger, 2 commits) and clear. Not verified: a real Claude session, and agent background shells that report no job (they are not seen).
- **Tidewatch is its own speaker in the Reader, and every send goes through one gate (branch `feat/tidewatch-speaker`).** The owner: "I want tidewatch to be a 3rd party in the conversation like I want to see HE sent the messages not appear as me", approved as drawn. Every sender now writes one line to the tab's `tidewatch-sent.jsonl` (reason, because, text, cap) and types through `flow_glue::deliver`, the only paste path. The Reader matches a transcript user turn to a record (exact words, else the start of a long paste or its placeholder inside 10 minutes, nearest first, claim saved) and draws it centred: mark, name, time, an outline reason tag, a dashed card folded to two lines with `Show all`, and `Sent because ... 1 of 3 auto-fixes` under it. Hook-told messages (ledger guard, avatar and ledger context) have no transcript turn and sit among the rows by time. Records survive a restart, so no Tidewatch message returns as a "You" bubble. The gate (`outbox`): one send per tab until the agent leaves Done or 10 s pass, the rest queue (a second Meeseeks note no longer overwrites the first), a half-typed draft or an open dialog over Claude's input box holds automatic sends, a shared cap of 3 automatic done-check and comment-sweep sends then a paused line (the user writing resets it), Enter waits for the terminal to go quiet, and a review marked sent that cannot be typed gives its notes back to the draft. Numbers: new tests for the record round trip, claim matching, the gate, the cap, the queue, the revert and the screen reader; fmt, clippy and `cargo test --workspace` clean. Checked: captures in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` (an auto-fix card and a ledger check card between a You bubble and agent turns, folded and open, then the same Reader from the saved file in a second launch). Not verified: a real agent run, a live send through the new gate with a real pty (covered by pure tests and the record seeded by hand), the keystroke-since-turn-end check of the audit (only the screen is read), question-menu keys still fire without checking the menu is open, Linux.
- **Every CLI reports like Claude (branch `feat/cli-parity`).** The owner: every agent CLI, "not just Claude", should report to Tidewatch reliably. Five findings fixed, one commit each. (1) Copilot got hooks: the tab loads a plugin from its state folder with `--plugin-dir`, PascalCase event names so the payloads come Claude-shaped, nothing written into the repo or `~/.copilot`; a finished turn now reads Done, and its MCP entry sets `timeout`. (2) A Codex, Gemini or Copilot permission holds until its own call ends (Codex `PostToolUse`, Gemini `BeforeTool` and `AfterTool`, Copilot `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PostToolUseFailure`); a shell call is keyed by its command because Codex's prompt carries a description its `PostToolUse` lacks. (3) Gemini reads the generated settings as its system settings file (`GEMINI_CLI_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_PATH`, merged over the repo's own, hooks concatenated), so a repo with a `.gemini/settings.json` no longer loses hooks and MCP and nothing is written into the worktree. (4) The ledger text promises "sent back" only to Claude; every other CLI is told it gets a reminder, and gets one pasted when a turn that used tools ended with no ledger saved (once per turn, idle only, never for the turn that answers it). (5) `tools/list` is filtered by tab role. Checked live against stand-in models on copilot 1.0.103, gemini 0.46.0 and codex 0.162.1: a turn with a permission prompt per CLI, real payloads kept as `tests/fixtures/*_hooks.jsonl`; a 1.5 s Copilot MCP timeout cut a 4 s call. Not checked: a real model run of any CLI, Copilot's `ask_user` held for minutes, a Linux run.
- **Later work no longer hides in Next (branch `feat/schedule-from-next`).** The owner: "I want to make sure the agents always report scheduled tasks and if the pending tasks were meant for today we should see that in the schedule." The ledger prompt now says Next is this turn only, work that waits goes in Scheduled with a `when`, and Next is emptied into Scheduled before the state turns waiting, blocked or done. `update_progress` still saves a stopped ledger that keeps real Next entries with nothing open in Scheduled, but adds a second line telling the agent to move them. Coming up shows those Next entries in the TODAY group with a muted `next step` note, in memory only (never in `schedule.toml`), and the Overview cards' `Next:` line ignores them. Checked: table tests for `Ledger::stranded_next`, the tool reply, the rows, their sort place and the rail note.
- **The ledger is enforced and Coming up forgets dead sessions (same branch).** The owner: "I want that section to work perfectly and the agents should be instructed accordingly to interact with the MCP such that we always have valid data." An agent told the user two dates and wrote a ledger file of its own; its tab had no `progress.md`. A ledger guard now rides on Claude's hooks (`--guard ledger` at UserPromptSubmit notes the turn start, `--guard ledger --stop` at Stop exits 2 once per turn when the turn used a tool and `progress.md` is missing or older than the turn), and the prompt says the Tidewatch ledger is required and dates told in chat belong in Scheduled. Coming up: a closed tab keeps only its dated items, an entry whose worktree folder is gone is dropped on load and when its tab closes. Checked: table tests for the stop decision, the transcript reader, `Index::close` / `prune`, `State::look`, and an end-to-end hook test. Not checked: a real Claude run firing the hook; leftover worktrees removed while the app runs keep their closed entry until the next start.
- **A pending permission holds until its own tool call ends (branch `fix/permission-sticks`).** The owner: a Claude tab at its "Do you want to proceed?" prompt read Working for a long time. Root cause: `from_hook` turned a permission into Working once the terminal printed for 2 s, and Claude keeps drawing (spinner, timers of the parallel tidewatch calls) behind a prompt. Claude's `PermissionRequest` now names the call (tool and input) and the new `PostToolUse` and `PostToolUseFailure` hooks end it, so completions of other parallel calls cannot hide it; the Reader's answer, a turn end and an idle notice also release it. Agents that do not name the call (Gemini, Codex) keep the old decay. Checked: table tests with the exact event order (permission for Bash, two tidewatch calls finishing, output every second), gates green. Not checked: a live Claude run; a command amended with Tab before approving ends on Stop, the Reader answer or the idle notice, not on its own end.
- **One chat composer for the Reader's reply and the first message: a 48 px box, picture chips and an emoji list (branch `feat/chat-composer`, stacked on `feat/prompt-ghost`).** The owner: "it's 2026 can we have a proper chat window? It needs to be supreme, pixel perfect." The reply looked like it had more padding on top than below. Root cause, read from a capture and the laid-out rects: the placeholder was drawn in egui's default hint font (a 15 px row against the typed text's 18 px one), so it sat 1.5 pt higher than typed text and jumped on the first key; the frame's stroke was added after the content was laid out, which put the box 1 px above the room it was given; the padding was a hand-set margin plus a `centred` spacer; and the bundled font's ink sits about 1.7 pt low in its row. `composer.rs` now works the padding out from the row height (`(46 - row) / 2` each side, less a 0.4 pt optical lift), paints the box itself with an inside stroke, and gives the placeholder, the ghost text and typed text one 16 px font and one origin. Both boxes are the same widget (`agent_ready::composer` is a thin caller; its old 164 px card is gone). A pasted screenshot is a chip (52x36 thumbnail decoded once on a thread, `Screenshot N`, `W x H · size`, an outline remove button) 8 px above the text; the text stays clean, Backspace at its start removes the last chip, a click opens the picture, the hover shows a 360 px preview, and on send the paths follow the text on lines of their own. A sent bubble recognises those closing path lines (`pictures::split_trailing`) and draws the same chips. `:` and two letters open an emoji list above the box (`emojis` crate, GitHub's names): prefix matches then contains, the most used first, the typed letters in bold teal, Up and Down, Tab or Enter inserts, Esc closes, `:tada:` and a space converts. Tab takes the ghost suggestion only in an empty box with the list closed.
  Checked: table tests for the matching, the chip text and the path recognition; headless frames measuring 48 px, gaps of 14.59 pt above and 15.41 pt below for one row and for three (the 0.8 pt difference is the optical lift), placeholder rect equal to the typed text's rect (x 32.0, y 658.6), Send centred on one row and level with the last of several, growth to 40% of the column then a visible cursor row, chips 8 px above the text, Backspace, send with paths, Tab and Enter with the list open (Enter sends nothing), Esc, `:tada:` conversion; captures in a throwaway home and repo with a stand-in `claude` (empty box, one line, three lines, two chips, the list open, a sent bubble with chips, the first-message box). Not verified: a real clipboard Cmd+V (the paste job is unchanged, its result now becomes a chip), a real agent run, a click on a chip opening Preview, emoji are drawn monochrome (egui cannot draw color glyphs) and only single code point emoji are offered. The emoji use counts live in memory for the run, not in a file: the app has no place for such a count yet.
- **The reply box shows Claude's next-prompt suggestion as grey ghost text, and Tab takes it (branch `feat/prompt-ghost`).** The owner approved option A. Claude Code 2.1.310 draws the suggestion as the placeholder of its empty input line: after the `>` prompt and a space, the first letter as the inverse cursor and the rest SGR 2 faint, between the two rules of the input box (read from the placeholder renderer in the installed bundle, then a stand-in `claude` printing exactly that). `prompt_ghost::read` takes the terminal's `GridSnap` (stdusk already exposes `dim`) and returns the faint run, or nothing for typed text, a prompt outside a box or anything odd; `Ghost` reads the screen only when the output count moved, and only for an idle Claude tab with rows. The reply box draws it as `MUTED` hint text; Tab in the empty box makes it real text with the cursor at the end, Enter on an empty box sends nothing, and Tab elsewhere keeps its focus move.
  Checked: table tests for the parser, headless frames for ghost shown, Tab fills, typing hides it, Enter sends nothing, a mutation of the Tab fill fails its test, and a capture in a throwaway home and repo with a stand-in `claude` (the grey `run the whole test suite` in the reply box). Not verified: a real Claude session (no login here), so the inverse-cursor and focus-reporting variants are read from the bundle only.
- **The Reader draws full CommonMark and GitHub markdown itself (branch `feat/reader-markdown`).** The owner: "I want this to be supreme!", and a list under a heading should be "tabbed compared to the heading / paragraph". egui_commonmark 0.24 (the build for egui 0.35; 0.25 and later need a newer egui) cannot host this: it drops a loose item's second paragraph, ends a list at a code block, draws no heading sizes and no quote bar, and gives no hook for our code card, links or selection. So parsing is `pulldown-cmark` 0.13 with tables, task lists, strikethrough and footnotes (`md_blocks`, never hand parsed) and only the drawing is ours (`md_view`). Lists nest to any depth (2 or 4 spaces, tabs, mixed, tight or loose, several paragraphs or a code block per item), a list sits 30 px in from the text above and each level 30 px more, bullets are a dot, a ring and a square by level, numbers keep their start, wrapped lines hang under the item text, task items are drawn check boxes (read only), h1 to h6 get sizes, quotes nest with a left bar, a table keeps its in-column scroll, images are a teal alt-text chip, footnotes land after a rule, inline HTML shows as inert text. Text is selectable and Cmd+C copies it, web links stay clickable. `reader_markdown::pieces` no longer cuts a fence or quote that sits inside a list item. `egui_commonmark` is no longer a Tidewatch dependency. Numbers: 21 `md_blocks` tests, 11 `md_view` headless frames (indent step, hanging wrap, numbers, bullet shapes, task boxes, heading sizes, quote bars, code in an item, selection plus copy, link click), 1 `pieces` test; fmt, clippy and `cargo test --workspace` clean. Checked: screenshots in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and a fake `gh` (the owner's example, a 4-level mixed list with wrapped lines, numbers from 3, tasks, h1 to h6, nested quote, table, code in a list item, inline styles, footnote, a user bubble with a list). Not verified: a real agent run, Linux, a very long message's frame time (the tree is parsed each frame for drawn rows), a table cell with a link on screen, right-aligned table columns by eye beyond one sample.
- **The Overview is a board with an agenda rail, and a quiet terminal no longer needs you (branch `feat/overview-agenda`).** The owner approved the design ("Looks great!"). A header with the summary line and repo chips (`All N`, one per repo; the pick lasts for the session only), a main column of cards (Needs you, Working three to a row with a ring and a thin bar, two rows then `Show all N`, Idle two to a row) and a 380 px rail: Coming up by day (`TODAY · MON 26`, `TUE 27`, `SAT 31 · ABOUT`, `NO DATE`; parked items say `parked`, items for the owner have a Decide button), Poofed today (the last three, `8 · 1h 37m`, `Show all 8` in place) and Running elsewhere as a compact card. Every card and agenda item carries the repo's colour square and name (a fixed palette picked by a hash of the repo name) and a Claude, Codex, Meeseeks or YOLO pill. Idle reads `Idle 3h · Next: today, <item>`, `Waiting until Sat 31` or `nothing scheduled`, from the data Coming up already reads. The Checklist moved to Settings, General. Status: `status_view::tone` reads `NeedsYou(Quiet)` as Done, so the Overview, its summary, the strip badge, the tab dot, the status bar count and notifications all stop calling it needs-you (a quiet tab is also no longer protected from auto-parking). `AgentRun::since_change` feeds the idle time. `timeline.rs` and `coming_up_view.rs` are gone. Numbers: 2268 tests in the first full run, with new ones for the board and chip filter, the Working cap, the agenda days, idle lines, the Quiet tone and notification, the Overview without a Checklist and the Checklist card in Settings; fmt and clippy `-D warnings` clean. Checked: captures of the full window, a repo chip, eight working agents and the Settings card, from `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_OVERVIEW` stand-ins in a throwaway home. Not verified: real agents, a real Coming up index, real Running elsewhere sessions, Linux, windows under 900 px by eye (a headless test only checks overlap).

## 2026-10-25

- **Tidewatch's own messages are quiet cards, queued replies show at once, GitHub HTML is hidden, and unanswered questions are picked after five minutes (branch `feat/reader-sent-messages`).** The owner: "this looks like a rendering bug yeah?", "is it possible these messages tidewatch sends or whoever appear differently", "when I send a message and something is queued I would like to see that it's sent", "if it asks a question from me and I don't answer in like 5 minutes tidewatch should somehow autopick the recommended option". Auto-fix prompts and every other paste Tidewatch types for the user (review notes, comment guard, done check, due and avatar asks, Meeseeks tasks, Build workflow) are matched against the transcript's user line by `reader_sent` (equal up to spacing, or a long paste the agent kept only the start of, or its `[Pasted text #1 +N lines]` note) and draw once as the quiet `Tidewatch` card, never a `You` bubble; the PR card timeline uses the same match with 60 s of clock slack. A reply sent while the agent is Working draws at once as a dimmed bubble with the app's spinner and `Queued`, and becomes the normal bubble when the transcript has it (dropped after 15 minutes if it never does). `github_html::scrub` removes HTML comments, turns `<br>`, `<details>`, `<summary>` and `<relative-time>` into markdown, and runs before every Reader markdown draw; a markdown table scrolls inside its own bar and every message is clipped to its column. A question left unanswered for 5 minutes in a YOLO or Meeseeks tab is answered with the recommended option, else the first (`TIDEWATCH_DEV_AUTOPICK_SECS` shortens the wait); the card shows `Picks the recommended answer in 4:12` and the answered card reads `Picked by Tidewatch`. Numbers: 46 new tests (scrub tables, table cuts and width, sent matching, queued and Tidewatch cards in real frames, countdown with a fake clock, the three glue paths); fmt, clippy and `cargo test --workspace` clean. Checked: screenshots in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and a fake `gh` (Auto-fix card with no `You` bubble, a queued reply, a review comment full of HTML and a wide table in a 1000 px window, the countdown line and the picked card). Not verified: a real agent run, how real Claude Code records a large paste (the match tolerates the shapes known from 2.1.310), a Codex, Gemini or Copilot transcript, Linux.
- **Move always works for a session running elsewhere (branch `fix/move-always`).** The owner: "this should be auto migrated or something we should never have a situation where the button is disabled, always a solution." A YOLO session in a folder that is not a git repo had Move disabled. Now Move is never disabled: a folder that is not a git repo keeps YOLO (`prepare_resume_in_folder` lifts the worktree-only launch guard for a moved session, writes nothing into the folder) with a neutral line "This folder is not a git repo, so it keeps running YOLO here as it did before."; a Claude YOLO session in a repo opens the dialog with "Move it into a new worktree first" ticked, and unticking it moves it here at the highest level the CLI has below YOLO ("YOLO only runs in a worktree, so it moves here as Auto."); a CLI that cannot resume from another folder does the same. Move all idle includes YOLO sessions by the same rules (a new worktree per Claude session in a repo). Push guard and every other guard unchanged. Tests: table tests for the three dialog cases, the default tick, the lowered preview, `window_plan` levels, the batch worktree choice, and a core test of the in-folder resume. Not checked: a real YOLO session moved out of a non-git folder, and a restart restoring that tab.
- **History keeps one control row (branch `fix/history-header`).** The owner: "these tabs changes / history look awkward". The History panel stacked two segmented controls, Changes | History in the header and This branch | Whole repo under it. The second is gone: the scope is a small ghost chip at the right end of the search field (outline only, the label and a chevron, sized for the longer label so nothing shifts), and a click opens a two-item menu that sets the same `TabView` scope as before. The field now matches the header segment (28 px high, 7 px corners) and its edges line up with the header row's content edges (18 px left, 10 px right). The match count sits left of the chip.
  Checked: search bar tests (hint and chip inside the field's right end, field edges and height, chip opens the menu and Whole repo changes the scope, chip keeps size and place across scopes), before and after crops in a throwaway home with a fake `gh` and a demo repo. Not verified: a very narrow side panel (the text area shrinks to its minimum), the menu popup by eye.
- **Slash commands in the reply box: dialogs open in the terminal, and the box completes names (branch `fix/input-polish`).** The owner: "if I type stuff like /login or /usage in the chat window can we deal with that nicely ... even more awesome would be also to have autocomplete for all commands / skills". Sending a bare `/login`, `/logout`, `/usage`, `/config`, `/model`, `/mcp`, `/permissions`, `/resume`, `/agents`, `/plugin`, `/status`, `/theme` (and the other built-ins that open a picker; `/model opus` with arguments does not) to a Claude tab sends it as before, then switches the tab to its terminal with the note `/login opened in the terminal` and a Back to Reader button. Output commands (`/context`, `/compact`, `/clear`) stay in the Reader. Typing `/` at the start of the box lists matching commands above it, in the command palette's row look: built-in commands, plus skills and custom commands read from the repo's `.claude`, the user's Claude folder and plugin folders, each with its one-line description. Up and Down move, Tab or Enter fills `/name `, Enter on a whole name still sends, Esc closes the list and keeps the keyboard. The disk is read once per folder on a job thread; the built-ins show at once. The built-in names come from Claude Code 2.1.310's own command table; other CLIs get no list. Numbers: 26 new tests (routing, filter and order, front-matter parsing, disk loading with dedupe, the keys in real frames). Checked: a crop of the open list in a throwaway home with two user skills and one repo skill, a stand-in `claude` and a fake `gh`. Not verified: a real `/login` round trip through the real CLI, plugin skills on a real plugin cache (a test builds one), a skill added while the app runs (the list is read once per folder per run).
- **The reply box centres one line and shows bullets (branch `fix/input-polish`).** One line of text is now centred in the box and level with the Send button (it was top-aligned, so it sat high whenever the row was taller than one line; at the Reader's usual row the gap was about a point). A line that starts with `- ` or `* ` shows as a bullet with a hanging indent while you type, like a chat app; the text sent stays exactly what was typed, and the caret, selection and copy still work on it (the bullet replaces the marker's first character one for one, drawn through an egui layouter). Enter on a bullet line starts the next bullet, Enter on an empty bullet ends the list (the next Enter sends), Shift+Enter is still a plain new line, and the Send button always sends. Numbers: 21 new tests (marker rules, bullet sections, hanging rows, list continuation and ending, sent text unchanged, centring at the Reader's row height and a taller one, growth from the top). Checked: before and after crops in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and a fake `gh` (`/login`, a 4-item list). Not verified: a real agent run, a wrapped bullet line on screen (covered by a headless test only), Enter on a bullet line in the new-tab and composer boxes (not changed).
- **A Meeseeks task is read-only or an edit (branch `feat/meeseeks-kind`).** The owner: "let's build A ... question / investigation basically readonly vs edit right? and something can start as readonly and be promoted to edit". A new Meeseeks starts read-only: stages Understand, Answer, Done, no tests, commit or PR check, and it poofs when it says it is done with its summary as the answer. It becomes an edit (Understand, Build, PR, Done, the existing checks) when the agent writes `kind: edit` in its ledger Status, or when Tidewatch sees a changed file or a commit since `start_head` (a look every 3 s and before every done, through kelp's working status), never back. An edit that says done with no diff skips its checks and shows Build and PR as skipped. `Meeseeks.kind` and `start_head` are saved in `session.toml`; a Meeseeks saved without `kind` loads as an edit. A summary starting `Blocked:` poofs with no checks and shows the reason. A push to a branch with an open PR (`gh pr list`, the open PR whose head is the worktree's HEAD) hands off that PR instead of opening one, and the Change check counts commits on HEAD as well as on the `agent/` branch. Read-only is a label, not a lock. A read-only summary is its answer: up to 4000 characters and several lines, while an edit's stays one line of at most 200 (Tidewatch pastes a note asking to shorten it). The summary tab titles its card Answered, Blocked or Nothing to check when no check ran. Not checked: a real agent writing `kind:` or `Blocked:`.
- **Nine Reader cards redesigned (branch `feat/reader-cards`).** The owner approved the design board ("the designs are a great success"): the answered question, quote, live question, permission, tool activity, background task, user bubble with Your answers, slash command, quick replies, compaction divider, PR and sent review cards. All buttons ghost, no coloured left edges. The old Skip and Go with your picks buttons are one `Let <agent> pick`: Go with your picks (answer every waiting question with its recommended pick) when the tab has picks, else Skip (tell the agent to use its judgment).
  Checked: headless tests (quote and image pieces, the open-list sections, the answered card layout, the background note parser), before and after crops in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and a fake `gh`. Not verified: a real agent run, a real image thumbnail or pixel size (the transcript carries no pixels, so the chip shows a glyph), a background task duration (the note has none, so none is shown).
- **Amphetamine asks for Touch ID once per run (branch `feat/amphetamine-once`).** The first activation of a run still asks (Touch ID, or the one-time setup prompt on a Mac without the helper) and starts the installed helper in a `serve` mode as root. It reads `on <deadline>` and `off` lines from a command pipe `.state/amphetamine.serve` that Tidewatch makes with mode 0600, and answers by writing its state into the token file, so later activations and deactivations in the same run send one line with no prompt. The helper still restores sleep at the deadline, on `off`, and when Tidewatch's pid is gone or its token is removed (then it exits). A serving helper that does not answer or is gone falls back to today's prompt. An installed helper that is an older script routes as Outdated: the start asks for the password with a notice, and the Keep awake row offers Set up again. The owner must run that setup once after upgrading. Tests: seven shell tests of `serve` against the fake pmset and sleep rig, system-seam tests for the pipe, the fallbacks and the outdated route, three glue tests (second activation, helper gone, outdated), and a real mkfifo mode check. Gates: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, and `cargo test --workspace --locked` with 3642 passing. Not checked live: a real Touch ID prompt, a real `sudo -n serve` on a Mac with the helper installed, and the re-setup. Password-only Macs still ask on every activation, since no helper is installed there.
- **An untracked folder is one row in Changes (branch `feat/untracked-folder-rows`).** A new folder of 300 files listed 300 rows and the pill said `99+`, where `git status` shows one line. Kelp (`3d846f0`, Hobo-Ware/kelp#30) now lists an untracked folder as one `dir/` entry with a file count; both kelp pins moved to it (lockfile `sed`, `--locked` build fine). `Groups` carries the folders; the Changes row is `dir/` with `N files` in place of `+a -d`, a click does nothing, and the footer and the pill count entries like `git status`. `Groups::diffable` (was `ordered`) leaves folders out, so `history::changed_files`, the default pick, next and previous file and the footer `+N -M` only see real files. Tests: `a_folder_row_shows_its_name_with_the_slash_and_how_many_files_it_holds`, `a_click_on_a_folder_row_opens_nothing_in_either_layout`, `the_footer_keeps_the_line_totals_of_the_real_files_beside_a_folder`, `an_untracked_folder_is_a_row_with_no_diff_and_the_file_walk_skips_it` (a real repo).
  Checked: before and after crops in a throwaway home and repo (a folder of 300 files, two changed files, one new file): before 301 new files and `99+`, after 2 new files, `300 files` on the folder row, pill `4`, footer `+6 -2`. Not checked: a folder of 300 files in a real checkout with live edits, a nested untracked folder inside an untracked one on screen, the graph's uncommitted row (kelp-ui draws it from the same entries).
- **Tidewatch stays up while Amphetamine asks for the password (branch `fix/amphetamine-prompt-focus`).** The owner: "when the prompt appears tidewatch goes somehow in the background, can we somehow avoid this?" Root cause: the start prompt (the osascript `with administrator privileges` dialog, or the Touch ID sheet) is drawn by another process, so Tidewatch loses key focus and app activation; with quake on, `Quake::hides_on_blur` read that as the user clicking away and hid the drop-down, and when the prompt closed nothing asked for the focus back, so the window stayed behind other windows. Fix: `Host::prompt_open` (state `Starting`, or the Touch ID setup or removal job) goes into `Shell::tick`; `quake::Prompt` is the one pure rule: it holds hide-on-blur while the prompt is open, and when it ends (any result) sends one `ViewportCommand::Focus` if the app was active on the frame before it opened, never otherwise (a window hidden meanwhile, and a background or screenshot run, get none). The hold lasts up to 1 s after the end so the hide cannot win the race with the focus coming back. Tests: table tests for `Prompt` and for `hides_on_blur` with the hold, shell frames with the recorder (hold, exactly one Focus however many frames follow, none when another app was active, none when hidden meanwhile, plain window, background), glue tests for `prompt_open`; the two shell tests that carry the rule fail when the hold is switched off. LAContext sheet in the window: not possible with `objc2-local-authentication` 0.3.2 (no `LAAuthenticationView`, which lives in the separate LocalAuthenticationEmbeddedUI framework; the sheet is drawn by coreauthd) and never for osascript, so not built.
  Not verified: any live behavior (no real prompt, password dialog, Touch ID or focus change was triggered, as asked), that macOS reports the app inactive on the frame after the click, or that `Focus` raises the window over other apps after the dialog closes. Not covered: the launch recovery prompt (`Recovery::NeedsPassword`) and a stop that falls back to the password.
- **Agent tabs may push any branch but the default one (branch `fix/push-guard-main-only`).** The owner, after a YOLO tab could not push the branch of an existing PR made by another tab (`agent/investigate-fix-season`): "I thought we only block pushes on main!" The push helper now refuses only the repo's default branch (`prepare` reads it with `start_base::remote_default` and sets `TIDEWATCH_PUSH_PROTECTED`; `main` and `master` stay refused if nothing resolves), tags and non-branch refs; any other branch goes in every form including force and `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<sha>`, and deleting a branch other than the default is allowed. `TIDEWATCH_PUSH_BRANCH` and `LaunchCtx.push_branch` are removed (only the helper read them; the done check, delivery and restore never did, and a saved session holds no branch, so an old session still launches). The refusal is one line naming the default branch. The tool guidance and the profile and Guards labels no longer say "own branch".
  Checked: `tests/push_guard.rs` against a real bare remote (another tab's branch in 8 forms plus by sha and a delete; 20 refused spellings of the default branch, tags, `--tags`, `--all`, `--mirror` and mixed batches that change nothing; a default named `trunk` refused by name with `main` and `master` still refused; no default resolved; no helper), and two `prepare` tests (default `trunk` read from `origin/HEAD` and named in the env; a main-checkout tab gets none). `cargo fmt`, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace --locked` pass. Probed by hand: `git push --all` with no local default branch pushes the other local branches (git sends it as one push per branch), so `--all` is refused only when the default branch is among the local ones. Not checked: Linux, an ssh or https remote with credentials, a live agent shell. A running tab keeps its old env (no `TIDEWATCH_PUSH_PROTECTED`) until restarted; it picks up the new helper as soon as any tab prepares, and then refuses `main` and `master` only.
- **Code blocks in the Reader get a proper copy button, and the end block lines become captions (branch `feat/reader-code-blocks`).** The owner: "The copy icon and stuff can use some loving". A fenced block in an agent or user bubble was a near-black slab with no padding and egui_commonmark's tiny unlabelled icon over the first line (the crate has no hook for it, so the Reader now cuts a message at its top-level fences, `reader_markdown::pieces`, and draws them itself; the crate is not forked). `code_block` draws a `SUNKEN` well (black tint in the teal user bubble) with a 1 px `ITEM_LINE` border, 8 px corners, 12 px padding, mono 14 px and a header strip: the language muted at the left, a ghost `Copy` button at the right (painted two-rect glyph, tooltip `Copy to clipboard`). A click copies the block's text exactly as fenced (no stripping of a leading `! `) and the button reads `Copied` with a check for 1.5 s at the same width. The code can never run under the button: it sits in its own strip. Long lines wrap with a hanging indent (the line's indent plus two columns) rather than scroll, because a long `git push` command is the one thing you want to read whole and a screenshot of it must not be cut off; the cost is that a selection across a wrap point copies the added newline and indent, and the Copy button is the exact route. `rust`, `py` and `toml` fences keep their highlighting through `egui_extras` (new direct dependency, already in the lock). In an agent bubble the heading-only lines of the last complete Done / Needs you / Next block (`end_block::caption_lines`; `**Done:** x` and a sentence starting with "Done" are not) are small 13 px semibold `MUTED` captions with 10 px above them; a heading inside a fence is not one.
  Checked: headless frames (the code never under the button from 180 to 720 px wide, wrapped rows hold all the text, the click puts the exact text in `platform_output` and flips to Copied and back after 1.5 s, a click on the code copies nothing, the caption font and colour), table tests for the fence cut, `wrap_text` and `caption_lines`; before and after crops in a throwaway home (`/tmp/twc`, a stand-in `claude` and a hand-written transcript with a long command fence, a short rust fence and the three sections; the user bubble with a sh fence). Not checked: the real clipboard (never touched), a fence inside a list or a quote (stays with egui_commonmark and its old look), Codex, Gemini and Copilot transcripts, a block of thousands of lines (wrapped each frame while visible), Linux.
- **A worktree tab's panels read its own worktree (branch `fix/meeseeks-panel-folder`).** The owner on 0.1.22: a Meeseeks tab's History card header, Changes and History showed the main checkout (`chore/doorkeeper_schema_cleanup`, 222 behind main, the main checkout's staged and untracked files) while the Meeseeks ran in its own worktree on `agent/<name>` with 4 changed files. Root cause: the new-tab form is a draft tab that gets a view over the repo root, and Start turns that tab into the worktree tab with the same id; `drive_views` kept one view per id and never checked its folder, so the draft's view stayed. Not specific to Meeseeks: any agent started from the form (a Meeseeks summon's extra tabs get new ids and were right). Fix: `ViewSpec::view_in` drops a view that reads another folder than the tab's spec (this also gives the tab its notes file, which the draft view had none of). Test `a_draft_that_becomes_a_worktree_tab_swaps_its_view_for_one_over_the_worktree` fails without the check (left `/work/web`, right the worktree path). `TIDEWATCH_DEV_DIALOG` with `TIDEWATCH_DEV_START=1` now presses Start once the draft's view exists, so a capture reproduces the real order (before, it started in the first frame and could not show the bug).
  Checked: before and after captures in a throwaway home and repo (main checkout on another branch with 60 untracked files and a staged one, a stand-in `claude` that edits 4 files, `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_MEESEEKS=on`): before the header named the main branch and Changes listed 61 files, after it names `agent/clean-up-doorkeeper-schema-3` and lists the 4. Not checked: restored Meeseeks and ordinary agent tabs by eye (they open from a Creating tab or a new id, never from a draft, and the unit test covers the id reuse), a real Meeseeks. **Not done:** an untracked folder as one row in Changes. The list comes from `kelp_core::status::working_status`, which runs `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all` (kelp `crates/kelp-core/src/status.rs`, pinned here at rev edf3eaa), so the folder is already expanded before Tidewatch sees it; the change belongs in kelp (use `--untracked-files=normal` for the list and keep a file count per folder, or a separate collapse) and then a rev bump here.
- **A poofed Meeseeks leaves a summary tab (branch `feat/poofed-summary`).** The owner: "meeseeks tabs autoclose but users should still receive a summary view at the end. The meeseeks should've poofed but the statuses etc should still be visible." The poof is unchanged (process ends, worktree folder removed, branch kept), but the tab no longer fades away: it becomes a read-only `Body::Poofed` tab with the Meeseeks face dimmed, its title kept, no status dot, never working or needs-you, parked like an idle tab. Its view replaces the terminal and panels: the task, `Poofed · 6m · 11:42`, the stage card with four bars, each check with its result and the line it said (`2 commits ahead of main`, `Opened PR #213 against main`), the agent's `meeseeks_done` summary, the PR link, the branch with commits ahead and files changed with +/- totals (read from the branch on a `Jobs` thread, a deleted branch says so), the last turn's Done and Next, the ledger's stages and one Close button (Cmd+W too). `.state/meeseeks.toml` records gain `#[serde(default)]` fields (task, repo, branch, `ended_ms`, checks, last turn, stages), so old files load; a summary tab is saved in `session.toml` as `SavedTab::Poofed` with its whole record, restored as a summary and carried through a restart handover; Overview's Poofed today rows are clickable and open (or select) it. The fade code went away with the change.
  Checked: table tests for the summary model and `check_lines`, headless tests for the view (Close on release only, PR link opens, no overlap from 320 pt wide), a real-git test that the branch is read after the folder is gone, restore and handover round trips, an old `meeseeks.toml` loads; `cargo test --workspace` 2135 app and 1269 core tests plus the integration files pass, clippy and fmt clean. Screenshots of the tab and the strip from `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_MEESEEKS=poofed` in a throwaway home with a fake gh. Not checked: a real Meeseeks poofing end to end with a real agent, and the summary after the owner's branch is merged or deleted on GitHub.
- **The Reader shows your answers as a card and background task notes as quiet rows (branch `feat/answers-card`).** The owner: when you answer an agent's questions (question card or Go with your picks) the Reader showed the pasted `My answers to your earlier questions:` / `Q:` / `A:` text as plain text, and Claude Code's `<task-notification>` records showed as a "You" bubble full of raw tags. Now `question::parse_answers` reads that exact message back (round trip with `answer_text` and `late_answer_text`, with or without the intro and the `<pasted_content>` wrapper; prose that only mentions `Q:` is not one) and the user bubble draws a muted `Your answers` heading, each question in muted 14 px and its answer under it in 16 px, left-aligned, links clickable. `user_markup::classify` turns a line that is only a `<task-notification>` (with a summary) into `Record::Background`, a muted `Background task - <summary>` note with the first line of its event under it; a message that merely mentions the tag stays a bubble. The transcript and what the agent received are unchanged. Multi-line user text was already left-aligned on main, checked and left as is.
  Checked: headless tests (the bubble draws the heading and pairs without `Q:`/`A:`, a prose `Q:` stays text), table tests for both parsers, before/after screenshots in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and a hand-written transcript. Not checked: Codex, Gemini and Copilot transcripts, a real agent's answers on screen, Linux.
- **The last turn's summary moves into the Progress panel (branch `feat/last-turn-panel`).** The owner: "Can these things under the text input be better placed in the side panel here in the progress? we have a lot of empty space anyway and I don't think they need to be these long ass slim cards they can be actions or something within the progress panel". The Done / Needs you / Next card and the done check band under the Reader are gone. The Progress panel has a Last turn section under the stages and above the checklist: Done as a list, Needs you as a list with Answer (the existing handler; hidden when the ledger already asks a question, so there is one Answer), Next muted, and the done check as a dot row with its lines and its two existing buttons. A tab with a summary or a check but no ledger now has the Progress tab too. While Changes or History shows, an orange dot on the Progress tab marks Needs you items or a done check the panel has not shown yet (`TabState.seen`); showing the section clears it. `LastTurn` is plain data (`last_turn.rs`), the section is `last_turn_view.rs`; `end_block_view.rs` and `AgentRun.end_open` are gone. Tests: 2076 + 5 + 14 pass, including headless frames for the section (order, buttons, no ledger, no overlap from 200 px wide up) and for the dot. Checked: before and after captures in a throwaway home (a stand-in `claude` posting a Stop hook with an end block, `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_DONE_CHECK=unverified`). Not checked: a dot while the panel is closed (focus mode and the Overview hide it, and there is no toggle to carry the dot), a real agent.
- **Ghost buttons everywhere, and a setting for what Enter does on close (branch `feat/ghost-buttons`).** The owner: "I want by default to have delete worktree and I want all buttons to just have that ghost look ... I no longer want huge splashes of color anywhere." `primary_button` and `primary_button_keyed` are ghost (1 px teal line, teal text, no fill, a faint teal tint on hover and press, 40% disabled), `danger_button` is a red line and red text with a faint red tint on hover; the welcome screen's Open repo and the file view's Send review pill moved onto the same look. The close dialog keeps Cancel, Keep branch, Delete branch; Enter presses Delete branch by default, and Settings, General, Tabs has "When closing a tab, Enter" (`[tabs] close_enter` = `delete` or `keep`; files without the key load as delete). The dialog itself always opens; no setting skips it. Left filled on purpose: switch tracks, check boxes and radio dots, the Amphetamine tablet, status dots, 2 px bars, selection and pill tints. Branch deletes that do not go through the close dialog: the merged PR card's Remove worktree and branch (after a second safety check), the leftovers dialog's Clean up (branches with nothing unmerged) and the rollback of a failed takeover's new worktree.
  Checked: before and after crops of the close dialog, the Reader's Send and Answer, the new-tab form's Start, the Settings save bar and the new row, in a throwaway home. Not checked: the PR cards and delivery card by eye (same `primary_button` call sites).
- **Agent tabs share the repo's memory and leave out attribution lines (branch `fix/tab-memory-trailers`).** The owner: every tab starts with empty memory and agents still add `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailers and "Generated with" footers. A Claude worktree tab's `--settings` file now holds `autoMemoryDirectory` (the repo's own `projects/<encoded path>/memory` folder, `claude_memory.rs`; `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` honored; nothing is written under the Claude folder) and, with the Trailers guard on for the repo, `attribution` with empty `commit` and `pr` and `sessionUrl: false`, plus a PreToolUse hook on Bash (`tidewatch --guard trailers`, `guard_bash.rs`) that refuses `gh pr create|edit|comment|review` and `gh issue create|comment|edit` text with a trailer or footer, through the same `find_trailer` the shims use.
  Checked: Claude Code 2.1.310 documents `autoMemoryDirectory` for `--settings` and `attribution` (empty string hides it, `includeCoAuthoredBy` is deprecated); a live `claude -p --settings` run files memory where the setting says and the real CLI blocks a `gh issue comment` with a trailer through the hook. A plain `claude -p` in an existing Tidewatch worktree already named the main checkout's memory folder, so the empty-memory report did not reproduce there; the setting makes the folder explicit. Not checked: an interactive tab, and Claude Code's own trailer being absent in a real commit.
- **The agent hears about open checks, and merged branches stay quiet (branch `fix/merged-branch-quiet`).** The owner: "So you see I verified live but it still says verified live is pending". He told the agent in chat that he had verified live, the agent never called `update_progress`, so the Not verified item stayed. Root cause: the `UserPromptSubmit` nudge only fired while the ledger awaited the owner. Now `prompt_answer::ledger_context` adds one line to every prompt while the ledger holds open items (Not verified entries, then unchecked, in-progress and blocked steps): `Open in your ledger: "Verified live"; ... (+N more). If this message changes any of them, call update_progress.`, up to 3 names of 60 characters, nothing when none is open; a ledger that awaits the owner keeps its own line. Second report: a YOLO tab whose PR had merged opened a new PR when it went idle with a commit on the branch (the lookup only finds open PRs, so the delivery card saw "no PR" and opened itself). Now `merged_branch::verdict` shuts the delivery of a tab that runs on its own once the PR merged (no probe, no card, no auto-open, no push-fix) and also while its saved cards are unread or its watch does not exist yet; the watch counts the commits beyond the merged head once per idle moment (`Cards::after_merge`, one `git rev-list`), and the merged card shows `1 commit after merge, not delivered` as its quiet note: no button, no color. Open PR, no PR and a tab that waits for the owner are unchanged. Numbers: table tests for the ledger line (nothing open, pending check, done ledger, steps, ordering, more than 3, long name, awaits-owner), the verdict, the watch (merged, closed, new PR on the same branch, recount, unread cards), the card note and the flow gate; fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean. Checked: a throwaway home and repo, a stand-in `claude` that commits on the branch, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PR_EVENTS` and a fake `gh`: a YOLO tab with a merged card shows the note, opens no dialog and makes no PR call. **Not verified:** a real agent reading the new line and calling `update_progress` (only the hook answer is tested), a real squash-merged branch with a commit left on it, a Meeseeks hand-off (`check::hand_off` is the agent's own call and is not gated). **Open:** in an early capture batch 6 of 8 runs still showed the "pull request was not opened" dialog on the merged tab; about 50 later runs, with and without CPU load, showed none, and the cause was not found.
- **A stale ledger question no longer says the owner is needed (branch `fix/stale-ledger-question`).** The owner: the Progress panel said `Waiting · updated 8 min ago` with an amber question and an Answer button, the Overview card a `1 question` tag, while the tab was Working. Root cause: the ledger was only ever read on its own, so an agent that saved `waiting` with a question, got its answer in chat and built on without calling `update_progress` kept showing the old ask next to a live Working status. Fix: `progress_stale::is_stale` (ledger awaits the owner, saved before the later of the last turn start and the start of Working, no Permission or Question hook asking) makes `fresh` read the ledger as Working with no question for the Progress panel, the Overview card and the tab ring, and the state line says `Working · progress updated 8 min ago`; a ledger saved after the turn started, a `Done` ledger with a hand-off, and a ledger with no known save time stand. Nudge: the listener answers `UserPromptSubmit` with `additionalContext` "The user answered; call update_progress so your ledger shows the new status before you continue." while the saved ledger has a question or a waiting or blocked state, joined with the avatar ask when both apply (`prompt_answer.rs`). Checked: a throwaway home and repo, a stand-in `claude` posting hooks, a ledger file written 8 minutes back and the tab restored: before showed Waiting, the question card and Answer, after shows Working, `progress updated 8 min ago` and no card; the stand-in read the nudge in the hook answer. Not checked: a real Claude session, the turn start of a tab restored with no hook seen (it falls back to when the Working status began, so a restored Done tab shows its old question until the agent saves).
- **Four stages, and the agent writes its own steps (branch `feat/four-stages`).** The owner: "Validate against production is a strange step to have before the PR ... super specific to how I work in one repo. Maybe it should be the agent that reports the steps instead of us having a list. We can have some basics like Understanding, Building, PR, Done and have the agent tell us what steps it's taking in between for each session so we're dynamic to the situation." Every session now has the same four stages (Understand, Build, PR, Done): the bars and `Step N of 4 · <stage>` use them, and the ledger instruction tells the agent to keep exactly those four `###` headings, write its own concrete steps under each as it plans, reshape them as the work changes and mark them as it goes. `checklist.md` (or a repo's `.tidewatch.toml` `checklist`) is now an optional list of must-do items the agent adds as steps where they fit; the default asks for nothing and a new install gets a note-only file. Old ledgers still load: `stages::stage_of` maps the old ten phases onto the four (the one table), their items are carried under their stage, and a phase with any other name draws as before. The Overview's Checklist row shows the four stages when there is no list, else the list's items (8, then `+N more`).
  Decisions: a `checklist.md` that still holds exactly the old 10-phase default counts as no file (never overwritten, never sent to agents), any edited file is sent verbatim as must-do items; the tab tip and the Overview card line name the stage too.
  Checked: before and after captures in a throwaway home (an old 10-phase ledger, a new-style one, the Overview). Not checked: a real agent writing its own steps.
- **Clickable links in the Reader, the Progress panel and the end block card (branch `feat/reader-links`).** The owner: "It would also be nice to have clickable links". `links.rs` finds `http(s)` URLs, markdown links and bare `localhost:<port>` / `127.0.0.1:<port>` (opened as `http://`), with trailing punctuation and closing backticks left out, and a URL in inline code counted as a link; `file.rs:12`, `a:b`, `16:02`, `PR #12`, emails and every other scheme stay plain text. The Reader rewrites each agent message through `links::markdown` before egui_commonmark draws it, so every link is a real markdown link, drawn in `brand::TEAL_TEXT` with the full address as its tooltip (Kelp's theme sets `override_text_color`, which had kept even `[text](url)` links white, so the bubble drops it for the markdown scope), and a click on any link that is not http or https is dropped from the frame's output (a reference-style `[x][r]` with `[r]: javascript:...` reached the renderer). The Progress panel (goal, question card, item lines and their why notes, verified, not verified and decisions lists) and the end block card (collapsed line and the three columns) lay text out through `link_text::Text`: teal, underline on hover, pointing hand, tooltip, click opens a new browser tab; a link click in the end block card does not toggle the card. Tests: 15 new (the finder tables, headless clicks in a chat bubble, the Progress panel and the card, a `javascript:` / `file:` link never opens); 2027 + 5 + 14 pass. Also fixed a test that no longer compiled on main (`agent_ready.rs` missed the new `Facts.tab`).
- **A finished turn shows its final answer and folds the progress notes (branch `fix/turn-final-answer`).** The owner: "there's a blob of text and then markdowned? I would've extended only the markdown output... from here onward". Root cause: a turn was one bubble holding every text the agent wrote in order, so ten one-line notes between tool calls stacked as a blob over the final markdown answer. Now the bubble holds only the turn's last text, finished or still running (a running turn shows its latest text with the live pill, as one live line, not a growing blob), and the earlier texts fold under a `12 updates` chip over the bubble (same pill as `45 commands`, a painted three-line icon); a click opens them as a muted list in order, a second click folds it. The list is the notes only, not interleaved with the calls: the call chips keep their own list. The open state lives in the same set as the chip lists, under the turn's key with `NOTES_BIT` set. A turn with one text is unchanged. The last text wins even when it is a short sign-off after a long answer (`reader_turns::Group::answer`). The Jump to latest pill is now opaque (card fill, 1 px line, teal text, a small shadow) at the same place. `reader_turns` is pure and table-tested (finished and running, one text, many texts, toggle); headless frames pin that a turn with notes is exactly one chip row taller than the same turn without them, that the notes stay hidden until the chip is clicked and fold again on a second click.
  Checked: `docs/reader-turn-before.png`, `docs/reader-turn-after.png`, `docs/reader-turn-notes-open.png` (a 1100 pt wide window, throwaway home and repo, a stand-in `claude` with a hand-written transcript of ten progress notes between tool calls and a final markdown answer). `TIDEWATCH_DEV_TURNS=notes` opens only the folded updates for the capture. Not checked: Codex, Gemini and Copilot transcripts on screen, a running turn in the live app with a real agent, the Jump pill over text at a real window edge, Linux.
- **The reply box wraps and grows instead of scrolling sideways (branch `fix/reply-box-multiline`).** The owner: "when I type too much within an input it just clips out of existence, so I cannot read what I am typing anymore", in the Reader's "Reply to the agent" box, a single line field whose start vanished. It is now a multiline, word wrapped `TextEdit` in a `ScrollArea`: Enter sends, Shift+Enter adds a line, a paste keeps its newlines, Send stays at the bottom right. It starts one row tall and grows by the composer's own rule (`agent_ready::grown_height`, now taking its base height; `grown_from` feeds it a text and a wrap width) up to half the column, then scrolls inside with the cursor row in view (a paste that resizes the box scrolls to the cursor again, because egui's own scroll to the cursor ran in a frame where the box still had its old size). The conversation above shrinks with it and a following Reader sticks to its newest message when its viewport changes height (`body_h`; before, only new content did that). The status bar shows Enter send, Shift+Enter new line while the box has the keyboard. Left single line: the review card's message (the card is absolutely placed rows of fixed height, so growing it is a card layout change of its own), the live question card's "Type your own answer" and the new tab form's name and filter fields (short by nature). The note composer lives in Kelp. Numbers: 8 new tests (60 typed lines and a 2000 character line, each in a 600 and a 360 high window: box above the status bar, at most half the column, newest message above it, Send bottom right, cursor row inside the visible part; Enter and Shift+Enter; a multiline paste; the pure height steps; the Due card above a grown box); the sixty line tests fail without the `body_h` change. Checked in a screenshot run (stand-in `claude`, throwaway home) with a six line reply: box about 200 pt of a 760 pt window, last line visible, Send at its corner. Not checked: typing by hand in a live window, a Cmd+V of a real clipboard picture into the grown box.
- **Amphetamine sets up Touch ID on the first use and shows it is starting (branch `fix/amphetamine-start`).** The owner on 0.1.21: "I pressed [Amphetamine] and it's not activating, it's been like 30-40 seconds and then it prompted for my password". The Touch ID helper was not installed on that Mac (the Settings step is easy to miss), so the start fell back to the osascript prompt. Checked: the start job is not queued behind anything (`Jobs::spawn` is one thread per job, no pool, and a test with 16 blocked jobs shows `start_lid` runs at once), and nothing slow runs before `osascript` (`touch_id_installed` is two `exists` calls). What was provably wrong: entering Starting asked for no frame, so the pending look was not drawn until the next input event (a test that fails without the `request_repaint`). Where the 30-40 s went inside `osascript` / the macOS prompt could not be reproduced without the real prompt. Now: `Route` (helper installed: fingerprint; none but a reader: `SetUp`; neither: password each time, nothing installed); on `SetUp` one admin script installs the helper and drop-in (same `visudo -cf`, PATH and token checks) and starts lid mode, so every later start is Touch ID; a cancelled prompt stops the start, any other setup failure falls back to the plain password prompt. Menu note and tooltip say it ("Asks for your password once to set up Touch ID"). `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_AMPHETAMINE=setup|pending` for captures. Captures (`docs/screenshots/amphetamine-pending.png`, off beside the pending look, 10.5 kB to 6.4 kB at 55 dB; `amphetamine-setup-menu.png`, 84 kB to 18 kB at 52 dB). The amphetamine_lid helper tests wait up to 30 s (were 5 s) so a loaded machine does not fail them.
- **YOLO and Meeseeks tabs act on their own (branch `feat/yolo-autonomous`).** The owner, angry: "When an agent is in yolo mode it should never ask me this type of shit, it should get things done! The whole point of yolo is hyper productivity so apart from design and architectural decisions everything else should be autonomous, no hand holding." and "Same rules apply for meeseeks also." Trigger: a YOLO tab's PR timeline showed an "Ask Builder to answer" card for a Codex review receipt in Ask me mode. `autonomy::autonomous(level, is_meeseeks)` is the one rule (YOLO or Meeseeks). On such a tab the PR watch is Auto-fix whatever the default (the popover still wins), hands comments, review changes, failed checks and conflicts to the idle agent, and keeps each card as a record (`Sent to Builder`) with no Ask, Reply or Push fix button; the loop guard is 10 sends per PR (3 elsewhere) and at the cap it pauses with one line and never falls back to Ask me. `pr_noise::is_noise` drops Codex receipts (marker or footer-only body), "no major issues" notes and deploy, coverage and CI bot posts from the timeline and the comment count for every tab (a ```suggestion fence is never noise; saved cards are cleaned on load). The done check sends its fix note by itself, once per failure, when the agent is idle; due items start by themselves once a day when idle (no Due today card); the delivery card opens, approves and closes itself once per stretch of work (a failure stays with the owner; a Meeseeks keeps its own hand-off), and commits left after an auto-fix message are pushed through it. The house style gets a short `AUTONOMOUS_GUIDANCE` block for YOLO and two folded-in lines in `MEESEEKS_GUIDANCE`. Numbers: new tests in core (autonomy table, noise table, send cap, house style roles, prepare) and the app (watch, card model, view, done check, due, delivery, flow); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PR_EVENTS` in a throwaway home and repo, a stand-in `claude` and a fake `gh`: the same YOLO tab before (Ask me chip, receipt card, three Ask buttons) and after (Auto-fix chip, no receipt, `Sent to Builder`). **Not verified:** a live push through the auto delivery, a live Codex comment, a live auto-sent done-check fix and due start (unit tests only); `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PR_EVENTS` without a fake `gh` still lets looks run once its seed is read (`Dev::seed` is taken), so captures need `TIDEWATCH_DEV_GH_DIR`.
- **0.1.22: slimmer tab strip.** The strip is 48 px (was 64): 4 px above and below the 40 px tabs and icon boxes. The repo chip keeps a 1 px outline at rest and uses 14 px text and a 20 px folder like the tabs, so it no longer floats. Tests: 1942 + 5 + 14 pass.
- **Each agent tab keeps its own Reader scroll and opens at the newest message (branch `fix/reader-scroll-per-tab`).** The owner: "when I switch agent tabs the conversation is not scrolled to bottom" (the Reader showed the conversation a little above the end, with the Jump to latest chip). Root cause: the Reader's scroll area had one id, `reader-scroll`, for every tab, so egui kept one scroll offset for all of them; a tab drawn after another inherited that tab's offset, and its own view state said following, so nothing moved it back. Now the id is salted with the tab's stable id (`Facts::tab`), so each tab keeps its own place (a tab scrolled up stays where it was), and a view that was not drawn in the previous pass (`reader_scroll::returned`: another tab, the terminal, Overview, Settings or a file view was in front) sticks to the bottom once when it was following; the existing re-stick on a content height change covers the first frames while row heights are still estimates. Numbers: 4 new tests (3 headless two-tab runs, 1 table test), all passing; the first two failed before the fix (the second visit to a tab sat at 2341 of 12077). Checked: a throwaway home with two stand-in Claude transcripts (20 and 70 turns), tab A, then B, then A, then B, screenshotted before and after with the same keys: before, the 70-turn tab sat at question 58 with the Jump to latest chip; after, the newest message is fully visible and there is no chip. Not checked: a tab switched while a wheel flick is still running (the old tab's smooth scroll tail can still unpin the new one for a few frames), Linux, a live agent streaming while its tab is hidden.
- **Long messages scroll inside their box instead of running off the window (branch `fix/input-max-height`).** The owner: "when I type too much within an input it just clips out of existence so I cannot read what I am typing anymore". The composer of an agent tab before its first message grew with every line and ran under the status bar, and the workflow task box pushed the stage cards and Start run off the window. The composer is now bottom anchored: it grows with its text up to half the column, then stops and scrolls inside a `ScrollArea` (the `TextEdit` keeps the cursor line in view), and the "<Profile> is ready" header drops its note, then its badge, then itself when the room above the box runs out. The workflow task box is capped to the room left above the stage cards and the footer. The reply box, the review message and the Reader's chat box are single line (they scroll sideways), the new tab form's message box and the delivery body already scroll, and the Settings text areas sit in a scrolled page, so none of them needed a change. Numbers: 5 new tests (2 headless 60 line runs of the composer in a 600 and a 360 high window, 1 of the task box, 2 pure helpers); the composer tests fail with the cap removed and the task box test fails on the old code. Checked in a screenshot run with a 45 line text: box capped, header moved up, Send and Start run above the status bar. Not checked: typing by hand in a live window.
- **The progress ring is one even band again (branch `fix/progress-ring`).** The owner: the arc on an Overview card showed as a short, thick crescent on the track. Root cause: egui 0.35 strokes `circle_stroke` outside its radius while `Shape::line` strokes the middle, so the track sat 2.7 px outside the arc, and the 40 px tile (corner radius 11, reaching 23.7 px at its diagonals) was painted over the arc's inner half. The track is now pulled in by half the stroke so both share one band, the ring is painted 56 px across (layout slot still 52) with a stroke of a fourteenth of that (about 3.9 px) so its inner edge, 24.1 px, clears the tile's corners. Tests: the vertices of the tessellated track and arc sit in the same band (fails on the old track), the arc is an open clockwise sweep of the fraction from the top at 0.1, 0.33, 0.5 and 0.9, and the ring clears the tile corners. The Overview card is the only place a ring is drawn now. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND=1` and `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and a ledger with one question and 2 of 11 items done, before and after crops zoomed 6x.
- **The Reader draws your messages as formatted text and hides Claude Code's internal records (branch `fix/user-bubble`).** The owner's report: the user bubble right-aligned every line, a pasted message showed `<pasted_content id=...>` and `[Image #1]` as raw text, a numbered list collapsed into one paragraph, and `/rate-limit-options` and its `<local-command-stdout>` showed as raw red cards. Also a message sent while the agent works appeared only in the terminal. Root causes: the bubble drew a plain label in a right-aligned layout; the parser kept Claude's wrapper tags and turned every `<command-name>` or `<local-command-stdout>` record into a red notice; a `queued_command` attachment line was never read. Now `user_markup.rs` (pure) sorts each Claude user text (a slash command as a muted `/name args` line, local output as a muted note with tags and terminal colors removed, empty output and caveats hidden, `<pasted_content>` pairs unwrapped, a tag mentioned in prose left alone), the bubble renders the text through the agent bubble's markdown (lists with hanging indent, line breaks kept, inline code, `[Image #1]` as a small `Image 1` chip) left-aligned and as wide as its text, and a human `queued_command` prompt is a user message at its own time, which ends the agent's turn there and starts another, and the summary Claude writes after a compaction (a giant user bubble before) is one quiet `Conversation compacted` divider that neither splits the turn nor opens a user turn. Numbers: 3,376 tests pass across the workspace (new: 6 `user_markup` tables, 4 transcript tables over three hand-written fixtures, two turn tests in `reader_turns`, the bubble width). Checked: a screenshot run at 1280x1400 in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and a hand-written transcript (command, local output, an empty output, a pasted numbered list with an image marker, a queued message mid-turn, a compaction summary). Not done: the tab title still takes the first user text raw, so a message that starts with an image marker titles the tab `[Image #1]`; `<task-notification>`, `<bash-input>` and `<bash-stdout>` records still show as the user's words.

## 2026-10-24

- **The terminal behaves like stdusk's (branch `fix/stdusk-terminal-parity`, stdusk PR #34).** The owner: "I no longer want to fix things that work perfectly in stdusk for tidewatch if they work there they should work here, period!" (Cmd+click on links worked in stdusk, never here). Root cause: stdusk's app computed `link_active` and the grid options from its `[terminal]` config; Tidewatch hand-built a `GridStyle` with `link_active: false` and its own `TerminalOptions`. stdusk now owns that policy (`stdusk_core::config::TerminalConfig`, `GridStyle::from_config`, `TerminalOptions::from_config`, `SpawnOpts::from_config`, middle and right click paste, the bell and its flash, the multiline paste question), and Tidewatch feeds it stdusk's default config with its own `copy_on_select` pref. Gained here: links (underline and open under the pointer, default modifier `none` as in stdusk, so plain hover and click; Cmd+click works too), cursor blink, ligatures off as default, middle-click paste, right click (stdusk's default `menu` opens nothing here: no pane menu), the bell flash over the terminal, and the multiline paste question (Paste / Cancel, Enter / Esc; a modal for the keys). Removed: `PASTE_RULES`, `MIN_CONTRAST`, `view_options`, the spawn constants in `pty.rs`. Kept overrides: `copy_on_select` (the pref, default on here), `Focus::Manage` and the `Gate`. Pinned to stdusk PR #34's head. Checked: headless frames through the real `show_shell` (hand cursor over a link under Cmd and under the default modifier, none over plain text, the settings honored, multiline paste held for the question), 3,230 tests, clippy `-D warnings`, a `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` smoke run in a throwaway home. **Not verified:** a real Cmd+click opening a browser (tests never click a link), real middle or right click paste (needs the system clipboard), the bell flash and the paste question window on screen, Linux. The pin moves to a merge commit once stdusk #34 merges.

- **Amphetamine asks for Touch ID, and flips at once (branch `feat/amphetamine-touch-id`, draft PR).** The owner approved the plan for 0.1.21: the admin prompt only took a password, and the button waited on `pmset`. One-time setup (Settings, General, Keep awake, Touch ID for Amphetamine, Set up) installs through one password prompt a root-owned helper at `/Library/PrivilegedHelperTools/tidewatch-amphetamine` (verbs `start <token> <pid>`, `stop`, `uninstall`; it checks the token path ends in `.state/amphetamine.token` with no `..` or shell characters and the pid is digits) and a sudoers drop-in `/etc/sudoers.d/tidewatch-amphetamine` for that account by name and that one path, checked with `visudo -cf` on a temp copy before anything moves and renamed into place from a name sudo ignores. Then the button shows the system Touch ID prompt (LocalAuthentication, device owner policy, so the login password is its own fallback) and runs `sudo -n` on the helper; a cancel stops the start, anything else (no helper, a sudo rule that no longer works, no prompt on this Mac) falls back to the old osascript password path. The state machine is on as soon as the start returns and checks `pmset` in the background (an unconfirmed start flips back with a toast); off is off as soon as the stop returns (the helper's `stop` when installed), with the check after and a StillOff toast if it fails. Jobs carry an epoch so a check from an earlier session is dropped. Decision of mine: the helper lives in `/Library/PrivilegedHelperTools`, not `/usr/local/libexec`, because `/usr/local` is often user-owned (Homebrew on Intel) and a user-owned parent lets the user swap a root-owned file; the install command also refuses a folder that is not owned by root. New dev values `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_AMPHETAMINE=touchid|start`, and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SCROLL_KEEP_AWAKE=1`.
  Numbers: 42 new tests, 3,247 passing across the workspace. Checked: the helper's real validator run by `sh` over a table of hostile token paths and pids, the helper run with a fake `pmset` (start, hold, restore on token removal, refusals reach no `pmset`, stop, uninstall into a temp dir), the sudoers text through `visudo -cf` on a temp file (and a bad one refused), the install command parsed by `sh -n`, the host through a fake whose `pmset` is gated (on and off flip before the check), screenshots of the Settings row in both states and of the strip after a fake `start`. Not checked: the real setup, the real Touch ID prompt, real `sudo`, the helper under root, `use_pty` behavior of sudo 1.9.17 with the detached watcher, a real lid closed.

- **Commit search in History (branch `feat/changes-and-search`, kelp PR #29).** A field above the graph (magnifier, teal border while it holds the keyboard, `3 of 214` style count at the right in a fixed-width slot) with the This branch / Whole repo switch beside it, which moved down from the card header. It uses kelp-core's `search::matching_rows` (message, author and email, or an id prefix of 4 or more hex digits) on a job thread after a 180 ms pause, answers to older text are dropped, and a history reload asks again.
