Matching rows stay at full strength through `GraphInput::lit`, the others fade to 35%, and the matched text in the title is marked with the new `GraphView::highlight` (kelp-ui, teal at 25%; Kelp itself never sets it, so its graph is unchanged). The mark is only drawn in the title: a hit on the author or the id lights the row but there is nothing to mark, because the graph shows neither. Enter and Shift+Enter select the next and previous match from the selected row and wrap, the count reads the selected row's place among the matches (`N matches` when it is not one), Esc clears (read in `app/frame.rs`, because egui drops the field's focus before any frame code runs), Cmd+F (Ctrl+Shift+F on Linux) focuses the field with the old text selected and is only taken while the History side shows, so it is a table entry (`find-commit`), in the palette and on the Shortcuts page. From the palette it also switches the card to History. Checked: `docs/screenshots/history-search.png` (the query `fix` on a throwaway repo: 7 of 30 commits match), Cmd+F, Esc and the Changes side by `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PRESS=find-commit@3` and `back-to-terminal@5` (new `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PANEL` and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SEARCH` switches). Tests: stepping and wrapping, the count text, the pause and stale answers, searching a real repo by message, author and id, Enter and Shift+Enter in the field, the field and the count keeping their size, the gate for the chord. Not checked: Linux, a repo with thousands of commits (the search is one job per pause, not cancelled), typing with a real keyboard.

- **Changes tab (branch `feat/changes-and-search`).** The owner: "there is no way to inspect uncommitted files", and the git UI stays view only: "we don't need manual commits agents should handle everything we only view files." The right card's switch is Progress | Changes | History (Progress only with a ledger; Changes carries a count pill in a fixed-width slot, hidden at 0). Changes lists Staged, Changed and New files (`tidewatch_core::changes::Groups`, from kelp-core's `WorkingStatus`; a conflicted file counts as changed, a file both staged and edited again sits in both groups and counts once), each row a status letter (M orange, A green, D red, ? blue), the name, the dimmed folder and +/- counts, 30 px rows and a 36 px footer that do not move as files come and go (`5 files changed since the last commit` and the totals when every file was counted, up to 60). A click opens the file's diff in the terminal's place (`open_file`; `select_file` in the Below layout). The watcher keeps it live. The card's choice is now remembered for every tab: `forget_except` only knew agent tabs, so a shell tab lost it each frame. The working tree's file list follows the Changes order so Up and Down walk what is listed. Checked: `docs/screenshots/changes-tab.png` (throwaway home and a throwaway `acme-web` repo with staged, changed and new files, one of them both staged and edited); a clean repo against it: the switch box and its labels sit at the same x and only the pill differs (174 changed pixels in the 1100x110 header crop). Tests: grouping and counts on a real repo, row and footer positions with 1 and 6 files, click opens the diff in both layouts, no stage or commit text, the pill slot keeps the switch width. `cargo test --workspace` 3269 passed. Not checked: Linux, a repo with more than 60 changed files (totals are left out), a window narrower than the card.

- **Background fetch (branch `feat/autofetch`, Kelp PR #28).** Tidewatch only fetched once, when a tab started, so ahead and behind counts went stale in a long-lived window. Now each open repo group is fetched in the background on an interval with Kelp's `Op::Fetch`, timed by `kelp_ui::autofetch::AutoFetch` (shared with Kelp). Settings, General, Git (after Window): "Fetch in the background" (default on) and "Every" 1, 2, 5, 10, 15 or 30 minutes (default 5), saved as `[git] background_fetch` and `fetch_minutes` (a hand-edited value is held to the nearest choice when read). One fetch per repo group, not per tab, so worktrees of a repo share it; a repo is never fetched while its last fetch runs, and a repo seen for the first time waits one interval. A success is silent and reloads History for that repo's tabs; a failure shows a quiet `Fetch failed` note in the status bar (the reason on hover) and the next interval tries again. Not live in a screenshot run or a background run unless `TIDEWATCH_DEV_AUTOFETCH=1`. The kelp pin is the PR #28 commit until it merges. Checked: unit tests (the schedule, the prefs round trip and the clamp, one fetch per repo group, the General card's clicks) and a real fetch against a throwaway bare remote (the remote branch moves, HEAD and the files stay, a moved remote leaves the note and the next interval recovers); `docs/screenshots/autofetch-settings.png` (a throwaway home and repo).

- **A crashed Tidewatch resumes its agents, and a tab with no running agent says so (branch `fix/crash-resume`).** The owner: "I would expect this things to happen automatically for me... it's very confusing" (after a crash a tab read Needs you with the old conversation, no agent ran, and its terminal showed a resume command cut off at an unclosed quote). Root causes: the Claude `--settings` JSON (hook tokens included) was on the command line, so the typed line passed macOS's 1024 byte canonical limit (MAX_CANON) and was cut, and the token was on screen; a crash was always typed, whatever Resume agents said; and nothing marked a tab whose agent was not running. Now `prepare` writes the settings to `claude-settings.json` in the tab's state folder (mode 0600) and the line carries `--settings <path>`; a line over 900 bytes is never typed (the Resume button runs it); one crash resumes as the setting says, and only a second crash within 5 minutes of a launch (`.state/running` now holds the pid and start time) or an agent that crashed on its own is typed; an agent tab whose agent waits at a shell prompt reads Stopped (grey dot in the strip, Overview and header) and its Reader, or raw terminal, shows an Agent is not running bar with Resume. Checked: `docs/screenshots/stopped-agent-reader.png` (1280x800 points, throwaway home, a stand-in `claude`, a hand-written transcript, resume mode Type only); background runs with a seeded marker: a crash 2 hours after its start resumed the agent (the stand-in ran with `--resume` and `--settings <path>`, no token in its arguments, the file mode 0600), a crash 1 minute after its start left the tab Stopped. Not verified: a real Claude Code reading the settings file (only `--help` says it takes a path or JSON), a real Codex tab (its hooks and house style stay inline, so its resume line is always too long to type and always runs through the button or auto), a Linux run, the Resume button against a real shell prompt (headless tests cover the click, the bytes sent are by construction), a Restart-to-update handoff of a Stopped tab (it comes back as an ordinary agent tab).

- **Settings sync (branch `feat/settings-sync`, stdusk PR #33).** Settings, General, Sync: a switch, a Repo field, Last synced with Sync now. It uses stdusk's sync engine (pinned to the PR's commit until it merges) with `Op::Reconcile`: commit local edits, fetch, merge, push; on a conflict this Mac's version wins and a quiet note says so. Only `settings.toml`, `profiles.toml` and `workflows.toml` travel (an ignore list of `*` and three exceptions); `trust.toml`, `.state` and the rest stay. The switch, the repo and the time live in `sync.toml`, which is not synced. `Autosync` is pure: a sync at launch, then 5 s after the files stop changing, a retry after 60 s. A missing repo offers Create private repo (`gh repo create --private`) and runs only on that click. A pull applies at once: the app reads `settings.toml`, `profiles.toml` and `workflows.toml` again, re-applies the window prefs (zoom, height, drop-down, hotkey, menu bar), the restore, update, new-tab, park, done-check, keep-awake, guard, GitHub and terminal font settings, reloads the catalog and re-probes changed agents, and refreshes the Settings editors that have no unsaved edits; the card says `Settings updated from <repo>`. A background watcher thread reads the three files every 2 s only while sync is on and asks for a frame only when they changed, so nothing wakes the idle app. Real git against a throwaway bare repo tests the files, the ignore list, the conflict and the missing repo; no GitHub repo was created or pushed to. Open: a live sync against GitHub. Running tabs keep the profile they launched with; the restore switches apply at the next launch, as they always did.

- **Interface size (branch `feat/ux-pass`, second commit).** The owner approved a setting: Settings, General, Window, Interface size, 80 / 90 / 100 / 110 / 125 / 150 percent, saved as `[window] ui_zoom` (held to 0.8 to 1.5 on load, NaN or a non-number is 1.0) and applied with `ctx.set_zoom_factor` at start and on change. egui's keyboard zoom is turned off (it was on, unsaved, and its Cmd+0 reset clashed with the Overview); Tidewatch's own commands step through the same values and save them: Cmd+= (and Cmd+Plus) bigger, Cmd+- smaller, Cmd+Shift+0 back to 100%, Ctrl+Shift on Linux, in the command table, the palette and the Shortcuts page. The drop-down already unzooms its viewport commands; its test now covers 0.8 and 1.5. Screenshots: `docs/screenshots/ux-interface-size-100.png`, `ux-agent-tab-80.png`, `ux-agent-tab-125.png`.

- **UX pass (branch `feat/ux-pass`, stdusk PR #32).** The owner approved the design board's "after" artboards. Strip: 64 px tall, 44 px tabs and icon boxes, Overview, main, new tab, Amphetamine and gear at 24 px (22 for plus and gear), 14 px titles, a 26 px avatar tile, the active tab a teal-nav fill with a 2 px teal underline, and an 8 px status dot at the right end of a tab instead of the colored bar along its top (the title is the proportional UI font, semibold when active, taking all the room up to the dot; the ledger ring and the job bar left the tab, their words are in its tooltip) (so tab tops no longer merge into a ruler line; the OSC 9;4 percent bar is gone with it). Amphetamine keeps its art and animation, only `GLYPH_PX` went 18 -> 28 (the slot 44 px, the pill 32 px tall). Agent tab: status line, content and status bar share `layout::EDGE` (24 px), status bar text 13 px. Overview: a 40 px avatar tile instead of the always-there ring (the ring only for a ledger with a fraction above 0), the repo named once in the header, a counts row (need you, working, idle). Settings: content column centered at 820 px, 14 px semibold SOFT section labels, 248 px sidebar with 44 px rows and 20 px icons. Text sizes in the touched views collapsed to 13, 14 to 15 and 16. Skipped: the finished-job line (`job_view.rs` belongs to the progress work). Screenshots in `docs/screenshots/ux-*.png`.

- **Progress shows only what is true (branch `fix/progress-honesty`).** The owner: "I would rather not have progress than have a shitty one" (a huge `0` with `ETA estimating` forever, and a Meeseeks ledger whose Understand bar stayed empty). A job with no total is one quiet line (name and elapsed time) and disappears after 5 quiet minutes; one with only an `eta_s` is a time bar with `about 13m left`; a card has a 15 px name, a mono whole percent (never 0, `<1%` under one, never 100 early), a 6 px bar and one detail line (`12,431 of 58,650 rows · about 1h 51m left`); the sparkline, RATE, DONE AT and `estimating` are gone. ETA needs 2 real samples with a positive rate (a name-only report is no sample) and `eta_left` returns none at zero instead of `0s`. Lines read off the terminal only update a job the agent or a script already reported, never start one. A finished job is one line (green check, name, result, duration) and leaves after 10 minutes; a stuck job is a compact orange row `<name> stuck at 97% for 6m` with Ask agent. Ledger phase bars count an item under way as half an item, so it fills its own phase; the Meeseeks card drops `turn N of 40` and shows its four stage bars only once a check has begun. The ring is never drawn at 0 (tab, Overview card), and the panel header has no ring or `0/6`: it says `Step 1 of 4 · Understand` instead. Screenshots: `docs/screenshots/progress-jobs.png`, `docs/screenshots/progress-meeseeks.png` (`TIDEWATCH_FAKE_JOBS=1` and `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_MEESEEKS=on` in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude`). **Not verified:** a live agent reporting real jobs, Linux, the half-item credit against a real Meeseeks ledger (the owner's exact off-by-one was not reproduced; the current-phase lookup was correct in every case tried). `agent_jobs::a_probe_reports_the_version_or_that_the_cli_is_missing` failed once under the full parallel run and passed alone.
- **A tab with no avatar is asked to draw one (branch `avatar-ask`).** The owner on 0.1.19: "I still see no avatars". Root causes: the owner's agent tabs were started by 0.1.18, whose `mcp.json` had no `alwaysLoad`, and they survived the upgrade through the restart handoff, so Claude Code 2.1.310 still hid `set_avatar` behind ToolSearch; and a resumed or taken-over session that understood its task long ago never calls `set_avatar`, because the only request is "once you understand the task" in the house style. Now: a Claude tab with no avatar (never a Meeseeks, Copilot, Codex, Gemini or a custom CLI) is asked once on its next prompt, and the ask never touches the input box: the listener answers the `UserPromptSubmit` http hook with `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` ("Also call set_avatar once with an icon for this task.") and writes the `avatar-asked` marker beside `avatar.json` first, so restarts keep it from repeating; a Meeseeks and a read-only host (screenshot runs, fake avatars) are exempt. A letter tile's right-click menu offers Draw avatar, which pastes the ask through the idle-only send path (a click, so pasting is fine) and leaves the marker. `prepare` and `prepare_resume` already rewrite `mcp.json` on every launch; a test pins that a resume over an old file without `alwaysLoad` gets it. The rule is pure (`avatar_ask::due`, table-tested). Checked against the installed Claude Code 2.1.310 (see the PR): its hook output schema takes `additionalContext` for `UserPromptSubmit`, and a headless run with a stand-in http hook and a stand-in Messages server showed the context in the model request. **Not verified:** a signed-in model then calling the tool, the Draw avatar menu item on screen (popups do not render in the screenshot harness), the full app against a real Claude. A tab started by 0.1.18 keeps its old `mcp.json` until it is relaunched, so the model may have to find `set_avatar` through ToolSearch.
- **The quake height is configurable again (branch `feat/quake-height`).** The owner: "the height to be configure-able again like stdusk". `[window] height_pct` in settings.toml (default 0.5, 0.2 to 0.9, pulled into the range on load, NaN is the default; 0.505 survives a save and load as `0.505`) and a Height row in Settings, General, Window (a 240 px teal slider and its value in mono, right-aligned in a fixed width so it does not jump; shown while the drop-down is on). It is applied when the slider is let go (or a key nudges it): a shown quake window resizes at once, a hidden one takes it on its next drop (`quake::resize`, `quake::present` takes the fraction). Checked: unit tests (size table, resize on the window's own screen, shown, hidden, plain window and background run, prefs clamp and round trip, editor settle, slider release over headless frames) and `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` with `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SETTINGS=general`: `docs/screenshots/quake-height.png` (the whole Window section). **Not verified:** a real drop and a live resize on a real screen (never live-tested from an agent run), dragging the slider by hand, Linux.
- **The Reader is a chat (branch `feat/reader-chat`).** The owner: "feel like a chat", "all the bash commands and stuff can be under just one thing, I don't want one line entry every time a shell executes, it's noise for me as a user", "The text is very tiny compared to the shell, I want better readability"; picked a combo of boards A and C. `reader_turns` (pure, table-tested) groups the rows into turns and counts the chips per kind; `reader_chat_view` draws the right-hand user bubbles, the left agent bubble with the tab's face, the chip row, the open list and the live pill; `reader_view` keeps the column, the cards, the permission and question docks, and the reply box (16 px, primary Send). A turn's list opens and folds on a chip click (state per turn in `ViewState`). Heights: a turn is keyed by its first row's `seq`, hidden rows take no height, the estimate follows the 16 px text. Per frame: the grouping runs again only when `Rows::revision` moves; the visible rows are the only ones drawn. `TIDEWATCH_DEV_TURNS=open` opens every list for captures.
  Checked: `docs/reader-chat-*.png` (1440x900 points, throwaway home and repo, a stand-in `claude` with a hand-written transcript and hooks): a finished turn collapsed and expanded, a turn in progress, a permission prompt and a question card. Headless tests drive the chip click open and closed. Not checked: Codex, Gemini and Copilot transcripts on screen (the kinds come from their tool names, table-tested), a very long session, Linux.
- **A finished agent reads Done, not Needs you (branch `fix/idle-not-needs-you`).** The owner: "I also don't understand there's these orange dots there all the time" (four resumed or taken-over Claude sessions sat as Needs you for hours). Root cause, from the status fold: a resumed session has no hook state until its next prompt, so `from_signals` fell to `NeedsYou(Quiet)` after 8 s of silence and stayed there; and Claude's `idle_prompt` notification, the only hook such a session sends, was read as `NeedsYou(Input)` (the old guard only absorbed it after a reported `Stop`). Now `idle_prompt` reads Done (it settles a turn still Working, as after an interrupt, and never overrides a finished, failed or prompting state), and `StatusTracker::agent_resumed` starts a resumed agent as Done (`Started.resumed`, set by the restore and take-over path). Permission prompts, `AskUserQuestion` and elicitations still read Needs you. The repo chip lost its needs-you dot; the Overview badge is the one count and its tooltip names the repos. The repo chip is now a fixed-width folder chip (180 px, name cut by an ellipsis, no fill until hover), `docs/repo-chip-folder.png`. Tests: table tests over the real payload shapes and idle sequences, a resumed-agent walk, the chip width for a 3 and a 41 letter name, the badge tooltip. Not run live: a real Claude needs a login the throwaway HOME does not have.

- **Shortcuts while the terminal has focus (branch `fix/terminal-shortcuts`).** The owner: "I still don't understand how to use shortcuts (to move and stuff) while I'm in the terminal", then "ALL of these" (line editing, between tabs, moving a tab, discoverability), and "I cannot click" Claude Code's "Jump to bottom (click)". Checked with headless frames that rebuild the real order (the command table consumes first, then stdusk's `TerminalView`) and by reading egui-winit 0.35 and winit 0.30 (macOS only folds Cmd+C, X and V into events; Cmd+Left, Option+Left and the Backspace variants arrive as key events, and the IME path is only on while a text field is focused). On macOS every table chord reached its command and sent nothing to the shell, and Option+Left, Option+Right, Cmd+Left, Cmd+Right, Option+Backspace and Cmd+Backspace reached the shell as ESC b, ESC f, ^A, ^E, ESC DEL and ^U, so nothing there needed a fix; the missing piece was discoverability. Fixed: an Alt chord (Linux tabs, repos, Overview) also typed its key into the shell because egui-winit only drops the text of a press while Ctrl or Cmd is down; the table now drops it. Added: Keyboard shortcuts in the palette and on Cmd+/ (Ctrl+Shift+/ on Linux, matched as a question mark too) open Settings on Shortcuts, and the page and `docs/shortcuts.md` gain an In the terminal section, checked against the real bytes by a test. Clicks: v0.1.19 only forwarded the wheel to a program with mouse reporting (`terminal_wheel.rs`), never clicks; #112's `TerminalView` sends SGR press and release for modes 1000, 1002 and 1003 with 1006, after a wheel scroll and with the press and release in one frame, pinned by new headless tests. Not checked: a physical click in a foreground window, Linux Ctrl+Left and Ctrl+Backspace (they send nothing; left alone).
- **Copy on select in the terminal (branch `fix/copy-on-select`).** The owner: "copy on selet doesnt work". The behavior already lives in stdusk's `TerminalView` (`TerminalOptions::copy_on_select`, with its own test) and Tidewatch passed `false`. Now `[terminal] copy_on_select` in `settings.toml` (default on here, off in stdusk) feeds `view_options`, with a Copy on select switch in Settings, General, Terminal (applies at once). A finished drag, a double click and a triple click copy the selection and toast "Copied". No stdusk change and no rev bump. Cmd+C checked again on main: the headless frames over a real pty (`Event::Copy` with Cmd) put the selection on the clipboard. Not verified: a real mouse selection or Cmd+C by hand in a foreground window, Linux.
- **v0.1.19.** Ships #108 (tab titles from the work, avatars with MCP `alwaysLoad`), #109 (YOLO pickable as the new tab default, with the reason a level is refused), #110 (take-over groups worktrees of one repo, saved groups merged on launch), #111 (toolchain detection, bun or deno by lockfile) and #113 (Running elsewhere hides moved sessions, ended ones under a collapsed Recent sessions bar). #112 (use-terminal-view) is not in this release. Main at 64e60a9 was green before the bump: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` (3,089 passing). Every site shot shows agent tab labels, which are now work titles, so all six were retaken from a throwaway acme-web repo, home and stand-in `claude` (2200x1375, `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_AMPHETAMINE=off`, background runs, neutral prompt) with titled stand-in transcripts (`ai-title` records such as "Add retry to the orders backfill"): hero 186 kB to 227 kB (PSNR 44.1 dB), overview 94 to 118 (46.6), needs 132 to 160 (45.1), newtab 142 to 169 (46.3), palette 300 to 296 (46.7), workflows 133 to 160 (46.8). The strip shows four agent tabs and a Parked pill of two, so titles fit as far as the tab width allows. The New pill names only these merged features.
- **Running elsewhere stops listing sessions that are not running (branch `elsewhere-recent`).** The owner: "why do we see these here? the non running ones?" (five `claude · ended 33 min ago` rows). Root cause: the card also listed ended sessions, up to three per folder, as "recent" rows; a session was left out only when its id was in the held set (live registry ids, running processes, the `launched.session_id` of open tabs). A tab that is closed, or whose transcript is another file than the id it launched with, is not held, so what the owner moved in earlier came back as a "not running" row. Now: a session is left out when its id or its transcript file (the open tab's Reader file, symlinks resolved) is held, and Tidewatch remembers every id and transcript an open tab held for the rest of the run, so a closed tab's session stays out too; ended sessions are no longer rows of the card but sit under a collapsed "Recent sessions (N)" bar, the newest one per folder across the CLIs; a card with nothing running is not drawn; a row is named by the session's transcript title (custom, generated, summary, as for tab titles) and says when it ended. Numbers: 3,089 tests passing across the workspace (new: held by id, by transcript, through a symlink, one per folder, newest across CLIs, titles, running apart from ended, no card with only ended, the bar's height and clicks, a scan with held ids and files). Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_RECENT=open` in a throwaway home with a `sleep` stand-in and hand-written transcripts: `docs/elsewhere-recent.png`. **Not verified:** that `claude --resume <id>` keeps the id in an interactive tab on 2.1.310 (`--help` says it reuses it unless `--fork-session`; a throwaway home has no login), a real session closed in a tab, Linux. A title is cached per transcript path by (size, modification time), so the 5 s scan reads a transcript again only after it changed (files over 64 MB are never read); a changed transcript is still read in full, because a title record can be anywhere in it.

- **The terminal is stdusk's `TerminalView` (branch `use-terminal-view`).** The owner: "when are we moving over to shared code from stdusk to stop repeat work / fixing?" Phase 1 of the parity audit: stdusk-egui now has one view for a pane's input layer (stdusk PRs 30 and 31, merged as e354c6ba and d3ecf6e5) and `app/terminal.rs` makes one call to it. Deleted from Tidewatch: `terminal_wheel.rs`, `terminal_focus.rs`, `paste.rs`, the Cmd+V monitor and `post_cmd_v` in `macos.rs` and `type_into`; the moved tests (wheel route and arrows tables, Tab/Shift+Tab/arrows/Esc focus frames, the paste table, bracketed paste) run in stdusk against the view, and the Tidewatch ones that test our wiring stay here (typing jumps to the bottom, the paste table with our rules, Tab and Esc through the view, the gate). New for free: Cmd+C copies the selection (toast "Copied"; Ctrl+C copies and clears a selection, else it is ^C, like stdusk), click, drag and motion reports to apps with mouse reporting on, drag autoscroll past the pane edge, Alt+wheel as arrows, typing clears the selection. Checked: headless frames over a real pty (copy puts "hello world" on the clipboard, Ctrl+C copies with a selection and is ^C without, Tab and Esc), and a `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND` run in a throwaway home with the new `TIDEWATCH_DEV_COPY=<seconds>` (select all, Cmd+C): `pbpaste` held the shell's text, the clipboard was restored after. Not checked: a real Cmd+C or click by hand, Cmd+V in a running window, Linux.

- **v0.1.18.** Ships #66 (terminal font picker), #69 (Linux tab strip as the title bar), #99 (keep awake while agents work), #100 (stdusk move grouping), #101 (Tab in the terminal), #102 (screenshot paste), #103 (terminal scroll), #104 (Overview first in the strip, main as an icon) #105 (Amphetamine button) and #107 (Amphetamine toasts in a Lovecraft voice). Every site shot shows the strip, so all six were retaken from a throwaway acme-web repo, home and stand-in `claude` (2200x1375, `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_AMPHETAMINE=off`, no real activation): hero 185 kB to 186 kB (PSNR 44.6 dB), overview 91 to 94 (46.9), needs 132 to 132 (46.1), newtab 170 to 142 (46.8), palette 301 to 300 (47.3), workflows 129 to 133 (47.3). The New pill names only these merged features.
- **Agent tabs are named by their work, and agents draw avatars again (branch `tab-titles`).** The owner: "the tabs show agent names?! I need to know what work is being done" and "no avatars are being generated?". Titles: `tab_title::pick` takes the session title Claude wrote to its transcript (`custom-title` from /rename, then `ai-title`, then `summary`), else Claude's terminal title, the ledger goal, the first message, and only last the worktree name, which moves to the tooltip; the strip, Overview cards, Parked list, palette and notifications all use it, and a moved or resumed session has it from its first read because the Reader tails the transcript from the top. Avatars, root cause: Claude Code 2.1.310 hides every MCP tool behind its ToolSearch tool unless the server sets `alwaysLoad` (checked on the real binary: the request's tool list had none of the Tidewatch tools, and all six with `alwaysLoad`), so `set_avatar` was never in front of the model; the Claude MCP config now sets it. Checked with a stand-in `claude` that resumed four sessions from a seeded session and called `tools/list` and `set_avatar` through the real listener (alwaysLoad true, tool listed, avatar landed on its tab). Not checked: a signed-in model actually calling the tool (no credentials in a throwaway home), a real generated `ai-title` line (shape taken from the 2.1.310 binary; `custom-title` was captured from a real `/rename`), Codex, Gemini and Copilot titles (their transcripts hold none), a `latest` session id (no transcript, so the label falls to the terminal title or the name).


- **Taken-over sessions group under their main repo (branch `takeover-groups`).** The owner: "I have repo_TREE and repo and repo_TREE_2 and Tidewatch didn't move them to the one master." Root cause: take-over opened the landed tab's workspace with `Workspace::open` (the folder's own `--show-toplevel`), so a linked worktree became its own tab group; only stdusk's Move tabs had #100's `open_in_main_repo`. A move now uses `open_in_main_repo`, and separate clones of one `origin` (ssh, https and `.git` spellings normalized) join the open repo of that remote, else gather under the clone that is not a `_TREE` folder, else the first (`takeover_group.rs`, pure). The tab keeps its own folder as its cwd (`Launched.dir`, saved for a tab with no worktree: it used to be saved as the group folder, which would have resumed `repo_TREE`'s session in `repo` after a restart); a taken-over tab has no Tidewatch worktree, so closing it removes nothing. Checked with throwaway repos and stand-in claude processes in a throwaway home: before, `session.toml` had two windows (`acme-web`, `acme-web_TREE`); after, one window with the agent tab in `acme-web_TREE`; a clone with the same remote joins the open repo, one with another remote stays apart. A session saved before this had one tab group per worktree folder: a launch now opens every remembered folder as its main repo and folds the saved windows of one repo into one (`Session::merge_linked`, run once in `main.rs` after the single-instance claim and written back; its tabs keep their own folders, order and park marks, the group is named after the main repo, the emptied ones go), and a repo added at runtime does the same. Checked with a seeded session.toml of four groups of one repo's worktrees in a throwaway TIDEWATCH_HOME: one group after launch, each agent tab still in its own folder. A repo you add yourself with only its main shell still shows as a group with 0 tabs. stdusk does not create `_TREE` folders (nothing in ../stdusk names them), so the clone case is a guess at how the owner's folders were made.

- **Amphetamine speaks Lovecraft (branch `amphetamine-voice`).** The toast lines of the Amphetamine button leave the Disco Elysium skills for public-domain cosmic horror, in the same four moments and structure: start (THE STARS ARE RIGHT, SHOGGOTH), each hour (NON-EUCLIDEAN HOURS), end (ELDER SIGN, R'LYEH SLEEPS). `Skill` is now `Omen`; the tests, the `project.md` line and `docs/screenshots/amphetamine-voice.png` follow. No new modules.

- **Wheel and trackpad scroll in the terminal (branch `terminal-scroll`).** The owner: "I also cannot scroll". Root cause: stdusk routes the wheel in its own app code (`src/workspace.rs`: scrollback, arrow keys on the alternate screen, SGR wheel reports under mouse reporting), not in `stdusk-egui`, so moving to Tidewatch left the grid with nothing that read the wheel. New `terminal_wheel` ports that routing and reads the raw wheel events (points, lines or pages) with a carry, so a slow trackpad swipe adds up; `app/terminal.rs` also jumps back to the bottom when you type or paste, as stdusk does. Not the Tab focus lock: the grid keeps the focus through every wheel frame in the tests. Differences from stdusk: arrows follow the cursor-key mode (CSI normally, SS3 in application mode) where stdusk always sends SS3, and Alt+wheel as arrows is not ported. Checked: headless frames with the real panel, `collect_input`, `render_grid` and the focus lock over a real pty (shell scrollback by lines, points and pages, a pointer elsewhere, an alternate-screen app receiving `ESC [ A` and `ESC [ B`, SGR reports `ESC [ < 64 ; 38 ; 13 M` in the alternate and the normal screen), and a `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` run with `seq 1 500` and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SCROLL=30`. Not checked: a real trackpad or wheel, Claude Code's own TUI, Linux; the Reader, History, file view and Overview use plain egui scroll areas and were read, not driven.

- **Tab in the terminal goes to the shell (branch `terminal-tab-focus`).** The owner: "when I press Tab to autocomplete in the terminal, accessibility Tab kicks in and it starts focusing button elements". egui walks keyboard focus on Tab, Shift+Tab, arrows and Esc unless the focused widget filters them, and nothing held the focus while the shell had the keys, so the shell got `\t` and the buttons around it took focus too. `terminal_focus::hold` runs after the grid is drawn: when the shell has the keys (no modal, no focused text field, no open popup, no Reader) the grid takes the focus when nothing holds it and sets an `EventFilter` with tab, both arrow kinds and escape, so Tab reaches the pty as `\t`, Shift+Tab as `ESC [ Z`. It lets go when the keys are not free, so Tab in the new tab form, dialogs, Settings and text fields is egui's as before. `remember_focus` ignores the grid, otherwise an Esc meant for the shell would be used up as "a widget gave up the keyboard". stdusk has no such lock (its window has no buttons for Tab to reach), so the fix stays in this crate. Checked: headless frames with a text field, a button and the real `render_grid` over a real pty (7 tests; the three about Tab, Shift+Tab, arrows and Esc fail without the filter, the Esc one without the `remember_focus` change), and a throwaway frame harness typing `ech` and Tab into a real bash and zsh, which both completed to `echo`. Not checked: the window itself with a real keyboard, Linux.
- **Screenshots paste with Cmd+V (branch `image-paste`).** The owner: "I STILL CAN'T PASTE SCREENSHOTS". egui folds Cmd+V into a text-only `Event::Paste` and sends nothing for an image-only clipboard, so Cmd+V with a screenshot did nothing. Ported stdusk's NSEvent local key-down monitor (stdusk keeps it in its binary, not a shared crate, so there is no stdusk change and no rev bump). It looks at the pasteboard types only (cheap on the main thread); for a picture or copied image files it swallows the key and counts a request, and the window turns that into a job for the tab in front. In a terminal tab Claude, Codex, Gemini and Copilot get Ctrl+V (0x16: each reads the clipboard picture on it; checked in the installed codex 0.162.1, gemini 0.46.0 and copilot 1.0.103), a plain shell or a custom CLI gets the path of a PNG saved in `<tab state>/pastes/`. In the Reader's reply box, the agent-ready composer or any focused text field the saved path is inserted (the Reader's reply when no field has the keyboard). Image files copied in Finder paste their own paths (Finder also puts the icon and the file name on the clipboard, so those are ignored). A clipboard with text and a picture keeps the text paste. Numbers: 2,910 tests pass across the workspace (about 20 new: the decision table, `shot_name`, `path_text`, saving a real PNG and reading it back, headless frames for the composer and the reply box, a mixed clipboard pasting its text). Checked live in a `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND=1` run (throwaway home and repo, stand-in `claude` that logs raw bytes, owner's clipboard saved and restored byte for byte): a screenshot clipboard put `16` in the stand-in's input, the composer got the saved path (the PNG decodes to the clipboard's 1224x754), a plain shell got the path at its prompt, a Finder-copied `Screen Shot.png` in a folder with a space pasted `'/tmp/ip files/Screen Shot.png'` and copied nothing, text alone and text with a picture pasted the text. The Cmd+V in those runs was a real `NSEvent` key-down queued in the app's own queue (`TIDEWATCH_DEV_IMAGE_PASTE`), so it passes the monitor. Not verified: a physical Cmd+V on the keyboard in a foreground window (a background window gets no key events from `CGEventPostToPid`), a real Claude Code, Codex, Gemini or Copilot reading the clipboard on the 0x16, Cmd+Ctrl+Shift+4 itself (a PNG on the clipboard stands in), Linux (egui-winit swallows Ctrl+V there too and nothing hooks it yet, so an image-only Ctrl+V still does nothing).

- **Amphetamine (branch `amphetamine`).** The owner approved board Round 6 ("ok build it"): the `cocaine` function as a button at the right end of the strip. State machine in `tidewatch_core::amphetamine` (off, starting, on, stopping; timer end, Add 1 h, hourly toast), the root helper script and the one admin command in `amphetamine_lid`, the `System` seam with a recording `FakeSystem`, the tablet in `amphetamine_motion` (pure motion on the 24 unit grid), `amphetamine_icon` (egui painter), `amphetamine_pill`, `amphetamine_view` (button, pill, menu, tooltip, toasts with omen tags, safety notice) and `amphetamine_glue` (host, files, launch check). Palette row Amphetamine, found by typing `cocaine` too; one line under the Keep awake card in Settings. See the Deviations log for the pmset facts and what is decided.
  Numbers: 85 new tests, 3,011 passing across the workspace (state machine table, helper script run with a fake `pmset` for deadline, token removed, pid gone, junk token, a later deadline and TERM, the admin command run with a fake `pmset`, quoting, launch recovery table, host flows through the fake system, motion timeline and seeded specks, label fit, headless frames: nothing to the right of the button moves, the pill never outgrows the gap, repaint delay `MAX` while off or unfocused and at most 33 ms while on, a click opens the menu); `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_AMPHETAMINE=off|on|forever|activating|short|notice|toast|hour|end` shows each state in a screenshot with no machine behind it (`docs/screenshots/amphetamine-*.png`).
  Not checked: the real password prompt and the real `pmset` (never run from a test or a dev run), the helper under root, a real lid closed on battery, Linux, a crash restore on a real machine, the macOS reduced-motion setting.
- **On Linux the tab strip is the title bar.** The owner, on Fedora GNOME Wayland: the top bar is "un-esthethic", make it "part of a continious UI". GNOME draws no server-side frame, so winit's grey Adwaita bar sat over the Night tide strip. Off macOS and outside quake (`Chrome::Drawn`, from `Env::own_frame` in the Plan) the system frame is off; the strip's empty space drags the window (`StartDrag`) and a double click does what GNOME's `action-double-click-titlebar` says; the window buttons sit after the gear in the strip's icon-button look (close turns red on hover, all dim while the window is in the background) and follow GNOME's `button-layout` (read once with `gsettings` before the window opens; Fedora's default `appmenu:close` shows Close alone; minimize, maximize and close without GNOME); 5 px edges with L-shaped corners resize (`BeginResize`) from a foreground layer, and a 1 px `SIDEBAR_LINE` outlines the window, neither while maximized. macOS keeps its native title bar. Numbers: 999 app tests pass (17 new: plan, zones, layouts, and headless frames that a click on a strip widget stays with it, a drag on empty strip moves the window, double click, each button, an edge press resizes and one inside does not). Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` (buttons, the update chip beside them, the owner's Close-only layout) and a `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND` run on GNOME Wayland that opened and quit cleanly. **Not verified live:** dragging, double click, resizing and the buttons on a real desktop (they need the pointer; for the owner). **Open:** no rounded corners or shadow (GNOME gives neither to a window without its frame; corners need a transparent window); KDE and other desktops get the default buttons. The workspace clippy gate stops at a new rust 1.97 lint in `tidewatch-core` (`duration_suboptimal_units`, `test_command.rs:20`), not touched here; the delivery tests fail inside an agent tab because its push guard blocks their `git push`.
- **Keep awake while agents work.** `tidewatch_core::keep_awake` is the pure state machine (take at once, release after 2 minutes with every agent non-working, switching off releases at once); `keep_awake_glue` runs it each frame over the working count of every tab of the window and takes or releases the hold in `keep_awake_hold` (macOS: an IOKit `PreventUserIdleSystemSleep` assertion named `Tidewatch: agents are working`; Linux: `systemd-inhibit` around a `cat` on a pipe). A small ring in the status bar, with a tooltip, shows while it is held; the Keep awake card of Settings, General switches it (`[keep_awake]`, on by default). Numbers: with a stand-in agent in a throwaway home, `pmset -g assertions` listed the assertion 0 ms after the agent turned Working, it was released 120.0 s after the agent turned NeedsYou(Quiet), and a `kill -9` of the window dropped it at once; a background run without `TIDEWATCH_DEV_KEEP_AWAKE=1` held nothing. A tab counts as work when it is `Working`, `NeedsYou(Quiet)` (a silent build or migration) or has a running, not stalled, job on its board (`keeps_awake`, table-tested); a real question, permission prompt, next-message wait, the bell, done or failed does not. Not checked: Linux, a real screen-off idle sleep, a long run with the lid closed.

## 2026-10-23

- **v0.1.17.** Ships #92 (keyboard-only new tab form), #93 (decision cards, Go with your picks), #94 (outbound guards), #95 (worktree setup recipe, merged-tab cleanup), #96 (done check) and #97 (comment guard). The site's new-tab shot was retaken for the form's `Enter type` hint (2200x1375, 140 kB to 170 kB, PSNR 45.5 dB, throwaway acme-web repo and home); the other site shots show nothing these PRs changed.
- **Comment guard (branch `comment-guard`).** The owner: "build 1 to 5 :P" (item 5 of the skill sweep). A Guards rule `comments` (on by default, per repo) with a classifier, a branch-diff sweep and the hooks described in PLAN.md's Deviations log; a chip `Comment guard: N flags` on the agent tab with the lines in a popover and an off switch per tab. Claude Code tabs get PostToolUse and Stop command hooks; the other CLIs are swept when their turn ends and told through the idle-only paste.
  Checked: table tests per syntax (Rust, TypeScript and JavaScript, Python, Ruby, shell, SQL), the diff sweep on a real temp branch, the hook output shape (`tests/guard_comments.rs` runs the real binary the way Claude Code runs a command hook), the host's sweep and paste, the strip headless, and a `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` run with `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_COMMENTS=1`. Not checked: a live Claude Code turn through the hooks (a throwaway home has no login), Codex, Gemini and Copilot, Linux.

- **Plain-English decision cards and Go with your picks (branch `decision-cards`).** From the owner's skill sweep (feature 2): agent questions in plain words with a marked top pick. The `ask_user` payload grows, backward compatible: a question may carry `decision` (one sentence, at most 200 characters) and each option `tradeoff` (one line, at most 160) and `recommended` (true on at most one option; a second one is a tool error to fix, and a trailing `(Recommended)` still counts, so old calls and Claude's own `AskUserQuestion` render as before). The tool description and the house style tell agents: plain words, no jargon, two or three options, mark your top pick, a delete names the exact items and the count. The card shows the decision sentence on top (the question under it in muted text), a muted tradeoff line per option, the Recommended pill, and the pick is preselected with the cursor on it, so Enter or Cmd+Enter takes it as it is. **Go with your picks** (button next to Skip, key `G` while the card has the keyboard) answers every waiting question row of the tab whose questions all have a pick, the rows keep their Answered summary cards, and a toast lists a few answers and names the rows left open (a row with a question that has no pick is left whole). New `question_bulk.rs` holds the pure plan and the toast text; `TIDEWATCH_DEV_QUESTION=all` drives the button.
  Guesses: the Overview lists agents that need you but not their questions, so there is no button there; a multiple-choice question preselects every recommended option; a Claude menu counts as answerable only while it is the newest row, as before.
  Numbers: 2,618 tests passing on `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`, up from 2,596 on this branch point (22 new: payload old and new shape, the one-pick rule, limits, `picks` and `all_picks`, the schema text, the card's preselection and cursor, headless G and Cmd+Enter, the headline, the bulk plan and its toast, the glue answering through held channels); `elsewhere_scan::a_child_the_test_started_is_ended_by_sigterm_through_the_real_system` fails inside a stdusk terminal and on main, as before.
  Checked: `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SHOT` runs (background, throwaway home) with a stand-in `claude` that calls `ask_user` twice through the real listener (`docs/decision-card.png`, `docs/decision-card-answered.png`). Not checked: a real agent writing the new fields, a click on the button by hand, Linux.
- **Worktree setup recipe and a merged-tab cleanup card (branch `worktree-setup`).** From the skill sweep (#1). Settings, Worktree setup: per repo, files copied from the main checkout (gitignored `.env*` files offered as one-click adds), folders linked to it (`node_modules`; a link git would not ignore goes into `info/exclude`, and an install command is refused as a generator while something is linked), the main checkout's `target` shared as `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` for a Rust repo, and a generator command run in the new worktree with a `Worktree setup` job in Progress. Missing sources are notes. The Merged card checks the worktree off the UI thread (uncommitted changes, untracked files, commits beyond the PR head, ignored files that are not build output or a copy of the main checkout's), offers Remove worktree and branch when nothing would be lost, and checks again at the click. See the Deviations log for where it differs from the plan.
  Numbers: 2,672 tests passing across the workspace (rebased on decision cards) (about 55 new: recipe validation, store, generator line run through `sh`, copy, link, missing source, no write through a link, the cleanup checks against real throwaway repos, the card states and buttons, the glue that checks and removes, the page and its editor); `elsewhere_scan::a_child_the_test_started_is_ended_by_sigterm_through_the_real_system` fails inside a Claude or stdusk terminal as before. fmt and clippy `-D warnings` clean.
  Checked: `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` runs in a throwaway home and repo with a stand-in `claude`: the page (docs/screenshots/worktree-setup.png) and a real tab where `.env` and `.env.local` were copied, `node_modules` linked, the generator ran and its job showed done in Progress, and the merged card (docs/screenshots/merged-cleanup.png) listed only the generated file. Not checked: Linux, a real agent, a real merged PR through the Remove button in the live app, a squash merge on GitHub (only a local simulation), the Generator card by eye.
- **Outbound text guards (branch `outbound-guards`).** The owner: "build 1 to 5 :P 4 can be a setting" (item 3 of the skill sweep). A `git` and a `gh` shim in `guard-bin` ask `tidewatch --guard` about `git commit` and `gh pr|issue|run`; five rules, each a switch with a reason the agent sees (no em or en dashes, no Co-Authored-By or Generated with lines, no private repo names in a public repo, PRs as drafts (off by default), rerun only failed jobs). Settings, General, Guards: a switch and the reason per rule, the private repo list, the push rule read-only, and an All repos / this repo scope. Design and gaps are in PLAN.md's Deviations log.
  Checked: unit tests for the scanners, the argument parsers, the rules and the real-tool resolution; `tests/guard_shims.rs` (the real binary, real git, a fake gh: a dashed commit refused and a clean one made, and the rest); `tests/guard_delivery.rs` (delivery with the shims on `PATH`); `tests/push_guard.rs` with the shims in front of git; a `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` run of the card in a throwaway home. Not checked: Linux, a real agent through the shims, a real `gh` visibility call, `git commit` with an editor.
- **Done check (branch `done-check`).** From the skill sweep (#4), the owner: "4 can be a setting". When an ordinary agent tab ends a turn that claims it is done (ledger state `done`, or an end block with something under Done and nothing under Needs you), Tidewatch checks committed, pushed (remote branch head equals the local head), the pull request (state, conflicts, checks on the pushed head) and the ledger's Not verified items, on a `Jobs` thread, and shows a Done, Blocked or Not verified card on the tab. Blocked names each failed check in one line and offers Ask agent to fix (through the existing idle-only paste); Not verified and Blocked offer Check again. A tab with no PR makes no `gh` call and shows that check as not checked with the reason. Settings, General, Done check turns it off (`[done_check]` in settings.toml, on by default); the Overview card gets a tag. Meeseeks are untouched.
  Numbers: 2,777 tests passing across the workspace on `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` (about 36 new), 1 failing that also fails on a clean checkout run from this session (`elsewhere_scan` refuses to end a child because stdusk is in its process chain). New tests: the outcome table, each check's wording, the PR line read from `gh pr view` JSON, real throwaway repos with a bare remote and a fake `gh` (no `gh` call without a PR, missing `gh`, merged PR, failing checks), the trigger and its dedupe, the card text, the settings round trip.
  Checked: `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` runs in a throwaway home and repo with a stand-in `claude` and `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_DONE_CHECK` (`docs/done-check-*.png`). Not checked: a live agent ending a turn, a live push and PR (never a real GitHub); the card shows below the reply box, not above it.

## 2026-10-20

- **The new tab form opens unfocused and Esc always lets go.** The owner: "the focus is trapped in the text field", "esc should always untrap us". No text field has the keyboard when the form opens, so A, M, W, S, 1-9 and the arrows work at once; Enter or / moves into the message box, and the footer says `Enter type`. egui drops focus on Esc before our code runs, so the end of each frame records it (`screen_kit::remember_focus`): an Esc that closes a dropdown or lets go of a field or button does only that, and the next one closes the draft; in terminal tabs a non-text widget that held the keyboard keeps that Esc from the shell. Tab is left to egui's order (it reaches the dropdown) rather than jumping to the message box. Checked: headless form tests, not by hand in the app.
- **v0.1.16.** Ships #89 and the push rule (#90).
- **Agents may push their own branch, nothing else (D7 changed).** The owner: "the push block just protects from destructive shit but the branch can still be pushed", "I want for all then not just for meeseeks", "Force push is allowed how else do you fold fixups?", "go ahead with the push rule". The `pushInsteadOf` rewrite stays; `git-remote-tidewatch-no-push` became a real git remote helper (list for-push from `git ls-remote`, push batches, `option cas` for `--force-with-lease`, `option force`, `option dry-run`) that forwards a batch to the real url with the guard key neutralised only when every ref is `refs/heads/$TIDEWATCH_PUSH_BRANCH`. `prepare` reads that branch from the worktree's HEAD (an `agent/` branch only, else nothing can be pushed). The house style tool guidance and the Meeseeks block say so, and the profile switch reads "Only let this agent push its own branch". Pull requests are still opened by Tidewatch.
  Checked: `tests/push_guard.rs` against a real bare remote (7 allowed forms land and move `origin/agent/...`, 15 refused forms change nothing and show the message, fetch works, no branch or no helper refuses all, a normal shell still pushes main), and a `prepare` test that pushes the tab's own branch through the prepared environment. Not checked: Linux (dash runs the helper script in CI), an ssh or https remote with credentials, Codex, Gemini and Copilot shells.
- **Meeseeks always run in YOLO and you pick only the agent CLI (branch `meeseeks-yolo`).** The owner: "meeseeks should always be yolo and should implicitly be builder so you only choose your flavor like codex, claude etc." A Meeseeks form's lane lists the four CLIs (dimmed with "not installed" when missing, keys 1-4) instead of profiles, the Permission row is replaced by one muted line ("Meeseeks always run in YOLO, in their own worktree."), and Left and Right do nothing there.
  How: the profile is implicit (`new_tab_meeseeks::profile_for`): the CLI's Builder (a profile named or keyed `builder`, or ending ` Builder` / `-builder`, ignoring case, so the seeded `Gemini CLI Builder` counts), else its first profile, else `Profile::defaults_for` (saved as profile `default`; a restore resumes it on the same defaults). `launch_command` still refuses YOLO outside a worktree and the never-push guard is untouched. The last Meeseeks CLI is `[new_tab] meeseeks_agent`, apart from `agent`; a Meeseeks no longer sets the last agent profile.
  Resume: unchanged. `resume_mode::decide` has no Meeseeks case, so a restored Meeseeks (saved level YOLO) is typed at its prompt and waits for Enter, like any YOLO tab.
  Guesses: the lane lists every CLI in the agent table (custom ones too), not only found ones; the ` Builder` suffix match is mine (the spec said named Builder, but seeded profiles of the second and later CLIs carry the CLI's name).
  Numbers: 2,584 tests passing, up from 2,572 on the main this branch sits on (12 new: the Builder pick table, the CLI defaults, the lane rows and subtitles, always-YOLO and the request, number keys, the remembered CLI, the form without a Permission row, the prefs key, the restore of a defaults Meeseeks). `elsewhere_scan::a_child_the_test_started_is_ended_by_sigterm_through_the_real_system` fails here as before.
  Checked: a `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` run (background, throwaway home, 1440x900) of the form with two tasks: four CLI rows with `Builder` subtitles, the YOLO line above the task box. Not checked: a real Summon with a real agent per CLI, a restart resuming a defaults Meeseeks, Linux.
- **v0.1.15.** Ships Meeseeks (#87).

- **Meeseeks: a one-task agent that ends itself (branch `meeseeks`).** The owner: "a one task type of thing that auto self destructs when it determines its completed", then "I want it to be Meeseeks and have a Meeseeks avatar". The new-tab kind control is Agent | Meeseeks | Workflow | Shell (key M); the box takes one task per line, each in its own worktree named from its line, with Done means ticks (Tests pass, Work committed, Open a PR; remembered in `[new_tab.meeseeks]`; Tests pass is off with its reason when the repo has no test command) and Summon N. A Meeseeks tab wears a fixed face (`assets/meeseeks.svg`), shows a stage card in Progress (Change, Tests, Proof, Hand off, `12m · turn 9 of 40`) and is told in its house style to call the MCP tool `meeseeks_done {summary}` once. Then Tidewatch checks off the UI thread, in order: tree clean with a commit ahead of the base, the repo's test command (its timeout), tree still clean, and the PR through `delivery.rs` with the summary as title when ticked. A failed check is pasted back (the failing tail for tests) through the existing Enter queue while the agent is idle. A pass ends the agent, removes the worktree folder, keeps the branch (a dirty tree is refused before the agent is touched), fades the tab over 600 ms, toasts `<name> poofed`, and writes `.state/meeseeks.toml` for the Overview's Poofed today. 3 failed checks, 40 turns (Stop hooks) or 60 minutes turn the tab orange with Take over (an ordinary agent, criteria dropped) and Give up (the normal close path); nothing is ever deleted on a struggle. A Meeseeks is saved in `session.toml` and the restart handoff and resumes as one; a restart waits while one is being checked. New dev switch `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_MEESEEKS=on|struggling|poofed`; `TIDEWATCH_DEV_DIALOG=meeseeks` opens the form on it.
  Decisions and guesses: the stage row is the check order (Change = the commit check, Tests, Proof = the tree is still clean after them, Hand off = the PR when ticked, then the poof), so the mockup's "npm test running" sits under Tests, not Proof; a PR that cannot open counts as a failed check and is pasted back; YOLO follows the agent form's rule (open in a repo, refused in Other, where a Meeseeks is not offered); the catchphrase "Caaan do!" is the face's hover and the toast when Meeseeks are summoned; the checks run even when the profile has the house style off (the one-task block forces it on).
  Numbers: 2,571 tests passing, up from 2,476 on the main this branch sits on (95 new: the check state machine and struggle rules, the checks against real throwaway repos with a bare remote and a fake gh, the poof log, the tool, form state and the ticks, the headless form (kind control, Summon, ticks), the card (Take over, Give up), the tab strip, avatar host, session, handoff and resume round trips); `elsewhere_scan::a_child_the_test_started_is_ended_by_sigterm_through_the_real_system` fails inside a stdusk terminal, by design.
  Checked: `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` runs (background, throwaway home and a throwaway `HOME` so the Overview lists no real sessions) of the form with three lines, a running, a struggling and a poofed Meeseeks, against the mockup; two real Claude Code (Haiku) runs through the real form path in a throwaway repo with `test = "true"`: `meeseeks_done`, the three checks passed, the worktree folder was gone and `agent/add-file-hello-txt` kept its commit, both times. Not checked: Linux, a real PR (only a fake gh), a real struggle or Take over with a live agent, a real restart handoff of a Meeseeks, Codex, Gemini or Copilot as a Meeseeks.
- **v0.1.14.** Ships parked tabs and the squash fix (#85).
- **Six tabs stop squashing, and quiet tabs park (branch `parked-tabs`).** The owner: "yes build parked tabs and the squash fix". Squash: an agent tab shows a 20 px face (its avatar, else a tile with its profile's first letter in its CLI's colors) in place of the number and the CLI logo, the job percent leaves the title (the 2 px bar stays), a tab is never narrower than 140 px (`layout::MIN_TAB_W`, `tab_capacity`), and the number, the Cmd+N chord and the job line are in the tooltip. Parked: a "Parked N" pill at the right end of the strip (three stacked faces, a list with the reason in its tone: `Running 64% · ETA 3h 10m`, `Waiting until Fri 23 Oct`, `Idle 6h`), a click on a row brings the tab back and selects it; a tab can be dragged onto the pill (it shows as `Drop to park` while a tab is dragged and nothing is parked), parked from its right-click menu or with Cmd+Shift+K (Linux Ctrl+Shift+K), and marked "Never park this tab".
  Rules (`park.rs`, pure): park after `park_after` untouched (Settings, General, Tabs: Off, 1 h, 4 h default, 8 h, 24 h), after 30 min with a long job (30 min left or run) or a dated open scheduled item, and the least recently touched eligible tabs when the strip does not fit at 140 px; never the active, pinned, draft, never-park or needs-you tab; a tab returns when its agent needs you, its job finishes or stalls, its item comes due or goes, or you open it (palette and the Overview too); manual parks stay until opened. A tab that returns by itself starts its quiet time again. Parked state and why survive `session.toml` (`park` marks per window, old files load unparked) and a restart handoff. A parked tab keeps running and is not painted.
  Guesses: the status "dot" the brief mentions is the top-edge status bar (the strip has no dot), kept as it is; a tab that needs you comes back even if parked by hand; the profile letter is the first letter of the profile name; the ledger ring stays and costs a title 22 px at the 140 px floor; day-only dates, so the `Waiting until` line has no time. Palette rows for parked tabs read `Open parked tab` with no number chord, and the number chords count only shown tabs.
  New dev switches: `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_PARKED=1` (three shown, three parked with the three reasons; `=visible` six shown with the rules on), `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PARKED_POPOVER=1`.
  Numbers: 2,475 tests passing, up from 2,417 (58 new: the rules, touches, work and schedule facts, reason lines, tab list parking, minimum width and capacity, palette rows, chord and gate, settings, session and handoff marks, restore, headless pill click, row click, drag onto the pill and the right-click menu); the one stdusk-terminal test fails as before. Thread-touching restore and handoff tests run 5 times clean.
  Checked: `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` runs (background, throwaway home, 1440x900 and 900x600) of the pill, the open list against the mockup, and six tabs before and after in a narrow window (before: titles of two letters; after: faces, three parked by overflow).
  Not checked: Linux, a real agent parking and returning by its own job or date, a real restart handoff of a parked tab, the Settings card by eye, a light theme.

## 2026-10-19

- **v0.1.13.** Ships the stuck update repair (#82), paste, GitHub links and origin/main (#83), and job progress with avatars (#84).
- **Long jobs show their progress, and agents draw their own avatar (branch `jobs-avatars`).** The owner approved both mockups with "build it". Jobs: each tab keeps a board of jobs merged by name from three sources: lines a script appends to the file in `TIDEWATCH_PROGRESS` (set for every agent, tailed at most once a second, bad lines skipped), the agent's `report_job` tool, and house-bar or `[n/N]` lines read off the terminal screen (new rows only). The tab gets a small percent and a 2 px bar, the Progress panel gets cards above the ledger (percent, counts, rate sparkline, RATE, ETA, DONE AT; a segmented card for a small total; an orange stalled card with Ask agent; one line per finished job), and the Overview card shows `21% · 1h 51m`. Rate comes from the latest 12 samples; ETA never shows before 3 samples, without a total, past the total, or beyond 100 days; a running job whose `done` stood still 5 minutes is stalled. Avatars: `set_avatar {svg, reason}` is cleaned by `avatar::sanitize` (roxmltree, a DOCTYPE or parse failure refused, only svg, g and the seven shapes with their own geometry and style attributes kept, no `url(`, `style` or `href`, 8 KB), rasterized once per picture and size with resvg, and drawn on the tab (20 px), the Overview card and the History header, with the reason on hover and Redraw or Use letter on right-click. Both persist in the tab's state folder (`jobs.json`, `avatar.json`).
  Decisions: no `egui_extras` (resvg 0.45.1 was already a dependency and the mascot already uses it); the one regex of the plan is a hand parser, so no regex dependency; `roxmltree` 0.20.0 (already in the lock through usvg) and `jiff` 0.2.38 (already in the lock through gix, for the local clock of DONE AT) became direct dependencies, no new crate. The Overview card stands in for the lane of the mockup, since the window has no list of running agents.
  New dev switches: `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_JOBS=1`, `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_AVATARS=1`. The `report_job` and `set_avatar` lines are a fixed tool-guidance block appended after the house style (like the ledger format), so an older or edited `house-style.md` gets them with no reset.
  Numbers: 2,417 tests passing, up from 2,287 on the main this branch sits on (128 new: job model, feed, bar parser, MCP tools, sanitizer tables with hostile input, host, cards, tab strip, header; the one stdusk-terminal test fails as before).
  Checked: `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` runs (background, throwaway home, 1440x900) of the four mockup jobs, six avatars on six tabs and the Overview cards, by eye against both mockups (a stalled card overlapped the finished line at first; fixed). One real Claude Code run with `--model haiku` in a throwaway repo (remote control off, `--allowedTools Bash` on that profile): the 5 lines the script appended to `$TIDEWATCH_PROGRESS` became a finished `demo import` line, `report_job` made the segmented CI checks card and the tab's 40%, `set_avatar` drew the bar chart on the tab and the header. A first attempt stalled on Claude's own Bash permission prompt.

- **Paste, open from a GitHub link, start from origin/main (branch `paste-clone-base`).** The owner: "allow me to paste in the window... it's disabled RN", "allow me to open a repo from a GH link", "Every worktree should start by default from origin/main".
  Paste root cause: egui turns Cmd+V into `Event::Paste` and never a key press, and the terminal only read typed text and key events (`collect_input`), so a paste into an agent or shell tab went nowhere; text fields were fine (egui's own). Now `paste.rs` reads the event when no modal or text field has the keys and writes it through `PtyTerm::paste` (bracketed when the program armed mode 2004), CRLF and LF as CR, control bytes dropped, the end trimmed. Ctrl+V already reaches the pty as 0x16 for Claude's image paste; a Cmd+V over an image-only clipboard sends nothing (it needs stdusk's NSEvent hook and a clipboard dependency). New dev switch `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PASTE`.
  Open from a link: Open repo (Cmd+O, palette, repo chip, welcome button) opens a dialog with a GitHub link field and Browse folders for the old picker; `GithubRepo::parse` takes web, `.git`, ssh and `/tree/` or `/pull/N` links; a known checkout whose `origin` matches (https or ssh, any case) opens as it is, else `git clone` into `<parent>/<repo>` (parent next to the repo opened last, else `~/Git`, editable) on a job thread with git's progress line, `gh repo clone` once if git failed; an occupied target is refused; a link pasted on the welcome screen opens the dialog filled. Dev switches `TIDEWATCH_DEV_LINK`, `_PARENT`, `_REMOTE`, `_GO`, `_FAKE`.
  Start from: the default is now `origin/<default>` (origin/HEAD, else main, else master), fetched first with a 20 s limit; a failed fetch falls back to the copy already here, then to the current commit, and the agent tab says so until you type (workflow tabs only log it). Worktrees are added with `--no-track`. Settings, General, New tabs and the form's Start from field read `origin/main`. Only a pick in Settings is stored (`start_base`); the old `start_from` key is ignored, because earlier versions wrote `current` for everyone on any save.
  Numbers: 2,287 tests passing, up from 2,246 (41 new: paste prep and routing, bracketed and bare writes to a real pty, the dev paste switch, link parser tables, known-checkout and plan tests on real repos, clone, cancel and gh fallback against a local bare repo, dialog state, welcome known folders, remote default pick, fetch fallbacks, prefs migration, base pick and options). The one failure is `elsewhere_scan::a_child_the_test_started_is_ended_by_sigterm_through_the_real_system`, which fails inside a stdusk terminal by design.
  Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` (background, throwaway home, 1440x900): a paste into a terminal tab showed `^[[200~...^[[201~` through a fake agent that arms mode 2004 and `cat -v`, a paste into the Reader composer, the link dialog, the clone card, an end-to-end clone from a local bare repo, a link that is already on the disk, the form's Start from `origin/main`, the Settings segmented control, and the fallback note over a tab whose remote is gone.
  Not checked: a real clone from GitHub or a private repo through gh, Cmd+V in a window (events were injected), an image paste into Claude, Linux, a Start pressed before the repo's facts load (it starts from the current commit).

- **Self-update repair (branch `update-repair`).** The owner: "fix the self-update bug". Seen live: Homebrew listed the cask as `0.1.10.upgrading`, the app ran from the Caskroom, every later `brew upgrade --cask` failed with "already an App", and the app never said so; it stayed on 0.1.10 for three releases. A killed `brew upgrade` (the app ran it from its own process group) leaves that folder, and the failure only showed in Settings.
  Decisions: `brew upgrade` runs in its own process group and refuses to start over a `.upgrading` folder; a failed or stuck update is a chip, not only a Settings line; Fix is a detached helper that waits for the app to exit, runs `brew reinstall --cask --force` and reopens it, started after a confirm and followed by the normal close so the session is saved.
  Numbers: 2,246 tests passing, up from 2,232 (14 new: the chip specs for failed, stuck and installing, the fake state switch, the Fix confirm's buttons, the wish and restart refusal, the repair script, the Updates section with a stuck update). Kelp side: 12 new tests in `kelp-core` and `kelp-ui`.
  Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` (background, throwaway home, 1440x900): the failed chip, the stuck chip, the Fix confirm and the Updates card, by eye. New dev switches: `TIDEWATCH_FAKE_UPDATE_STATE=failed|stuck|confirm`, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SCROLL_UPDATES`.
  Not checked: the helper against a real Homebrew (never run `brew` from an agent session), a real stuck folder, Linux.

A click on a card of a closed tab reopens it through the restore path.
