## 2026-10-17

- **Every agent keeps a live progress ledger: the engine (branch `progress-core`).** The owner: "Basically I want any tidewatch agent to always keep a ledger of what we're working on, status, etc. A progress / checklist view. We can render this beautifully if we prompt for the correct data" and "part of this we should also have scheduled things like if we need to do things in a week". This step is core and glue, no screens (a second worker builds them on `Ledger`, `TabEvent::Progress`, `progress_glue::Host` and `coming_up`). An agent sends its whole ledger (markdown, at most 32 KB) through the MCP tool `update_progress`: it is parsed by a parser that never fails (an unknown `##` section becomes an `Extra`, an unknown line stays as text under its section, an outer code fence is unwrapped), only an empty goal is refused (as a tool error to fix), the canonical text is saved atomically in `<tab state>/progress.md`, `TabEvent::Progress` goes to the app, and the agent gets one line back (`saved: phase 2 Build, 3/9 done, 1 scheduled`). `get_progress` returns the saved text after a context reset. The format with the checklist filled in (the default 10-phase Task to done list in `~/.config/tidewatch/checklist.md`, installed like `house-style.md` and never overwritten, or the file `checklist = "<path>"` in `<repo>/.tidewatch.toml` names) and one example per `<when>` kind, `you` included, is appended to the house style each agent reads: now a per-tab copy in the tab's state folder for all four CLIs (the user's `house-style.md` is never touched), so an edited house style gets the ledger too. Scheduled items of every tab sit in `.state/schedule.toml` (repo, tab, session id, profile id, each item with its `When`), rewritten when a ledger is saved and kept, marked closed, when the tab goes away; `coming_up(today, &Index)` sorts them (due first, dated, about-dates, then on, parked, you). Dates are plain civil-date arithmetic (`civil_date.rs`, table-tested over five centuries). `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PROGRESS=<file>` loads a ledger into the first agent tab for captures.
  Decisions: the ledger rides the house style, so a profile with the house style switched off gets none; the stored text is the canonical rewrite, not the raw text, so `get_progress` and the file always parse back to the same ledger (an unknown section moves to the end); a `When` the parser cannot read is kept as `Unknown` instead of being dropped; "today" is the UTC date; `is_blocked` is the agent saying so or any blocked item while the work is not done.
  Numbers: 2,090 tests passing, up from 2,008 (82 new: civil dates over five centuries, the parser on four real snapshots and hand-written ones, a seeded fuzz that writes then reads 300 random ledgers, the default checklist, the instruction's examples read back as their kind, prepare for all four CLIs, both tools over JSON-RPC and over a real socket including a new process adopting the old token, the schedule file and its writer, the app state, host and Coming up order); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean. A stand-in `claude` that reads the real composed house style and calls the tools over HTTP in a background Tidewatch saved 3 ledgers (phase 1, 2, 3; 0, 3, 9 done), `progress.md` and `schedule.toml` came out as expected.
  Not checked: a real Claude following the instruction (two launches stopped before the first message: Claude's folder-trust prompt defaults to "No, exit", and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_START` sends the task before `TIDEWATCH_DEV_KEYS` has answered it, so the key answer probably has to come earlier with a shorter `TIDEWATCH_DEV_KEYS_AFTER`, not tried), the other three CLIs reading the new file, a ledger past the 32 KB cap from a real agent, Linux.
- **The new tab form gets a lane, and every button belongs.** At the owner's real window size the grid was a strip one card tall and the footer sat inside the message box. The form is now a header over two columns: a 240 to 280 px lane at the left (the filter, then profiles grouped by CLI or workflows, one 46 px row each, scrolling by itself with the edge fade and keeping the picked row in view; 1-9 and Up/Down move the pick) and the pane at the right (permission with its text wrapping under it, worktree name and start branch side by side, the message box taking the rest of the height, never under 96 px, the footer pinned under it). Short of that the pane scrolls as a whole and the footer stays. The geometry is a pure function (`new_tab_layout.rs`), table-tested at 1280x720, 1440x900, 1100x640 and a 980x560 column, plus a headless run that checks no two parts meet. Buttons are one system in `screen_kit`: `Size::Regular` 32 px (forms, dialogs, Send) and `Size::Compact` 26 px (card actions, rows, Settings rows), 8 px corners, hover, pressed and 40% disabled looks, a shortcut as a key cap inside Start, and `footer_row` right-aligns dialog buttons 8 px apart; Settings calls the same system. `TIDEWATCH_DEV_WINDOW=<w>x<h>` opens the window at a size for captures. Numbers: tests 1,978 -> 2,008. Open: the showcase screenshot of the form is of the old layout; the owner's live check.

- **v0.1.10.** Ships the new tab as a form (#74).
- **A new tab is the form (branch `new-tab-form`).** The owner: "I choose agent for example and I only see the first option I think we can have a better UI/UX here maybe 2 per row slightly more condensed, Also I'm thinking this panel Couldn't it be integrated in the left side here?", then of the board: "Yeees, legit!". Cmd+T (the strip's +, the palette's new-tab commands) opens a draft tab at once, labelled in italics `New agent` / `New workflow`; its left column is the form (kind control with the keys A W S, profiles or workflows as a two-column card grid with 1-9, the five permission levels with Left and Right, worktree name, start branch, first message or task, Start with Cmd+Enter) and History stays on the right. Nothing exists until Start, then the same tab becomes the creating tab (worktree from the chosen branch under the chosen name, `worktree::add_named` now takes the base) and the first message is the agent's first prompt at launch (`prepare::task_args`: `--` for Claude and Codex, `--prompt-interactive` for Gemini, `-i` for Copilot), so it never races a TUI that is still booting. Esc (while no text field has the keyboard) or closing the tab drops the draft; a draft is never saved in `session.toml` or handed over on a restart. The name follows a slug of the message (`branch_name`, table-tested) until typed over, is checked by git's rules and against the names taken, and a taken one offers `<name>-2`. The modal (`new_tab_dialog.rs`, `new_tab_pick.rs`) is gone; `TIDEWATCH_DEV_DIALOG` opens the form, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_FORM_MESSAGE` types into it and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_START=1` presses Start.
  Decisions: the History of a draft is the repo root's branch (it does not follow the start branch field); the PR base of a workflow run stays `detect_base`, the start branch only cuts the worktree; the status bar's `branch from base` still says the checked-out branch for a tab started from another one; the site page and its screenshot still show the old dialog until the next release.
  Numbers: 1,979 tests passing, up from 1,933 (46 new: slug and branch-name tables, `add_named` from a base, taken names, repo facts, the form's state, cards geometry, headless clicks and keys, draft in the pane, session, handover, view and status bar); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home and repo with stand-in CLIs: the agent form, with a message, with a taken name, the workflow form, and an agent tab right after Start with its message in the terminal.
  Not checked: a real agent starting with the first message as its prompt, Linux, a long list of branches in the dropdown, a window shorter than the form (the cards scroll).
- **v0.1.9.** Ships notes as one review (#65), GitHub PR cards (#67), agent questions as controls (#70), taking over sessions running elsewhere (#71), and block notes with waiting badges in History (#72, Kelp #21 and #22).

## 2026-10-16

- **Terminal font setting (branch `feat/terminal-font`, #66).** The owner, on Fedora: "font looks weird" (Powerlevel10k icons drew as boxes), then "this feature shouldn't be tied to my case". Settings, General, Terminal has a Font row: Built-in (the default, nothing changes for anyone) or any installed family from a search; `[terminal] font` in `settings.toml`, empty for the built-in face, like stdusk's `appearance.font`. The face goes into its own egui family (`tidewatch-terminal`), so the chrome's monospace text keeps the bundled face; a real bold face, when the family has one, draws bold cells. A pick saves and loads on a job thread and applies at once; a family that is not installed falls back to the bundled face with an orange note. The system font list (132 families, 18 ms here) is read on a thread at start, so the list never stalls a frame.
  Decision: no automatic Nerd Font pick. A first version picked an installed Nerd Font by default; the owner turned it down, because it would change the look for anyone who has one installed and does not use it.
  Needed stdusk #27 (`b227a05`, logged in stdusk's ledger): on Linux every face of a family had the same full name, so stdusk took the first one listed (Bold Italic, the whole terminal went italic) and never found a bold face; it now scores the PostScript name too. stdusk repinned from `c907c8c` to `b227a05` (also picks up stdusk 1.10.0's version bump).
  Numbers: app tests 980 to 988, core 551 to 553 (10 new: the family split, bold only with a bold face, the status line, the filter, the picker rows, a headless draw of the section, a real installed family and a missing one, the prefs round trip and an old file); fmt and `cargo test --workspace` clean; clippy `-D warnings` clean apart from Rust 1.97's `duration_suboptimal_units` in `test_command.rs`, which `main` fails too. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` on Fedora 44 in a throwaway `TIDEWATCH_HOME`: Built-in by default, and MesloLGS Nerd Font Mono set in `settings.toml` draws the prompt's icons upright. **Not verified:** macOS, a click on a font in a live window.
- **Notes UX round on Tidewatch's side: block notes and "waiting" badges in the timeline (branch `notes-timeline`).** The owner: "once I've left a comment there is not way to see in the timeline that a note is pending wihtin a commit / file", and block comments by drag like GitHub (the diff widget half is Kelp #21, pinned here). A note that still needs someone (a draft, or sent and not answered; `NoteState::is_waiting`, the one rule) shows as a speech bubble and count on its commit's row, on Uncommitted changes (notes with no commit) and before the +/- counts on its file's row in the open commit's list. A block note shows `path:a-b` on the review card, the sent review card and the notes strip, pastes as `path:a-b` with the whole block quoted, and posts to a PR with `start_line` and `start_side` (`line` is the last line); a pulled PR comment with `start_line` becomes a block note, and the Post check needs both ends in the diff. New dev switches: `TIDEWATCH_DEV_COMPOSER`, `_NOTE_SPAN`, `_SIDE`, and `row@` and ranges in `_NOTES`.
  Decisions: replied and agent-written notes do not count as waiting; the file view header at 0.3 width and Kelp's saved card at that width are Kelp's and the header's to fix, not touched here. Numbers: 1,933 tests passing, up from 1,909 (24 new: the waiting rule and counts, block paste and quotes, PR post and pull of ranges, the Post check, line labels, dev switches); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean. Checked in the background with a throwaway `TIDEWATCH_HOME` and repo: History badges, the open commit's file badges, the composer on lines 5-8, a saved block note ("Lines 3-6") and the review card (`:3-6`), wide and at `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SIDE=0.3`.
  Not checked: a real PR post of a block note (fake gh only), Linux, split layout with a block note.
- **Take over agent sessions running elsewhere (branch `session-takeover`).** The owner: "Like I asked you about a migration path of active sessions to tidewatch how can we take over an existing session?" then "build it". The Overview gets a Running elsewhere card (also a line on the welcome screen and the palette command Move sessions to Tidewatch): Claude Code sessions from its registry (`~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`, `.key` files never opened), Codex, Gemini and Copilot from `ps`, each with its host app (parent chain), permission level, status and a button. Nothing can be pulled out of a running process, so a move ends it (SIGTERM to that pid only, after a fresh scan found the same pid and start time idle, 5 s wait, never SIGKILL) and resumes the same conversation in a tab of its repo with the flags read back from its command line; a session in stdusk only hints at stdusk's own Move tabs to Tidewatch; a busy one arms a watch and moves when idle.
  Decisions: the confirm shows the command without Tidewatch's hooks wiring; YOLO cannot move without a worktree (Move is off, nothing is ended) instead of ending the session and opening a shell; "Move all idle here" is one dialog and leaves YOLO out; the worktree box is offered for Claude only (`claude --resume <id>` found a session from another folder on 2.1.301 in print mode, the interactive path could not be checked without a login); a watch keeps scanning every 5 s while the Overview is closed, which is the one time the scan runs unseen (never while the quake window is hidden otherwise).
  Numbers: 1,674 tests passing, up from 1,571 (103 new: ps and registry parsing, host chain, argv to flags per CLI, scan on a fake system, a real `sleep` child ended by SIGTERM, the row rules, watches, scan schedule, dialog content, the one-tab plan, card and dialog frames); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. Checked in the background with stand-in `claude` scripts and a throwaway `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `TIDEWATCH_HOME`: the card and the dialog screenshots, a move (stand-in got SIGTERM and exited, a tab opened with `claude --resume <id>` and the session's flags, `session.toml` has the tab) and a move into a worktree.
  Not checked: a real Codex, Gemini or Copilot move, a real interactive Claude resume from another folder, Linux, a registry written by Claude on Linux.
- **Agent questions as radio buttons and checkboxes (branch `agent-questions`).** The owner: "I also want if possible to get Q's from the agent as radio or checkbox and for me to interact with it more dynamically", then "build it". The Reader shows a live card when the agent asks a multiple-choice question: radio rows (label, description, a Recommended tag, the number key), checkbox tiles, a Something else field, `1 of 2` progress, Next, Back, Send answers (Cmd+Enter), Skip let <profile> pick; keys work only while the card has the keyboard (it takes it when it appears unless the reply box has text, a click gives it back, a click outside or the reply box takes it away). After the answer a compact summary card lists question and answer. Two sources: Claude's own `AskUserQuestion` (parsed from the session file in `transcript.rs`; the answer is typed into its menu as separate key presses 0.3 s apart, checked against claude 2.1.301 in real pty runs, see PLAN.md findings) and the new MCP tool `ask_user` (Codex, Gemini, Copilot or Claude; the listener holds the HTTP response up to 30 minutes, the tab shows as needing you meanwhile, a late answer after a timeout is typed as a normal message; Codex and Gemini get a 35 minute tool timeout). Under an agent's last message a yes or no question gets Yes and No chips, a numbered list offered as a choice gets one chip per item, plus Reply...; permission prompts use the same card (Allow once, Deny, the tool and its command). The default house style asks agents to ask this way; an existing `house-style.md` is untouched (Reset to default adds it).
  Numbers: 1,641 tests passing, up from 1,571 (70 new: the question model and parsers, the Claude fixture, `ask_user` over JSON-RPC and over a real socket, the key sequences, the card state, headless clicks and keys, the quick-reply parser table, rows, the typing queue, answering each origin). fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home with a stand-in `claude` and hand-written transcripts: the card (single, multiple, Recommended, Something else), the summary, Yes and No and numbered chips, the permission card; a stand-in that logs its stdin saw the five separate key chunks, and its `ask_user` call came back as `Q: ... A: ...`. **Not verified:** a real `ask_user` call from Codex, Gemini or Copilot, Copilot's tool timeout, whether Claude fires a Notification hook for its menu, "Always allow" (only `1` and Esc are verified), Linux.
- **Linux shows the Tidewatch icon (branch `fix/linux-app-icon`).** The owner: "add an icon to the app". On GNOME Wayland the window showed a generic icon: eframe set the app id to the app name, `Tidewatch`, which matches no desktop entry. The window now sets the app id `tidewatch` (the Wayland app id and X11 class), `packaging/tidewatch.desktop` gains `StartupWMClass=tidewatch`, and Linux also sets `assets/icon-512.png` as the window icon for window managers that read it from the window; macOS keeps the bundle's icon. Numbers: app tests 980 to 982 (2 new: the app id names the shipped desktop entry and its class, the icon decodes at 512x512); fmt and tests clean. Checked on Fedora 44 GNOME Wayland with the desktop entry and icons installed under `~/.local/share`: the dock and Alt+Tab show the icon. **Not verified:** X11, KDE, the AppImage.
- **Notes are one review, sent from one place.** The owner: "how we would 'Send' the agent all our comments on certain files 🤔 it's unclear to me right now", then "build it". A note never sends by itself: the open notes not sent yet are the tab's draft review across every file (state derived from `notes.json`, nothing new stored). `Review: N notes` is a chip in the file view header and the status bar (a click shows the Reader), Send review (N) replaces Send all, and "Your review" sits above the Reader's reply box and the composer (notes by file, optional message, Send review to <Profile>, Cmd+Enter, Discard in two clicks). A Working or NeedsYou agent is never pasted into: the review is held and goes when it is Done (the Deliver when idle switch is gone, its hold now serves this); a single note is sent from the `...` menu of its card. The paste may open with a message and `note_paste::parse` reads it back, so the Reader draws a sent review as one card (K of N answered, each reply under its note, Open in the file view, Resolve answered) and never the raw block. Pulled PR comments land in the same draft with their reviewer's name. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home and repo with a stand-in `claude` (hooks, transcript, `reply_to_note` over MCP): the file view, the card with the agent idle and busy, the sent card with one answer in and one note in progress. **Not done:** the `draft` tag on Kelp's own note card in the diff (needs a per-thread tag in kelp-ui, so a Kelp change and a `rev` bump; its hint says draft for now), opening a note at its line (DiffBody has no scroll-to-line, so the file opens). **Not verified:** Linux, a real agent CLI, a review sent to a NeedsYou agent end to end.
- **Site mascot mask in place.** The owner: "the maks is wronlgy offset here, like the line is exiting on the left". The animated SVGs set `transform-box: fill-box` on every element, so browsers turned the mask's tilt around its own box and it slid down and left, strap out of the body. The rule now covers only the animated parts. Checked in headless Chrome with motion off against the reference render: 46.8, 46.5 and 48.4 dB for hero, peek and lost (was visibly offset). The og image uses the still SVG and was never affected.
- **v0.1.8.** Ships the UI pass: batch 1 (#60), batch 2 (#61), batch 3 and the cleanup (#62), with Kelp's embedded note card (Kelp #20).
- **UI pass, batch 3: dialogs, palette, Overview, welcome and Settings (branch `ui-pass-3`).** The owner: "do an entire UI pass and make sure everything is sexy". Every dialog (new tab, close, leftovers, folder trust) is the shared `dialog_frame` with one muted key-hint line (`notice::key_hint`); selection is `selection_row` everywhere in these files; either/or settings are `segmented::inline` and long lists (the nine profiles, six efforts) `wrap_chips` through `settings_ui::pick`; Settings pages get a 48 px header with a full-width divider, bordered `quiet_button`s and red `danger_button`s for Delete, Remove stage and Reset; notice and toast are item cards centered on the left column while History shares the window. Numbers: 930 app tests, up from 929 (1 new: `fits_inline`); fmt and clippy clean apart from `section_label` and `card_stroke` in `screen_kit.rs`, now unused. Left: Panels in `window_view.rs` still uses `settings_ui::choice`; Overview's tab-strip `button` and the leftover chip are batch 1's.
- **UI pass, batch 1: workspace chrome and the notes strip (#60).** The owner: "the notes strip still looks old, make it a card too do an entire UI pass and make sure everything is sexy". Shared primitives first (`brand::ITEM`, `ITEM_LINE`, the CLI badge hues, `screen_kit::{quiet_button, danger_button, primary_button, dialog_frame, selection_row, paint_pill_card}`, `segmented::{inline, inline_toned, wrap_chips}`). The notes strip is one small card per note (ITEM fill, 8 px corners, 6 px gap; dimmed folder, file name in text ink, snippet cut with an ellipsis, a state pill, quiet Send and Post to PR); Kelp's thread card stays as `DiffStyle::embedded` draws it (`note_card`). The Below layout is two cards with the file view's 48 px header (`layout::split_at`, `history_pane`, `diff_pane`); tabs get the nav look (`app/strip.rs::nav_look`); the open commit row gets a teal bar and its files are small cards; the status bar also shows on the welcome screen and shows the composer's and task box's keys.
  Decisions: the left column (terminal, Reader, workflow tab) stays on the window ground, only the file view is a card there (G1); both the strip and Kelp's card stay (N7); Below stays, restyled (BL4); the per-CLI badge hues stay as named consts (X2). Not done here: the Overview button's active look (`overview_view.rs`, batch 3), a selected History row that is not the open one (Kelp paints its own rounded fill and exposes no row rects, so no bar), the welcome screenshot (the harness did not reach that screen), `app/panes.rs` still fills the empty Below panes with `kelp_theme::bg()`.
  Numbers: app tests 924 to 934 (10 new: the strip's card height, ellipsis, place split, state inks and click map; wrap flow; button and badge checks; nav look; status bar keys); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean. Checked in the background (throwaway `TIDEWATCH_HOME=/private/tmp/twu1`, stand-in `claude`, never focused): the side layout with a note and a file open, the Below layout, a shell tab.
- **UI pass, batch 2: Reader, delivery and workflow surfaces.** The owner: "do an entire UI pass and make sure everything is sexy". User message, tool lines, permission strip, end block card, delivery card, ask and stopped cards and the workflow stage cards are small cards (ITEM, 1 px ITEM_LINE, 8 px) at one 14 px inset (`reader_view::INSET`); tool lines show worktree-relative paths (folder FAINT, leaf TEXT, status dot, open on click); Send is quiet in the Reader and the one filled button in the composer; delivery, post confirm use `dialog_frame` with one muted hint line; the stepper is a 48 px header with a divider and 8 px pills joined by a connector; ask text is stripped of markdown. The small card, field and ellipsis helpers live in `reader_view.rs` until `screen_kit` takes them. Numbers: app tests 929 to 934 (5 new: relative tool paths, commands, code spans, post location, markdown strip); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home, repo and `HOME` (stand-in `claude`, fake `gh`): composer, tools, permission, end block, delivery card and dialog, post confirm, workflow start and running. **Not verified:** Linux, a focused field's teal line (the harness has no focus), R10 (prose still repeated above the end block).
- **v0.1.7.** Ships tab reordering (#57) and the file view card with Kelp's embedded diff look (#58).
- **File view is a card (branch `file-view-card`).** The owner: "The file viewer also looks pretty shit, again feels stitched how the tree and terminal used to before". Side by side the file view is a card like History: `brand::CARD`, 1 px border, 12 px corners, 10 px from the window edges and from the History card (`rect.shrink(CARD_MARGIN)`), the square diff body is masked at the bottom corners. One 48 px header (`CHROME_H`, tops line up with History's): `‹ Terminal` back button (the agent's profile and state moved into its tooltip), the path (folder dimmed, mono), `+a -d`, `in <sha>`; right side Send all (only when notes wait), Deliver when idle, the PR chip and Pull PR comments, `N of M` with previous and next file arrows (`TabView::step_file`, the same call as the commands) and the shared segmented control for Unified | Split. The toolbar row is gone: the change-jump arrows (Alt+Up and Alt+Down still work) and the notes summary went with it, and `Hover a line and press + to leave a note` is on the left of the status bar while a file is open. The notes strip stays above the diff. In the open commit the file the file view shows has a 2 px teal bar and a teal tint. Kelp repinned to `efca687` and the body uses `DiffStyle::embedded(brand::TEAL)` (tinted rows, left bars, hunk dividers, flat note cards); the Below layout keeps the default.
  Decision: the right-hand buttons are shed from the end of the priority list (Send all, Deliver, PR chip, Pull) when the card is narrow, and `in <sha>` goes before the path is cut; at a 720 px card with a note the sha is dropped and both note buttons fit, with a PR chip the PR buttons can disappear.
  Numbers: 1,553 tests passing, up from 1,545 (8 new: position text, origin, buttons that fit, left plan, back tip, path split, row mark, note hint); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home and repo (a commit of 10 files; plain, with an agent tab and a note, Split, Below unchanged). **Not verified:** Linux, a PR chip (no fake `gh` run), the card at other widths, a live click on the buttons.
- **Tabs can be reordered (branch `tab-reorder`).** The owner: "no I cannot drag tabs they are stuck". Ported from stdusk: a tab drags along the strip (`tab_row` in `app/strip.rs`: a decided drag past the next tab's middle emits one `Action::Move(i, Step)`, re-derived every frame, Grabbing cursor, a highlight on a middle layer) and `Cmd+Shift+Left / Right` (Linux `Ctrl+Shift+PageUp / PageDown`) move the active tab (`move-tab-left`, `move-tab-right`, also in the palette). The reducer (`Tabs::move_tab`) swaps with the neighbour of the same group only, refuses the pinned main tab and the ends, and the active index follows its tab; other groups and the remembered tab of a group out of sight are untouched. The keyboard chords wait for a focused text field (`Focus::TextField`, new) and every dialog, Settings, the palette and the Overview; the terminal does not forward Cmd+Shift+Left/Right or Shift+Page keys. A move is not an `Action` that closes Settings or the Overview (`dispatch` applies it first). The saved session keeps the order (the signature hashes the group's tabs in order; restore opens them in saved order).
  Numbers: app tests 901 to 916 (15 new: reducer table, ends and pinned, active, groups, remembered tab; chords and labels, gate and text-field rows; five headless egui drag frames: one move past the midpoint, a long drag walks two places, plain and sloppy clicks never reorder, the pinned tab neither moves nor is passed, only the visible group; session order and signature); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. **Not verified:** a live drag with a real mouse, Linux.
- **v0.1.6.** Ships the six built-in habit workflows (#54) and Build workflow from this session (#55).
- **v0.1.5.** Ships the History card and status bar (#48), moving tabs from stdusk (#49, needs stdusk 1.10.0), the clean list rows (#50), YOLO resume with flags (#51) and the retaken site screenshots (#52).
- **Six house workflows built in.** The owner: "analyze my local memories, workflows, activity and skills and use those to build predefined workflows for tidewatch." Hunt a root cause (4 stages: prove the cause with a failing test, approve it, fix, review), Finish with proof (3: run the checks, audit every ask against the real state, a verified / not verified report), Change the UI with proof (5: baseline renders with a noise floor, change, re-render and explain every difference, review, hand off), Port to a platform (5: parity audit you approve, port behind platform gates, review for drift on the original, same-behavior gate, hand off), Prepare a release (4: version, lockfile and notes, review, artifact checks, a checklist that never tags) and Refactor safely (4: pin behavior with tests, refactor, review for drift, hand off). They live in `builtin_workflows.rs`, are written in tool-neutral words, and none pushes or tags: the last stage of each is a Handoff at an Approve gate.
  Decision: an existing `workflows.toml` gains the new built-ins once, appended after the user's own text (never rewritten), with the ids recorded in `workflows.offered` beside it. A built-in the user deleted stays deleted, an edited or same-named workflow is left alone, the original three count as offered so a file that dropped one never gets it back, and a file that does not parse is untouched and not marked. The dialog lists built-ins in `BUILT_IN_IDS` order, the editor stays alphabetical by id.
  Numbers: 1,489 tests passing, up from 1,473 (16 new: every built-in validates with known placeholders and plain hyphens, shapes and loop targets, the upgrade plan table, byte-for-byte append, delete and edit survival, a bad file); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home and repo: the new-tab dialog's workflow list and the workflow editor. **Not verified:** a run of any new workflow end to end with a real agent, Linux.
- **Build workflow from this session.** A palette-only command (`build-workflow`, no chord on either platform, shown as "palette only" on the Shortcuts page and in `docs/shortcuts.md`) on an agent tab that is Done or Ready and not a workflow stage. It pastes `propose::prompt()` into the agent (the same paste and Enter queue as a note) and waits. The prompt asks the agent to review its whole session and turn the repeatable part into a workflow for a new task; its schema text is written from `Action::ALL`, `Gate::ALL`, the levels and the placeholders, with an example that is itself rendered from a `Workflow` and tested valid. The answer is the MCP tool `propose_workflow` (name, description, stages with action, prompt, gate, agent, permissions, on_fail, max_loops, approve) or, when the turn ends without a call, the last `toml` block of the Reader's assistant rows after the prompt (the 1.5, 4 and 8 s looks after Done, because the transcript lags the hook). Both pass the editor's rules plus no YOLO, no profile, known agent ids; the tool answers problems as a tool error so the agent fixes and calls again. A valid proposal opens Settings, Workflows with a new draft marked "From <tab>", the name de-duplicated ("Name (2)"), its own file key, a toast, and nothing written until Save; Revert (new on this page) throws away every unsaved edit. A row that cannot run shows dim in the palette with its reason ("agent is working", "agent is waiting on you", "needs an agent tab", "not in a workflow run"...), and Enter skips it.
  Decisions: a stage with no `agent` takes the agent that proposed it; `model`, `effort` and `profile` are not in the tool (they name this machine's CLIs and profiles). Not changed: the built-in workflows, the gates, how a run starts.
  Numbers: 1,515 tests passing, up from 1,473 (42 new: the prompt content and schema, the tool schema and argument parsing, validation messages, toml extraction, name and key de-dup, command enablement and its table rows, the palette's disabled row, the editor draft and revert, the wait and the fallback scan, one MCP unit round trip and one through the HTTP listener); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean.
  Checked in the background (throwaway `TIDEWATCH_HOME=/tmp/bw/home`, never focused) with a stand-in `claude` first on `PATH`: `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PRESS=build-workflow@12` pasted the 5,106-byte prompt, the stand-in's first `propose_workflow` call (YOLO) came back as a tool error, its second opened the draft ("Proposed by <tab>", Unsaved changes, `workflows.toml` untouched); a second run where the stand-in answered with a `toml` block in its transcript opened the same draft through the fallback; `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PROPOSE=<file>` and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PALETTE="build wo"` on a shell tab captured the draft and the disabled row.
  Left: a real agent run (Claude, Gemini, Codex, Copilot each decide how much of the prompt they follow), and the Ready state of a fresh tab that has gone quiet (it shows Needs you, so the command waits for its first turn).
- **GitHub PR statuses as cards in the agent chat (branch `pr-cards`).** The owner: "I also want tidewatch to prompt to the agent to respond when PR is opened and shit like this to have first party integration statuses that we can display in our markdown chat window to make it as sexy and interactive as possible", then "build it". After a delivery Tidewatch no longer goes silent: a background look (`gh pr view <n> --json ...`, every 20 s while checks run, every 120 s otherwise, only while the PR is open, off when the tab's mode is Off) is diffed against what was seen into cards drawn in the Reader between the transcript's rows by time: opened, checks running (live, updated in place), failed (jobs, the failing step, the log tail of `gh run view --job --log-failed`, Ask / Full log / Re-run), passed, changes requested (each inline comment, Ask / Open as notes through the existing pull), comment (Ask / Reply myself), conflict, approved (Merge on GitHub only opens the browser), merged (Close tab and clean up goes through the close dialog, Keep it), closed, Push fix (the existing delivery card and dialog), the message Tidewatch pasted as a user bubble ("You, via Tidewatch" or "Tidewatch, auto-fix", the transcript's own copy is left out), the waiting row and "PR status paused: gh is not signed in". A PR strip under the status line (pills, the mode chip and its popover), a PR pill on each Overview row, a status bar item, a desktop notification through `notify.rs` (window away, one per tab and kind per minute) and a GitHub card in Settings, General (`[github]` in `settings.toml`). Per tab: Ask me, Auto-fix when idle (`note_send`'s gate, the Enter queue, at most 3 sends per PR, then Ask me) or Off, with three ticks. Cards are saved in `.state/<repo-dir>/<tab>/pr_cards.json` (atomic) so a restart shows them.
  Decisions: times on cards are ages (`4m ago`), because nothing in the app knows the local clock; the conflict message asks the agent to merge `origin/<base>` rather than rebase, because the agent cannot force-push and Tidewatch's delivery pushes plain commits only (the button still says rebase, as in the approved mockup); a failed card waits until every check settled; a bot's comment is a card like any other, the user's own are skipped (`viewerDidAuthor`).
  Numbers: 1,692 tests passing, up from 1,571 (121 new: the snapshot parse and diff on two redacted real `gh` outputs, the log tail on a real failed job, the cards store and when a button shows, the messages, the watch rules (cadence, auto-fix gate, hold, cap, waiting rows, pause), the timeline merge, each card's words and buttons, headless frames of every card and the strip, the Reader rows' times, `[github]`, the notifier, a fake `gh` for one look in each shape); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home and repo with a stand-in `claude`, a fake `gh` and `TIDEWATCH_DEV_PR_EVENTS`: the running and failed cards with the bubble, every card state, the live buttons, the waiting row (auto-fix, agent working), the paused row with the popover, the Overview pill, Settings. **Not verified:** a live look at a real PR of the user's (only public PRs were read, read-only), a real agent's transcript echo of the pasted message (matched by text, falls back to showing both), a live click on any button, `StatusContext` checks and a PR with a still-running check (shapes from the GraphQL schema, not seen live), Linux, the paste into a real agent.

## 2026-10-15

- **History card on Kelp's embedded graph (#50).** Both kelp crates repinned to `31200ee` (Kelp PR #18). Side by side the graph is `GraphStyle::embedded(brand::TEAL)`: no header, inline ref chips, no avatars, no row bands, teal lanes and selection, 36 px rows, the Uncommitted changes row on top (the status Tidewatch already loads). The open commit's files are no longer laid over the rows (the card was cut off at the bottom): the graph opens a gap under the row (`expanded = (row, height)`), the card is painted in `expanded_rect` clipped to `viewport_rect`, and opening a commit sets `scroll_to` so the row and its whole card come into view (the request waits until the files have loaded, so the gap's final height is the one scrolled to). Card: 9 px corners, teal tint and border around row and files, meta line `sha · author · N files · +a -d`, 30 px file rows (kind letter, dimmed folder, name, +/- in fixed columns); past the room it scrolls inside (rows virtualized), never under 160 px. Removed the overlay math (`Geometry`, `row_geometry`, the scroll-state reads, `band_height` and `fit`). Below keeps Kelp's default look.
  Decision: the graph's gap hangs under commit rows only, so the Uncommitted row has no card: a click on it opens its first changed file (Up and Down still step the files). Not changed: keys, double-click checkout, file view.

Checked with `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` in a throwaway home and repo (20 commits, a dirty file, long titles, one commit of 34 files): the plain list, the last commit opened (card fully visible), a middle commit with a file view (card scrolls inside), and Below unchanged. **Not verified:** Linux, a very narrow window, many lanes in Whole repo.

It now resumes with `launch_command` for its profile at the level it ran at plus the CLI's resume args (claude, codex, gemini, copilot), keeping every other check (agent known and installed, profile present and same agent, transcript, no duplicate, never-push guard, hooks, MCP). `resume_mode::decide` holds the rule: a YOLO tab is typed at the shell prompt and waits for Enter in Auto and Prefill, clean or crashed, with its own note ("YOLO tabs wait for you. Press Enter to resume with the same permissions."); Off is a plain shell as for every agent. Unchanged: a YOLO workflow run is still not resumed (`Reason::Yolo` now only says so for workflows), and a YOLO tab outside a worktree is still refused by `launch_command` (the reopen job turns it into a shell with that reason). `ResumeError::Yolo` is gone.

The update pill moved to the right end of the strip, before the gear, as a quiet chip (dark fill, teal dot, `0.1.5 available` and a teal Update, or `0.1.5 ready` and Restart once installed); no Kelp green. No new repaint requests. New: `segmented.rs`, `status_bar.rs`, `update_chip.rs`, `app/bar.rs`; brand tokens `SEGMENT`, `SEGMENT_LINE`, `SEGMENT_ON`.
  Numbers: 1,452 tests passing (864 app, 507 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 17 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 1,440 (12 new: segment geometry, header pills, hint choice and chords, counts, chip spec, status line control); fmt, clippy `-D warnings` and tests clean on macOS. **Not verified:** Linux, a real agent CLI (a stand-in `claude`), the Restart state of the chip (a fake update is only Available), the card at a narrow window width.

- **Moving tabs from stdusk, the receiving side (branch `from-stdusk`).** stdusk's "Move tabs to Tidewatch" launches `tidewatch --from-stdusk <socket>` and sends its live panes (`stdusk_core::handoff::export::Export`, stdusk PR #25, both stdusk crates repinned to `d40ae81`). New instance command `adopt-stdusk <socket>` (`Command::AdoptStdusk`, answered `ok` or `closing` like `open`): a running window of the same home gets it and the launch exits; with none, or once a `closing` window is gone, the launch becomes the window and receives at startup. The window receives on a job thread (`from_stdusk::receive`): `Incoming::<Export>`, `pty::adopt` each pane (cwd from `PaneMeta::cwd`), then `handoff::confirm_terms` (the restart's `confirm`, now generic over the layout and over terminals not in tabs yet: ack, then arm teardown). Any failure before the ACK adopts nothing, shows a notice and stdusk keeps its window. Placement (`from_stdusk::plan`, pure): each pane is a shell tab in the group of the git repo of its folder (git look-up on the job thread): the group the window holds, else a new group (`add_workspace`, split out of `adopt_workspace`), else the folder's own Other group; the title is stdusk's, else the folder name; the focused pane becomes the active tab; the welcome screen is replaced as usual.
  Decision: "the Other group" is one group per folder, as everywhere else in Tidewatch (a Workspace is a folder), so panes outside any repo group by their folder. Known edge: `adopt_pane` reaps the process group of a pane that fails to adopt, so a failure on pane N reaps that one shell although stdusk keeps its window; every other pane is only dropped unarmed (left alone).
  Numbers: 1,451 tests passing (862 app, 508 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 17 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 1,440 (11 new: the command on the wire, the `--from-stdusk` parse, the single-instance hand-over and takeover, the plan tables, the names, and a real pty round trip in one process with stdusk's `send_session`, the shells still answering); `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS.
  Checked live in the background (`TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND=1`, `TIDEWATCH_HOME=/tmp/twf`, throwaway repos a and b plus a plain folder, `examples/fake_stdusk.rs` as the sender with three shells; everything started was killed and the folders removed): the three pids stayed alive with parent 1 after the sender exited, the status log showed `shell adopt-stdusk <socket>`, and `session.toml` had a shell tab in the existing group of repo a, one in an Other group for the plain folder and one in a new group for repo b, whose tab (the focused one) was active. `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SHOT` showed the b group with the "moved 3" tab and its prompt. With no window running the launch became the window, received at startup and `session.toml` had the same shell tabs in their repos. **Not verified:** the real stdusk sending (its PR #25 is not merged here) and the `open -n -a` launch from a bundle.

Start an agent with Cmd+T.", the chord read from `commands.rs`), `caught` above the typed-resume note on a tab whose resume command waits for Enter. `busy` for the restart is skipped: the hand-over runs on the UI thread and the pill has no restarting state, so no frame is painted while it runs; the busy art is not bundled. - **v0.1.3.** Ships the live handover on update (#43), Open at login and resume modes (#42).
- **Open at login and agent resume modes (branch `login-resume`, off `main`, not pushed).** Settings, General, Startup now has Resume agents (Resume, Type only, Off; `resume_agents` in `settings.toml`, default `auto`) and, on macOS, Open at login (`SMAppService`, status read from the system each time the page shows, never stored; disabled with a reason outside an `.app`; no row on Linux). A resumed agent is run, or typed at its shell's first prompt and left for Enter, by `resume_mode::decide` (setting x how it ended x YOLO, table-tested; YOLO never resumes, Off is a shell). A crash always means typed: Tidewatch leaves `.state/running` from start to clean quit, so a launch that finds it knows the last run crashed or was killed, and an agent whose process exits with a status other than 0, 130 or 143 is saved with `crashed = true`. A typed tab keeps its profile and session id and shows a pill that says why until the user types. Workflow tabs ignore the setting. Details and the differences from stdusk are in the PLAN.md Deviations log.
  Numbers: 1,390 tests passing (808 app, 503 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 15 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 1,346; `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS (Rust 1.93 here, CI uses 1.99, not run). Checked live with `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND` in `TIDEWATCH_HOME=/tmp/twr/h`, a throwaway repo, a stand-in `claude` that logs its arguments and a seeded `session.toml`: Auto resumed the agent (stand-in ran with `--resume`); after a `kill -9` the marker stayed and the next launch typed the command without running it (pill: "Tidewatch stopped unexpectedly last time..."); a clean quit removed the marker; Type only typed it; Off opened a plain shell and never ran it. `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT` of Settings, General (`/tmp/twr/general.png`) shows the new rows. **Not verified:** a real register, approve and unregister of the login item from a signed `.app` (it changes the real login items; no test or run calls it), macOS older than 13, a real agent killed by a signal or by the system (a signal reads as exit status 1 through the pty library, so it cannot be told from a failing exit, and both are typed), a shell that marks no prompt (fish: fallback timer, not run), Linux. **Open:** a typed tab's status line says Working although no agent runs yet; `DEV_QUIT` never fires in a background run that sits idle with an agent running (needs a repaint), so a clean quit there was checked with typed or shell tabs.
- **Restart keeps every shell and agent running (branch `live-handoff`, off `main`, not pushed).** The update pill's Restart no longer runs kelp-ui's `open -n` and close (that cannot pass an argument, and Kelp is out of scope): Tidewatch draws its own Restart pill once the update is installed and drives the restart itself. The old window starts the new one with `--handoff <socket>` (`open -n -a <bundle> --args ...` from a bundle, forwarding `TIDEWATCH_HOME` and `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND` as `--env`; the executable directly otherwise), sends the layout (repo groups, tabs in order, each group's selected tab, the group in front, focus mode, per tab its worktree, title and, for an agent, profile, level, session id, first-prompt state, notes file, workflow run file with timeout and stage generation) and the master fd of every live pty (stdusk `641ceee`, `stdusk_core::handoff`). The new window adopts them instead of restoring the session, answers ACK only when every tab is live, then arms teardown. Agent tabs are not resumed: the same process runs on. The agent's launch baked the old hook listener's port and token into its hooks and MCP address, so the layout carries the port and each agent's token (`Listener::credential_of`), and the new window binds the same port (retrying while the old process leaves, 5 s) and adopts each token under the tab's new id (`Listener::adopt_tab`). Notes, reader feeds and workflow runs re-attach from their files. The old window changes nothing until the ACK: every failure (no socket, successor never connects, protocol mismatch, no ACK) leaves it open with a notice and `closing` is cleared again.
  The single-instance socket: the old window answers `closing` for the whole hand-over, and the new window claims the socket only after the ACK with `instance::claim_replacing`, which sends nothing and waits (up to 10 s) until the old one stops answering, so a folder is never handed to a window that is leaving. A launch that cannot adopt follows stdusk's `fallback`: give way when the old window still answers, open a normal restored window when nothing does. A window with a tab being made or removed, or a workflow stage starting or running its tests, refuses to restart (that work cannot move) and says so.
  Numbers: 1,382 tests passing (815 app, 486 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 17 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), 36 more than the 1,346 before (today's total minus the tests added); `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS (Rust 1.93 here, CI uses 1.99, not run). New tests: the layout and its order, which tabs can travel and what blocks a restart, pairing terminal N with live entry N, agent and workflow tabs rebuilt (reader session, credential under a new id, stage generation, a lost workflow file, an adoption that fails), the layout crossing the real wire with pipes, a real pty round trip in one process (three shells cross a socket, the same pids come back in the same tabs and outlive the sender), the boot and fallback decisions, the successor command from a bundle and directly, the Restart pill (one click, one request), the listener serving the old port and honouring the old token, `claim_replacing`, `--handoff`, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_RESTART`.
  Checked live in the background (`TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND=1`, `TIDEWATCH_HOME=/tmp/twhand/h`, throwaway repo, stand-in `claude`, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_RESTART=14`; everything started was killed): the old window handed over and exited; the three ptys (main shell, a worktree shell, the stand-in agent) stayed alive with the same pids, now children of launchd; the new window had the same three tabs and the same session id (no second agent started); a hook posted to the old URL with the old token after the restart returned 200 and moved that tab's status to Done; `tidewatch --toggle` reached the new window (it owns the app socket); on its own quit the new window stopped all three. With a `TIDEWATCH_HOME` too long for a socket path the restart was refused ("restart cancelled"), the window and its shell stayed. **Not verified:** the real Restart click (a foreground window takes focus), `open -n -a` from a real `.app` (including `--env`, which needs a recent macOS), a real claude, codex, gemini or copilot across a restart, an MCP call after the restart, a successor that connects and then fails mid-way, Linux (the code is unix-only and built for it, not run). **Known limits:** pty bytes the old window's reader threads read between the screen capture and its exit are lost; hook posts between the old listener closing and the new one binding are refused; if the hook port stays busy the agents keep running but cannot report (a notice says so); a workflow stage that is starting or testing blocks the restart instead of moving. See PLAN.md, Deviations log, 2026-10-15 live handoff.

- **v0.1.2.** Ships layout C (#40) and drops the seeded YOLO profiles (#39).

On load, a `*-builder-yolo` profile that is exactly what older versions seeded is removed and `profiles.toml` is rewritten atomically; anything edited, renamed or user-made stays, and an untouched file is never rewritten. The match is pure (`Profiles::drop_seeded_yolo`). Seeded workflows never named a YOLO profile. A saved tab of a removed profile reopens as a shell with a note.

## 2026-10-14

- **New-tab flow rebuilt to the owner-approved design (branch `ux-new-tab`, off `main`, not pushed).** The owner: the prompt should not be asked in the dialog, there should be a choice between agent and workflow, and the prompting should happen in the tab. Cmd+T now opens two cards (Agent A, Workflow W) and Plain shell (S); Agent opens a filterable list of profiles grouped by CLI with the picked row open on its five levels (Left and Right change it); Workflow lists workflows; Enter opens the tab at once. No task field and no command preview in the dialog (the flags of a level are the tooltip of its chip). An agent tab opens ready ("Builder is ready", a composer, a status line with Ready, profile, CLI, level, branch from base and the Reader or Terminal choice) and its first message goes in like a Reader reply. A workflow tab opens with the stepper all pending, a card for the task and the stages and Start run (Cmd+Enter); nothing runs before. A window launched with no repo (Finder, nothing remembered) shows a welcome screen with Open repo (new Cmd+O, Ctrl+Shift+O on Linux), the repos used before and the command line hint. Choices, the colors and what differs from the mockups are in the PLAN.md Deviations log; `.agents/rules/project.md` has the new modules and dev switches (`TIDEWATCH_DEV_DIALOG=choose|agent|workflow`, `_TASK` types into the composer or task box, `_START=1` sends it).

- **Codex checked against the real CLI (codex-cli 0.162.1, branch `codex-live`, off `main`, not pushed).** Codex is now a verified agent. Four things were wrong and are fixed: the Auto level passed `--sandbox` with `--approve-for-me` (Codex rejects the pair); the MCP server needed `default_tools_approval_mode="approve"` or its tools were refused under approval `never`; the transcript parser read neither `custom_tool_call` items nor their list-of-parts output; and the app neither passed `CODEX_HOME` and the start time to the locator nor saved the session id, so a Codex tab could not resume. Verified live: launch flags per level, hooks (SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PermissionRequest, Stop) in `exec` and the TUI, `stage_done` over MCP, the never-push guard in Codex's shell, the rollout locator, the parser on a redacted real rollout (`codex_live.jsonl`), `codex resume <id>` recalling the earlier turn, the folder trust rules (a parent does not cover a worktree; the main repo and the worktree itself do), `developer_instructions`, and an app run in the background (Reader shows the answer; the relaunch resumed the session). Details and the evidence per check are in PLAN.md ("Codex live checks").
  `live_check` now also drives Codex (through `codex exec`) and wires the push guard.
  Not verified: Codex's permission prompt keys (the Reader shows "Switch to terminal" for Codex), Ask and Auto edit in a TUI that actually asks, YOLO, Linux. Open: a seeded Codex YOLO builder now exists like for the other verified CLIs.

- **Phase 10, step 1: one window, several repos.** `tidewatch <other repo>` no longer starts a second process: there is one socket for the app, every launch hands its folder to the window and exits, and the window adds the repo as a group of tabs with its own pinned main shell and its saved tabs. The tab strip shows only the repo on show (the concept's "tabs never mix repos"); a repo chip that is a menu, `Cmd+Ctrl+]` / `[` (Linux `Alt+]` / `Alt+[`, a proposal) and palette rows switch; a dot on the chip says an agent in another repo needs you. The Overview lists every repo. Session save writes one entry per repo from its own group; restore plans each repo's saved window on its own job and puts the tabs in that group without taking the window from the repo in front; the saver stays quiet until every repo is back. A pathless launch (Finder, update restart) reopens the front repo and the others that had tabs open (`also` in `last-workspace.toml`; an old file still loads). Choices and what is left (no close-repo action at that point) are in the PLAN.md Deviations log.
- **Phase 10, step 1: the repo menu.** The chip is always a menu. Open repo... runs `rfd`'s folder dialog on a job thread and hands the folder to the same path as `tidewatch <path>`. Close repo closes the repo on show tab by tab through the normal close path (same confirmations); a kept, cancelled, failed or starting tab leaves it open, the last repo cannot be closed, and the closed repo is saved with only its main shell and left out of `last-workspace.toml`. Palette rows for both, no new chord. Unit-tested; the dialog and a click on the menu are not checked live.
  Numbers: 1,229 tests passing (681 app, 468 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 15 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 1,204 (659 app, 466 core measured on a clean export of the base commit); `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. New tests: tab groups (ids, selection, closing inside a group, stepping, quiet selection), a save and a fingerprint per group, the writer with two repos, the restore of a second repo into its own group (first repo keeps the window, focus only from the first, the saver waits for a repo added later), the Overview with two places, the remembered list and its old-file load, the hand-over of another repo, the palette rows and chords. The single-instance test `a_stale_socket_from_a_crash_is_taken_over_by_the_next_launch` failed once under the full suite and passed on 6 reruns alone (a forking test thread can inherit the dropped listener on macOS); it is not new.
  Checked in the background (`TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND=1`, short throwaway `TIDEWATCH_HOME` and `HOME`, two throwaway repos): a window for `web` with one worktree tab; `tidewatch api` handed over in 15 ms and the window added `api` (screenshot: chip `api`, its own main tab, History from `api`); the quit-time save held both repos; a pathless launch from `/` with a seeded `last-workspace.toml` (`dir` api, `also` web) opened both, web kept its worktree tab, and selecting web's main tab switched the strip. **Not verified:** a real foreground window, the chip menu opened by a click, the next and previous repo chords pressed (only driven as synthetic events in tests), the attention dot with a real waiting agent, Linux, `last-workspace.toml` written at a real quit (background runs never write it; the choice of its content is unit tested).
  Open: no way to close a repo before quitting; no in-window picker to open a folder; two repos with the same folder name show the same chip label.

- **Phase 10, step 2: the restart after an update keeps your repo and agents.** Kelp's Restart button runs `open -n <bundle>` with no argument, so the new copy starts in `/`, like a Finder launch, and used to open a window for `/`. Now a launch with no path and no git repo around it reopens the folder of the window used last (`.state/last-workspace.toml`, written when a window gains focus and when it closes), and session restore brings its tabs back and resumes the agents as before. A path on the command line or a working folder inside a repo still wins; a remembered folder that is gone falls back to the old behavior. The pick is `tidewatch_core::last_workspace::pick`, pure and tested.
  **The single-instance race is real once that works.** The new copy and the old window now share a folder, so they share a socket, and `open -n` starts the new copy before the old window has finished closing. The new copy connected to the dying window, handed over its folder and exited: nothing opened. Fix, all in Tidewatch: every command on the socket is now answered `ok` or `closing`; a window sets `closing` on the close request and in `on_exit`, and keeps the socket until it is dropped, after the session flush and after its terminals are gone. A launch that hears `closing` retries every 50 ms for up to 10 s and takes the socket over (then runs without single-instance if it never frees). An older build that answers nothing counts as `ok`; a probe never waits. Changed: `instance.rs`, `last_workspace.rs`, `last_used.rs`, `single_instance.rs`, `shell.rs`, `app.rs`, `main.rs`, `paths.rs`. No Kelp change.
  Numbers: 1,204 tests passing (659 app, 466 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 15 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 1,185; `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` clean on macOS. New tests: the folder pick for every input (argument, repo, remembered, gone, nothing), the file round trip and a corrupt file, the focus tracker, the reply line, `ok` then `closing` then `ok` again, a launch that waits for a closing window and takes over, gives up after the patience, a probe that does not wait, an older window that never replies, and the whole restart launch in `single_instance`.
  Checked: a throwaway test on the old code showed the launch exiting beside a window that was leaving. A `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND=1` run from `/` in a throwaway home with `last-workspace.toml` seeded opened that repo (its `session.toml` window was the repo). **Not verified:** a real Restart click after a `brew upgrade` (no release exists to install), and the race with a real foreground window (it takes focus, and background runs never hand over, so the wait-and-take-over path is covered by tests on the socket only).
  Open: `tidewatch` typed in a plain folder that is not a repo (not `/`) now reopens the last workspace too; `tidewatch .` still opens that folder. If Kelp's `restart` ever passes the folder (`open -n --args`), "last used" can become exact.

A pill in the top bar ("Update to X" with a cask install, "Tidewatch X is out" otherwise, "Restart to update" when installed) and an Updates section in Settings, General (status line, Check now, the pill, "Check for updates automatically", and "Install updates in the background" only where there is an installer). - **Phase 10, step 1 and the `--toggle` part of step 5: quake window, one instance, menu-bar item.** A second `tidewatch <path>` for a folder that already has a window sends the path over a Unix socket (`.state/instances/<hash>.sock`, so `TIDEWATCH_HOME` runs never meet the real one) and exits before opening anything; the running window comes forward. A stale socket from a crash is taken over. `tidewatch --toggle` shows or hides every running window and exits 1 when none answers, for Wayland and scripts. In quake mode (the default, like stdusk) the window is borderless, the monitor's width and half its height, summoned and dismissed by a global hotkey (default `Ctrl+Alt+Grave`, so stdusk's `Ctrl+Grave` can run beside it), hides when you click elsewhere, and hides the way stdusk's does on macOS (2 point sliver, alpha 0, never ordered out). A macOS menu-bar item offers Show / Hide, Overview, Settings and Quit. Settings, General, has a Window section: drop-down on or off, the hotkey (checked as you type, applied on leaving the field only when it parses, with the registration result under it) and the menu-bar item, saved in the `[window]` table of `settings.toml`.
  **The background rule is code, not habit:** `shell_plan::Plan` is the one gate. A screenshot or `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND` run gets a plain window, no hotkey, no menu-bar item, no activation (no Focus, show, hide or move command, no Dock policy call). A headless test drives the whole shell under a recorder with everything switched on and asserts it made no OS call and sent no window command; a second test proves the plan for every combination of settings and platform. Background runs also never hand over to a real window: they skip single-instance unless `TIDEWATCH_HOME` is set, and even then only probe.
  Provenance: the quake geometry, hide rules, `apply_visibility` shape, hotkey spellings, the socket start-up decision and the tray are ported from stdusk `43aba30d` (`src/main.rs`, `src/macos.rs`, `src/tray.rs`, `src/instance.rs`, `src/config.rs`); stdusk exports none of them from a library crate, so they are re-written here, with the tray click handler (instead of per-frame polling) and a strict hotkey parser as the differences. Numbers: 1,126 tests passing (605 app, 442 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 15 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 1,033; `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` clean.
  Checked in the background with a throwaway `TIDEWATCH_HOME` (short path, see below): a screenshot of the General page with the Window section; a background instance answered `--toggle` (exit 0, logged), a second `tidewatch <repo>` (exit 0 in 15 ms, no window, logged `open <repo>`), the same from inside the repo with `.`; `--toggle` with nothing running and with another home exits 1; after `kill -9` the socket file stayed and a new launch took it over; a graceful close removed it. **Not checked live, by design (they take focus):** quake show and hide, the hotkey, the menu-bar item, switching quake on and off in settings, and anything on Linux (no Linux toolchain here; the macOS-only code sits behind `cfg` stubs and `global-hotkey` 0.8 is the sibling's version).
  Open: a Unix socket path is limited to about 100 bytes, so a `TIDEWATCH_HOME` under macOS `$TMPDIR` (`/var/folders/...`) is too long and runs without single-instance, with a line on stderr; use a short one such as `/tmp/x`. More than one repo window means more than one process: the hotkey belongs to the first, and a press or `--toggle` toggles every window together.

- **Phase 10, step 2 (app half): Tidewatch puts your tabs back.** Every change to the window (a tab opening, closing or moving, an agent starting, a workflow stage launching, the active tab, focus mode) is written to `.state/session.toml` by a debounced thread (0.4 s after the last change, at most 2 s after the first), and the quit path writes it synchronously from `on_exit`. At launch, once the profiles are loaded, one job reads the session, repairs a moved repo, plans every tab against the disk (`plan_window`) and scans for leftovers; the app then opens the tabs in saved order: a shell in its worktree, a shell with the reason printed above it, an agent through `prepare_resume` (a fresh listener token registered first, the worktree's folder trust granted again when the user agreed, never YOLO) or a workflow run through `restore_workflow`. The active tab and focus mode come back unless the user already opened tabs. What did not come back as it was shows in a notice that stays until Dismiss. `--no-restore` and a new General settings page (default on) turn it off. The saver stays quiet until the restore is done, so quitting during a restore keeps the old session.
  The leftover chip is now a button. "N leftover" counts worktrees that no open tab and no saved tab (of any window) owns; its dialog looks at each one and lists what removing it would lose. Clean up removes the clean ones, deletes the branch only when no commit lives only there, and a worktree with uncommitted changes is refused until its own Discard is pressed. `app.rs` went from 1,400 to 1,414 lines (pty spawning moved out to `pty.rs`, the leftover chip and its tip moved out too; the restore, save and dev glue came in), still over the soft limit; the logic is in the new modules.
  Numbers: 1,033 tests passing (533 app, 421 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 15 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 942; `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` exit 0. New tests: building the saved window from tabs (every kind, a tab still being made, the active index, the Other group, workflow tabs) and its fingerprint, the debounce (pure) and the writer thread, the saver gate, the startup job (plan, owned and unowned leftovers, moved repo, the flag and the setting), the reopen job (resume command, listener token, folder trust with and without consent, a YOLO refusal), the apply glue with fake ptys (order, active tab, focus, a job finishing out of order, unsettled while an agent is made, a user's own tabs kept), the leftover model and its jobs on real repos (dirty refused, discard explicit, a branch with commits kept), the arguments, the settings page click and the notice.
  Runnable check (claude 2.1.296, haiku, Plan level, throwaway repo and `TIDEWATCH_HOME`, `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_FORCE_SESSION_PERSISTENCE=1`, never YOLO): run 1 opened the main tab, a plain worktree shell and a Claude tab ("The secret word is kiwi. Reply with just the word ok."), turned focus mode on, selected the shell tab and quit through the close request with a 10-minute debounce, so only the quit-time save could have written the file: `session.toml` held the three tabs, `active = 1`, `focus_mode = true`, the shell's worktree and the Claude tab's session id. Run 2 relaunched: the tabs came back in order, the shell tab was active with focus mode on, the Claude tab ran `claude --resume <the same id>` in its worktree, and "what word did I give you?" from the Reader's reply box was answered "kiwi" in the same transcript file. Run 3 resumed again from the re-saved session (the id survived a second round) and answered "kiwi" again. With the shell's worktree removed behind its back the tab was skipped and the notice said "Could not reopen 1 tab: sunny-reef (worktree folder gone)"; the active tab moved to the Claude tab. `--no-restore` opened only the main tab and the saved worktrees became leftovers; screenshot runs left `session.toml` untouched (byte for byte). SIGTERM to the app's own PID leaves the window as last written (the debounce had already written it); `on_exit` does not run for a signal. Leftovers dialog: Clean up removed the clean worktree and its empty branch and left the one with a commit and two uncommitted files, with its files intact.

- **D14: Tidewatch asks once, then stops the folder trust prompts.** On first launch a dialog lists each agent CLI file Tidewatch would edit and the line it gets; Yes applies, No records the choice and changes nothing, Esc is No. Settings has a new Folder trust page to apply or undo later. Copilot gets one `trustedFolders` entry for `~/.tidewatch` (parents cover worktrees; skipped when an ancestor is already listed). **Claude's trust does not cover worktrees** (checked: the lookup stops at the git root), so Claude (and Codex, unverified) get an entry for each new worktree just before the agent starts, removed when the worktree is deleted. Gemini needs no file: its launch variable already does it. Edits are text splices (comments and formatting kept), refuse files that do not parse, keep the original's permissions, re-read before the rename, and leave `<file>.tidewatch-bak` before the first change. Undo removes only what Tidewatch added. Files follow `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `COPILOT_HOME` and `CODEX_HOME`.

- **Phase 10, steps 4 and 5 (release half): the release is packaged and the workflow is written, nothing tagged.** `scripts/bundle-macos.sh` builds the arm64 and x86_64 release binaries, joins them with `lipo` into `Tidewatch.app` (`dev.hoboware.tidewatch`, `packaging/Info.plist`, version stamped from `Cargo.toml`), ad-hoc signs it (`SIGN_IDENTITY` overrides) and zips it with `ditto` into `target/dist/`. `scripts/package-linux.sh` builds `tidewatch-<version>-x86_64-linux.tar.gz` (binary, README, LICENSE, `.desktop`, icon) and, when `appimagetool` is on the PATH or in `APPIMAGETOOL`, an AppImage; it refuses to run off x86_64 Linux. `.github/workflows/release.yml` runs on `v*` tags and manual dispatch: a `meta` job checks the tag against `Cargo.toml`, `macos` (macos-14, tests, both targets, bundle) and `linux` (ubuntu-latest, apt packages as in `ci.yml`, appimagetool 1.9.1 pinned by checksum) build in parallel, `release` writes `SHA256SUMS` and the filled-in cask and cuts the GitHub Release, and `tap` commits `Casks/tidewatch.rb` to `Hobo-Ware/homebrew-tap` only when the repository variable `PUBLISH_TAP` is `true` (unset while the repo is private). `packaging/tidewatch.rb` is the reference cask, `packaging/README.md` the release flow and one-time setup. Icon: a placeholder tide-wave (`assets/icon.svg`, `scripts/make-icon.sh`, `Tidewatch.icns`, `icon-512.png`; 512px PNG 8.0K to 6.1K at 58.6 dB PSNR), to be replaced by real art.
  Runnable checks (macOS 26, arm64, nothing launched): `scripts/bundle-macos.sh` exit 0 in 1m53s; `lipo -info`: `x86_64 arm64`; `codesign -dv`: `Identifier=dev.hoboware.tidewatch`, `Signature=adhoc`, `Format=app bundle with Mach-O universal (x86_64 arm64)`; `codesign --verify --deep --strict` valid; `plutil` shows version 0.1.0 and the bundle id, executable and icon names; bundle 55M, zip 24M. `package-linux.sh` refuses on macOS with a plain message; its tarball and AppDir steps ran against stand-in `uname`, `cargo` and `appimagetool` (tarball contents, `AppRun` link, `ARCH` and extract-and-run env, the skip message when the tool is missing). The workflow loads as YAML (ruby and PyYAML); the cask passes `ruby -c`; the cask version and checksum substitution was run by hand.

Still open in steps 4 and 5: self-update, the site, a Linux update path and `tidewatch --toggle`.
- **Phase 9, app half: a workflow runs in a tab from Work to Hand off.** The new-tab dialog lists Ship a feature, Fix a bug, Review a PR and the user's own next to the profiles (repo windows only), asks for a task and previews every stage (agent, level, gate, loop). Starting makes the worktree (a job that also finds the base branch, the repo's test command and its timeout), then each `StartStage` launches that stage's agent in the same tab: the old pty is dropped, the new one prints `--- Stage 2: Review (gemini, Read only) ---` first (`, round 2` after a loop) and the Reader keeps the earlier rows with the same divider row. Every launch registers the tab again with a higher generation (core addition: `TabRegistration`/`TabMessage` carry it, the old token is revoked), and the run moves its own number the moment the runner starts a stage, so the Stop hook the previous agent sends right after `stage_done` is dropped (seen in the live log). `RunTests` runs on a job with a timeout (core addition `run_in_timeout`: kills the process group; `test_timeout_secs` in `.tidewatch.toml`, default 600 s). Findings still become notes and also go to the runner. The run is saved to `stages.json` after every event. A header on the tab shows the stepper (todo, running, needs you, done, failed per stage, `loop 1/2` on the stage that sent work back, the gate, what the run is doing), a clicked stage's summary and findings, and, when the runner asks, a card with the choices per reason: Approve, Retry, Reject with feedback (a reason field), Stop; a missing test command asks for one in a field. A stopped run says why. The tab's status is held by the run while it has the next move (starting a stage, running tests, a question, the end). The Overview row shows `Ship a feature: Review 2/4`. A finished run offers the Phase 8 delivery card with the last stage's summary as the PR text. Settings get a Workflows page (list, stages, every stage field, problems shown next to the fields, Save refused until every workflow can run). A saved run resumes with `TIDEWATCH_DEV_RESUME=<worktree name>` (session restore is not wired yet). Dev switches: `TIDEWATCH_DEV_WORKFLOW`, `_RESUME`, `_ASK`, `_OPEN_STAGE`, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SETTINGS=workflows`, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_DIALOG=workflow:<id>`.
  Also fixed: in the new-tab dialog a refused Enter used to land on the Cancel button (egui moved keyboard focus with the arrow keys and the field lost it); Enter is now taken before the field sees it and the field keeps the vertical arrows. Latent since Phase 6, found because "a workflow needs a task" made refusals common.
  Numbers: 869 tests passing (433 app, 357 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 15 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 745; `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` all exit 0. New tests: the timeout (a hung command and its children are killed), generations on the listener, the run state (every ask reason, stale messages by generation, held status, save errors, resume), the stepper model, the ask card model, the scripted Ship a feature flow through the app glue (a real throwaway repo and worktree, fake agents answering through generation-stamped messages: loops once, reaches Hand off, saved run reloads in a new tab and approves), failed launches and failed processes, the launch job against a live listener (the old token gets 401), the dialog (workflow rows, task required, Enter), the workflow editor state (round trip of the defaults, relinking on remove and move, nine kinds of problem) and its headless render.

A failing test command asked "the gate failed, tests failed (exit 1)" with Retry, Pass anyway, Stop. (2) real Claude 2.1.296, haiku, three stages (trimmed Ship a feature, Work at Auto edit with `git add` and `git commit` allowed, Review at Read only, Hand off at Read only): the reviewer caught the planted bug in `max` (all-negative input returns 0) with a blocking finding, the run looped to Work with the finding in its prompt, Work fixed `max` and added a test, the second review reported nothing, and Hand off waited for approval with the PR text as its summary (`real3-final.png`). With a looser review prompt ("find bugs in any function") the same reviewer found new bugs every round and the run reached its loop limit, which is the stop-and-ask path working.
