- **Phase 8, Reader view (D12): agent tabs open as a readable transcript.** Claude, Gemini and Copilot tabs show the agent's own session file as markdown (`egui_commonmark` 0.24): your turns in a bubble, the agent's answers as markdown with headings, lists and highlighted code blocks, each tool call as one collapsed line (name and the telling argument, a tick, cross or dots for done, failed or running; click to see the output, cut at 40 lines), errors in red. The view follows new rows until you scroll up, then shows "Jump to latest". A reply box at the bottom pastes your text into the pty and presses Enter 0.3 s later, like a note send. When the agent waits on a tool permission, a strip shows Allow and Deny (Claude: the keys `1` and Esc) and the reply box is off, because Enter in a menu would pick its default; for a question or a CLI whose keys are not verified it says to answer in the terminal and offers "Switch to terminal". Cmd+Shift+T flips the active agent tab between the Reader and the raw terminal (the pty runs underneath either way; per tab; the main tab and plain shells have no Reader). The Reader appears once the transcript is found; until then, and for Codex (unverified) and custom agents, the terminal shows with a small note saying why, so a folder-trust prompt is never hidden. The end-block card works under the Reader.
  How it works: Tidewatch now gives every agent that takes `--session-id` a random UUID at launch (new `tidewatch_core::session_id`), because the locator needs it. Each Reader tab has a thread that finds the file (every 0.5 s for 30 s, then every 5 s; "no transcript" is shown after 20 s) and then tails it. It reads when poked (the pty printed something, at most every 0.3 s; any hook, which also hands it the hook's `transcript_path`), and otherwise on a backoff 0.4 s, 0.8 s, 1.6 s up to 15 s while the file stays quiet. It wakes the UI only when entries arrive. Memory: the Reader keeps the newest 400 rows (a counter says how many older ones are not shown); rows far outside the viewport are not drawn (measured heights, estimates for rows never drawn), one markdown cache per tab. A tool call and its result are one row.
  Fixes found on the way: (1) a hook's last message was cut at 400 characters, so the Done / Needs you / Next card vanished for any answer longer than that (the block sits at the end); the Stop message is now kept up to 20,000 characters (notifications and the Overview only use its first line). (2) `scroll_to_cursor` every frame kept egui repainting at full speed (87 % CPU on a Done tab); the view now scrolls down only when a row arrives or the content height changes. (3) Gemini's own "Workspace Directories" message was shown as a user turn; the parser skips it. (4) After a denied prompt the status stays NeedsYou (no hook says it was answered), so the Reader hides its buttons after one answer until the next hook; a second tap would type into an idle input.
  Dependency bump: kelp `ef47f28`, stdusk `43aba30` (kelp-ui `fonts::extend` replaces the semibold-family registration and the startup font swap; Tidewatch passes its own `DiffTexts`: notes go to this tab's agent).
  Numbers: 745 tests passing (318 app, 349 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 14 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree), up from 700; `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` all exit 0. New tests: the entry to row mapping (merge of call and result, cap, a result for a dropped call), tool line text, autoscroll state, height cache, the permission key table, the toggle per tab and what is shown for every link state, the retry and backoff schedule plus a real feed on a temp home (file appears late, a poke reads at once), the reply box Enter, blank Enter, focus once (headless egui), a settled Reader asks for no continuous repaint, the dev switches.
  Runnable checks (claude 2.1.296 haiku at Ask level, gemini 0.46.0, copilot 1.0.96; throwaway repos and `TIDEWATCH_HOME`, `TIDEWATCH_BACKGROUND=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_FORCE_SESSION_PERSISTENCE=1`): a live Claude tab in the Reader showed the markdown answer (heading, three bullets, a four-line Python block with highlighting), a collapsed `Read` line, the end-block card, and the permission strip with Allow, Deny and Switch to terminal. Real keys: `1` created `hello.txt` (tab NeedsYou to Working to Done), Esc did not (the tool row turned red, the transcript got "[Request interrupted by user for tool use]", the buttons went away). Cmd+Shift+T (injected through the real key path by `TIDEWATCH_DEV_READER_KEY`) showed the raw terminal with the note "Raw terminal. Cmd+Shift+T for the Reader". Without the env var, the child Claude wrote no transcript (this session is itself a Claude session) and the tab fell back to the terminal with "No transcript found, showing the terminal". Gemini and Copilot tabs also showed their answers in the Reader. Idle CPU with a Reader tab open and the agent Done: 0.01 s of CPU over 15 s (`ps` cputime 0:02.29 to 0:02.30, debug build), same as the raw terminal; before the repaint fix it was 13 s over 15 s.
  Not verified: Linux; Gemini and Copilot permission buttons (no keys listed, so they show "Switch to terminal"); a transcript of hundreds of megabytes (the first read of `Tail` loads the whole file once, on the feed's thread); the reply box in a Copilot tab while it runs (Copilot's status was NeedsYou(Quiet) after answering). Open: the exit notice and the reply box overlap when the agent has exited under the Reader; headings use the regular weight (the markdown viewer does not use the semibold family); the Reader fills only the terminal area (the top 45 % with History and Diff open, so use focus mode, Cmd+., for a tall view).
  Screenshots (scratch only, not committed, in the session scratchpad): `reader-md.png`, `reader-tool.png`, `reader-endcard.png`, `reader-perm.png`, `reader-allow.png`, `reader-deny.png`, `reader-toggled-raw.png`, `fallback-no-transcript.png`, `reader-gemini.png`, `reader-copilot.png`.

- **Phase 8, app half: notes reach the agent, finished work becomes a PR.** The Diff pane gets a notes strip under its toolbar: one row per open note (every file) with where it stands (`queued`, `sent 3 min ago` with the UTC time on hover, `replied`, `from agent`), Send (Resend once sent), Post to PR (Reply on PR for a thread already on GitHub) and a click on `path:line` to jump to the file. The header has "Send all open (N)" and the per-tab "Deliver when idle" switch. A send pastes one compact markdown block per note into the agent's terminal (bracketed paste, then Enter 0.3 s later) and ends with a line asking for `reply_to_note`; control characters are stripped, so a pulled PR comment cannot end the paste early. A send is refused while the agent waits on you (the paste would answer its prompt); Deliver when idle sends only once the agent is Done, never while Working or NeedsYou, and holds until the agent leaves Done (or 10 s) so one idle moment sends once. Sends read the notes file first and write it back under one lock, so an agent's reply written behind the app's copy survives. A Done worktree agent with commits ahead gets a card above the panes ("N commits ready" - Push and open PR); the dialog shows the commits and an editable title and text (prefilled from `propose` and the last `stage_done`), Cmd+Enter approves, Esc cancels, and the outcome is the clickable URL or the typed error with a plain fix (`brew install gh`, `gh auth login`, rebase after a rejected push, no remote). The branch's open PR shows as a "PR #7 open" chip (a click opens it) with "Pull PR comments" (reloads the notes, `fetch_threads`, `merge_into`, saves); the lookup runs on first focus of a tab (60 s refresh), after a delivery and never per frame. "Post to PR" names the PR and repo in a confirm (Enter posts, Esc cancels) and shows gh's message if GitHub refuses; it is disabled with a reason when the note's line is not in the PR diff (read once per PR with `git diff base...head`). The agent's Done / Needs you / Next block (parsed from the Stop hook's last message) is a compact card above the panes that opens into three columns, and a line under the tab's Overview row. All git, gh and file work runs on jobs; nothing was added to the idle frame budget (the only timers are the hold end and a pending Enter).
  Numbers: 661 tests passing (275 app, 314 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 14 listener, 12 PR, 2 PR lines, 1 push guard, 16 worktree), up from 566 (206 app, 290 core unit); 699 after rebasing onto `main` with Phase 10 core; `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` all exit 0. New tests: paste text (incl. escape stripping), sent marks and note state, the end-block parser (markup variants, wrapped bullets, trailing prose, misordered), PR diff lines (unit and a real repo), the send gate, the deliver-when-idle state machine (every status, hold, no double send), `decide` for probe, focus lookup and auto send, the delivery card transitions and fix hints, the PR chip and post eligibility, jobs against a fake `gh` script (send keeps an agent's reply, pull is idempotent, post links the thread, a 422 comes back as gh says it), and headless egui frames for the post confirm (Enter, Esc, no repeat while posting), the delivery dialog (Cmd+Enter, Esc, in-flight cannot be dismissed), the end-block card click and the strip's Send and jump clicks.

With the PR already open, the chip, "Pull PR comments" (3 comments in 2 threads and a reply imported, each with Send and Reply on PR) and the post confirm ("Post to PR #7 in Hobo-Ware/demo?", the exact text) were captured. Screenshots (scratch only, not committed): `card.png`, `dialog.png`, `success.png`, `failure.png`, `prchip.png`, `confirm.png` under the session scratchpad `check/shots/`. Finding: in the confirm capture the strip's Post to PR was disabled because the fake `gh` answered the first `pr view` with HEAD at the base (the fake agent had not committed yet), so the PR diff read as empty; a real PR head only changes on a push, and a changed head drops the lines and reads them again.

- **Phase 10, core half: a saved session knows how to come back.** `tidewatch-core` gets `session.rs` (`.state/session.toml`: per window the repo or folder, the tabs in order as main, shell or agent with worktree, profile, level, agent, session id and workflow file, the active tab and focus mode; atomic save, a bad file loads as empty with a stderr note) and `restore.rs` (a pure planner from the saved session, the agent table, profiles and a `Disk` to one action per tab: plain shell, resume the agent, resume the workflow, a plain shell with the reason, or skip when the repo, folder or worktree is gone). `resume_command` is `launch_command` plus the CLI's resume form, so the guard environment, hooks and MCP wiring match a fresh launch; `prepare_resume` does the same through `prepare`. A YOLO tab is never resumed. Live Claude check: `--resume` found the session from the same folder and, unexpectedly, from other folders too (PLAN.md findings). Numbers: 546 tests passing (148 app, 322 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 14 listener, 12 PR, 1 push guard, 6 session restore, 16 worktree). Open: the app half (save on change and quit, plan and apply at startup, Codex id capture); Gemini and Copilot resume and Codex are not run live.
- **Phase 7, app half: you can tell at a glance which agent needs you.** The app starts the Tidewatch listener once at launch (a bind failure is logged once and status falls back to the terminal and process layers), registers every agent tab with its own token when the tab is made (the hook and MCP wiring in `prepare` now carries it) and revokes it when the tab closes. Each frame hook messages go to their tab's `StatusTracker`, and the pty's progress (OSC 9;4), title change, bell, output activity and exit code feed the same tracker. The tab shows the CLI's logo in stdusk's own slot and a status bar on its top edge: working moves (only while working), needs you is amber, done green, failed red, setting up dim blue. `Cmd+0` opens the Overview (needs-you tabs first, then each repo's tabs with status, title, branch, elapsed time and the agent's last message; a click jumps to the tab) and the strip has an icon button for it with an amber badge counting the tabs that need you. `Cmd+.` is focus mode: History and Diff hide, the terminal fills the window, and the hidden panes load nothing. While the window is unfocused or minimized, a tab that newly needs you, is done or failed posts a desktop notification (`osascript` on macOS, `notify-send` on Linux, off the UI thread, once per tab per kind of change in 60 s). `report_finding` becomes a note on that file and line in the tab's notes, `reply_to_note` makes the open view read the notes file again, and `stage_done` and the latest finding are kept per tab for the workflow UI. Core got one function: `status::next_change`, the next moment a status decays on its own, so a quiet tab costs no frames and the 8 s quiet rule and the 2 s prompt settle still fire on time.
  Numbers: 486 tests passing (206 app, 236 core unit, 13 history, 14 listener, 1 push guard, 16 worktree), up from 426 (148 app, 234 core); `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` all exit 0. New tests: status and notification mapping, the rate limiter, Overview rows and groups, an Overview row click in a headless egui frame, `next_change` checked against `resolve` for every decay, and an integration test that registers a tab on a live listener, posts a real Claude Notification payload and sees the tracker go to NeedsYou (and a closed tab's token refused).
  Runnable check (claude 2.1.296, haiku, throwaway repo and `TIDEWATCH_HOME`, debug build, Ask level, task "create a file hello.txt containing hi"; `TIDEWATCH_DEV_STATUS_LOG=1` prints hook and status times in ms): the `PermissionRequest` hook was drained and the tab turned `NeedsYou(Permission)` in the same frame (both lines at the same millisecond; the hook came 5.4 s after `UserPromptSubmit`, which is the model's time). The amber tab and the Overview with its badge are in the screenshots below. Claude's folder-trust prompt (it shows in a worktree it has not seen) turned the tab amber through the quiet rule 8.8 s after start, and the tab went back to Working when the answer printed. A finished turn flipped to Done on the Stop hook (12.6 s after start in the Edits run, green bar), and a later "waiting for your input" notice left it Done. Focus mode shows the terminal alone. Idle CPU with a Done agent tab open (no screenshot harness, `ps` cputime over 10 s windows): 0.00 s, 0.01 s and 0.05 s of CPU (0.0%, 0.1%, 0.5%); the 0.05 s window is probably the idle notice Claude sends about a minute after finishing (not confirmed). `osascript -e 'display notification ...'` exits 0 from this environment; the notifier itself is tested with a recording sender, and the "window hidden" path was not exercised live.
  Screenshots (not committed, scratch only): `ask.png` (amber tab, Claude permission prompt), `done.png` (green tab), `overview-needs2.png` (Needs you group, badge), `overview-needs.png` (Overview, done row), `focus.png`, all under the session scratchpad `check/shots/`.
  Gaps in stdusk (not changed): its notification helper is `pub(crate)` in the app crate, so `notify.rs` repeats the same two commands; `PtyTerm` has no output timestamp (only a one-shot `take_activity`, so "last output" is the frame that saw it), no "title changed" event (the app compares `title_osc()` each frame), and no bell or exit wake-up beyond the repaint the pty already asks for. All three were enough for the status rules. Open: only Claude was checked live in this half (Gemini and Copilot states are covered by the core fixtures); a Claude permission prompt gets a second `Notification` hook a minute later whose generic text replaces the specific "Write: <path>" detail in the Overview; `app.rs` is now about 1,090 lines (over the 1,000 soft limit); the Codex logo is stdusk's letter chip.
- **Phase 8, core half: delivery, PR comments and the Reader transcripts work without the UI.** `tidewatch-core` gets `delivery.rs` (D7: the commits a branch has on top of its base, a proposed PR title and text from the commits or the last stage summary, `deliver` = `git push -u origin <branch>` then `gh pr create --body-file`, returning the URL or a typed error: no remote, no GitHub remote, gh missing, gh not logged in, push rejected), `pr.rs` (D4: find the open PR with `gh pr view`, pull its review comments with `gh api ... --paginate` into notes threads, post a note or a reply as a review comment), `transcript.rs` (D12: one `Entry` model, parsers for Claude, Gemini, Copilot and Codex, and a `Tail` that returns only new entries and survives partial lines, truncation and replacement) and `transcript_locate.rs` (where each CLI keeps a tab's session file). `tools.rs` holds the shared `git`/`gh` runner, `rfc3339.rs` the timestamp parser. Numbers: 508 tests passing (148 app, 290 core unit, 14 delivery, 13 history, 14 listener, 12 PR, 1 push guard, 16 worktree), 84 new, all in core; `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` all exit 0. Verified live (claude 2.1.296 haiku, gemini 0.46.0, copilot 1.0.91, a throwaway repo each, a read-only tool prompt, never YOLO): the real session files were captured, redacted and are the parser fixtures; Claude's folder encoding was checked on a path with `_`, `.` and a space; Gemini and Copilot honour `--session-id` and the files appear where Phase 0 said. Delivery ran against a real bare remote with a fake `gh` (the real `gh` never ran): plain push, rejected push (a rival clone pushed the same branch), no origin, missing `gh`, no login, existing PR, non-GitHub remote. Not verified: Codex (parser and locator follow Codex's source, no real rollout was read), the real `gh pr create` and the review-comments endpoint against GitHub (argument shapes and JSON come from the documented schema and `gh` 2.98.0 error messages), Linux. Open for the app half: it must reload the tab's notes before merging pulled PR threads (same as `reply_to_note`); `Tail::poll` and the locator read disk, so run them on a job thread; the Gemini and Claude files may not exist for a second after launch, so poll the locator until it answers; `deliver` has no network timeout.
- **Phase 6, UI half: Cmd+T opens a dialog that starts a real agent.** Cmd+T and "+" open the new-tab dialog: a list of profiles from `profiles.toml` (seeded on first launch from the agents on PATH, each with its CLI version probed in the background, "not found on PATH" when missing) and a plain Shell, the five-step permission ladder (YOLO greyed out with its reason in Other, D11), an optional task, a live command preview and, in Other, a warning when another agent already runs in the folder. Enter starts, Esc cancels, Up/Down picks. Start makes the worktree and gets the agent ready on a job thread (house-style file, Copilot instructions copy, never-push helper, Gemini settings plus `info/exclude`), then the user's login shell runs `setup && exec <agent>` in the tab's pty, with `PATH` carrying the Homebrew and `~/.local/bin` dirs even when started from Finder. Only worktree agent tabs get the push guard; the main tab, Shell tabs and Other get none. The gear (Cmd+,) opens a small profile editor (list, name, agent, model, effort, level, setup, extra arguments, never push, house style) that saves `profiles.toml`; saving rewrites the file, so comments in it are lost. An agent tab whose process ended shows "<profile> exited with code N". Numbers: 297 tests passing (148 app, 119 core unit, 13 history round trips, 1 push guard, 16 worktree round trips), up from 215; headless egui frames cover Enter, Esc, arrow keys, typed task and the Other-group YOLO rule. Runnable check (claude 2.1.296, throwaway repo and `TIDEWATCH_HOME`, haiku, a one-word task): a Builder tab made `agent/lively-sparrow` (locked) and `ps -o args= -p $(pgrep -n claude)` showed `claude --permission-mode acceptEdits --model haiku --append-system-prompt-file <config>/house-style.md -- Reply with the single word ok. Do not touch any files.`; the tab's environment carried `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=1` and the `pushInsteadOf` entry. A "Shell probe" custom profile run through the same pty path printed the Tidewatch message and `exit=128` for `git push` and `git push --no-verify`, `exit=0` for `git fetch`, and the bare remote kept 0 refs. `TIDEWATCH_DEV_AGENT=builder TIDEWATCH_DEV_LEVEL=yolo` in an Other folder started nothing ("YOLO is not open here"). Screenshots (`TIDEWATCH_DEV_DIALOG`, `TIDEWATCH_DEV_SETTINGS`) checked by eye for the dialog in a repo, in Other, on a Copilot profile, and the editor. Findings: Claude shows its own folder-trust prompt in every new worktree (not answered by Tidewatch); Copilot has no supported per-session pre-trust (details and the one-time `~/.tidewatch` workaround in PLAN.md, Deviations log); zsh ends a `-c` script when `exec` cannot find its program, so the agent is looked up first. Not verified: Gemini, Codex and Copilot tabs started with a task (flags come from `--help`), the Gemini settings file in a live Gemini run, Linux, idle CPU with a running agent tab. Open: the hook listener and MCP fields stay unset until Phase 7; a repo that tracks `.gemini/settings.json` gets no house style for Gemini; tab strip text "N leftover" crowds the first tab (existing).
- **Phase 9, core half: workflows run as a state machine, every gate has a test.** `tidewatch-core` gets `workflows.rs` (`Workflow`, `Stage`, `Action`, `Gate`; `~/.config/tidewatch/workflows.toml`, bad file -> built-in defaults with a stderr note, seeded on first launch with a missing agent swapped for an installed one), `runner.rs` (a pure `Runner`: `RunEvent` in, `Command` out), `prompt.rs` (stage prompt placeholders) and `test_command.rs` (`<repo>/.tidewatch.toml` `test = "..."`, else cargo, npm/pnpm/yarn/bun, deno, go, pytest, rspec, mix or make; `run_in` runs it for real). Built-in workflows from the concept: Ship a feature (Work -> Review -> Refactor -> Hand off), Fix a bug (Reproduce -> Fix -> Review), Review a PR (Review -> Review -> Hand off). Gates are checked by Tidewatch: Tests, TestPasses and SameBehavior need exit 0, TestFails needs a non-zero exit, NoBlocking counts the stage's blocking findings, Approve waits for the user. A failed gate with `on_fail` loops back while under `max_loops` (default 2), then asks (D11). A stop without `stage_done` asks pass, retry or stop (D6). The run saves to `.state/<repo-dir>/<tab>/stages.json` and `resume()` repeats the command it was waiting on. Numbers: 344 tests passing (90 app, 210 core unit, 13 history, 14 listener, 1 push guard, 16 worktree), up from 267; 77 new, all in core. `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --all --check` all exit 0. The scripted Ship a feature run (fake test runner, first review reports a blocking finding) loops once, passes, reaches Hand off and waits; every step of it is saved, reloaded and resumed in the test. Not verified: no real agent was run through a workflow (the app half wires the runner to tabs, the listener and the test command), and `SameBehavior` is "tests pass" only (the prompt asks the agent to keep behavior). Open: a stale `Stop` hook from the previous stage's agent must be dropped by the app once the next stage started; a hung test command has no timeout; workflows list alphabetically by id (BTreeMap), so Ship a feature is last.
- **Phase 5: the bottom half of layout B works.** The History pane (bottom left) draws Kelp's commit graph for the active tab only: a worktree tab shows its branch and the commits under it, the main tab its checked-out branch. The header names the tab, says how far it is ahead of or behind the default branch (`2 ahead · 1 behind main`, from `origin/HEAD`, else `main`, else `master`) and has a This worktree / Whole repo switch. A live "Uncommitted changes" row sits on top. The Diff pane (bottom right) shows the selected commit or the working tree: header, file list, Unified/Split, jump arrows, word highlights, all from `kelp-ui`. Threads written in the diff are notes to the tab's agent in `~/.tidewatch/.state/<repo-dir>/<tab>/notes.json` (atomic write on a background thread, flushed on quit); nothing is sent to the agent yet (Phase 8). Double-clicking a branch chip checks it out only in the main tab and only when the tree is clean; a worktree tab shows a toast instead. A non-git folder (Other) now has no panes and a full-height terminal. Refresh is event-driven: Kelp's file watcher runs only for the visible tab, and history, status and diff reload on background threads, so an agent's commit shows up without input. Numbers: 159 tests passing (90 app, 40 core unit, 13 history round trips, 16 worktree round trips), up from 83. Runnable check on a throwaway repo and temp home (git 2.55.0): a worktree tab opened by the app, two commits and an uncommitted edit made behind its back appeared in the graph and the Diff pane; a note written on the main tab was in `notes.json` and showed again, on the same line, after a restart; idle CPU time stayed at 0:00.42 over 10 s (0.0%), RSS 182 MB in a debug build. On Kelp's own repo (242 commits, Whole repo) the panes filled within the 6 s capture wait. Not verified: the checkout toast and a real double-click (the screenshot harness cannot click; the rule and the git outcomes have tests). Open: Kelp's thread card is a fixed 640 pt wide and clips in a narrow pane, and says "Saved in this repo only" (Kelp wording); `kelp_ui::fonts::ensure_fallback` replaces the whole font set, so Tidewatch triggers it once at startup (non-Latin glyphs then rely on stdusk's fallbacks); commit summaries are read lazily for visible rows on the UI thread (cheap object reads, no git process); Alt+Up/Down also reaches the shell; Tidewatch tabs are not restored, so notes of a closed tab stay on disk but are not shown again; no avatars (offline store); no Overview, focus mode or send-to-agent yet.

- **Phase 7, core half: agents can now report to Tidewatch, and their status folds into one value.** `tidewatch-core` gets a loopback listener (`listener.rs`, `127.0.0.1:0`, started once by the app) with per-tab bearer tokens: `POST /hook/<agent>` takes hook payloads, `POST /mcp` is the Tidewatch MCP server (`mcp.rs`: `initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`, notifications answered 202, `GET` 405). A missing or unknown token is 401 on every path. Tools: `stage_done`, `report_finding` (events to the app), `list_notes`, `reply_to_note` (read and write the tab's `notes.json` through `notes.rs`, then an event). Bad arguments are JSON-RPC -32602, bad JSON -32700, and nothing panics on input. `status.rs` holds `AgentStatus`, per-CLI hook parsing and a pure `resolve` over three layers (hooks, terminal signals, process facts), with a small `StatusTracker` around it. HTTP is hand-rolled on `std::net` plus `httparse` (already in `Cargo.lock` through kelp): keep-alive, chunked bodies, `Expect: 100-continue`, 16 KiB head and 2 MiB body caps, 64 connections. `tiny_http` (0.12.0) was rejected: maintained forks advertise CVE fixes, so it is not the safe default, and the glue it would save is about the same size. The only new dependency is `httparse 1.10.1`. Tokens are 16 bytes from `/dev/urandom`.
  Status rules (my design, PLAN.md only says "decays"): a dead process wins (exit 0 Done, else Failed with the code); a hook state wins over terminal and process layers; `Working` with no output for `STATUS_QUIET_SECS` (8) becomes `NeedsYou(Quiet)`; a permission or question prompt flips back to `Working` once the terminal prints again 2 s after the hook (no hook reports the answer); an idle notice never turns `Done` back amber; with no hook state, progress, bell, title activity and quiet time decide.
  Verified live (claude 2.1.296 haiku, gemini 0.46.0, copilot 1.0.91, throwaway repos, `cargo run -p tidewatch-core --example live_check`, `launch_command` wiring, Auto edit level, never YOLO): **Claude** hook events arrived on the tab's token (UserPromptSubmit at 4.0 s, Stop at 8.0 s with the last message), `stage_done` with "hello" arrived at 6.9 s with no permission prompt, exit 0. At the Ask level with a `touch` request the PermissionRequest hook arrived at 6.2 s and the command stayed blocked. **Gemini** SessionStart, BeforeAgent, AfterAgent and SessionEnd arrived plus `stage_done` "hello" at 7.2 s. **Copilot** `stage_done` "hello" arrived at 9.8 s; no hook events, as Copilot has no hook wiring in v0. Hook payloads captured from the real CLIs (redacted) are the fixtures in the `status.rs` tests: Claude UserPromptSubmit, PermissionRequest, Notification (permission_prompt), Stop; Gemini SessionStart, BeforeAgent, Notification (ToolPermission), AfterAgent, SessionEnd; Copilot event types from real `events.jsonl` files.
  Findings: Claude's http `SessionStart` hook never fired, in `-p` and in an interactive run, so the first Claude status comes from `UserPromptSubmit` or the lower layers (the Phase 0 "recheck" is settled). No hook reports a permission being answered, hence the decay by output. Claude also probes `server/discover`; the -32601 answer is fine. Copilot's `assistant.turn_end` fires after every model round, so its Done is a final `assistant.message` (no tool requests) or `session.task_complete`, and its `permission.requested` / `permission.completed` pair gives NeedsYou and back; `parse_hook("copilot", ...)` reads `events.jsonl` lines, for the Phase 8 tailer. Numbers: 202 tests passing (45 app, 126 core unit, 14 listener integration, 1 push guard, 16 worktree), up from 139; this branch carries the notes store commit from `phase-5-panes` (and the kelp dependency commit under it), because `reply_to_note` needs `notes.rs`; both merge as no-ops when Phase 5 lands.
  Not verified: Codex (no `codex login`; its hooks are assumed to use Claude's event names), the `StopFailure` payload fields (`error`, `error_details`, from the docs, no real capture), a Gemini idle or question notice. Open for the app half: `reply_to_note` writes `notes.json` behind the app's in-memory copy, so the app must reload the file on `NoteReply` (or its next save overwrites the reply); `Finding` carries file, line and text only, the app builds the note's anchor from the diff; the listener closes the connection after a 401; `SettingUp` has no percentage (PLAN.md's pseudo-signature has `SettingUp(u8)`).

## 2026-10-12

- **Phase 6, core half: launch an agent with the right flags, pushing blocked.** `tidewatch-core` now holds the
  agent table (Claude, Codex experimental, Gemini, Copilot; five levels each, plus `[agent.<id>]` overrides and
  custom agents in `profiles.toml`), `profiles.toml` load/save with first-launch seeding from the agents on PATH
  (Builder = Auto edit, Reviewer = Read only, never-push on; a YOLO builder was seeded until `drop-yolo-profiles`), `launch_command` and the default `house-style.md`. YOLO is refused unless the tab is a worktree tab.
  The never-push guard is the D7 env setting appended after any existing `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` entries plus a
  `git-remote-tidewatch-no-push` helper on PATH. Verified live (claude 2.1.296, gemini 0.46.0, copilot 1.0.91,
  throwaway repos, a local MCP and hook listener): hook payloads arrived with the bearer header for Claude and
  Gemini, the `tidewatch` MCP tool was called without a prompt by all three, and the house style produced the
  Done / Needs you / Next block in all three. Push guard against a real bare remote (git 2.55.0): plain,
  `--no-verify`, explicit-URL, new-branch and `--force` pushes all exit 128 with the Tidewatch message, fetch
  works, the remote kept its 1 ref, and a push from a normal shell still lands. Numbers: 139 tests passing
  (45 app, 77 core unit, 1 push guard, 16 worktree round trip), up from 83. Findings: Claude `--permission-mode plan`
  blocks the Tidewatch MCP tools ("Cannot call mcp__tidewatch__stage_done while in plan mode"), so a Read only
  reviewer cannot call `stage_done` or `report_finding`; `--permission-mode dontAsk --allowedTools=mcp__tidewatch__*`
  allowed reads and the MCP call and denied writes, an override candidate for Read only (Bash is unchecked).
  Copilot still shows the folder-trust prompt with `--add-dir`, so the caller must answer it or pre-trust the
  worktree. Codex hooks, MCP and `developer_instructions` are encoded from the binary's config keys, not run
  (no `codex login`). Left for the UI half: new-tab dialog with command preview, settings editor, wiring
  `launch_command` into tabs (writing the MCP file, the Gemini settings and `info/exclude` lines, the helper dir),
  a PATH that includes Homebrew when the app starts from Finder, and the hook listener itself (Phase 7).
- **Phase 4: Cmd+T makes a worktree tab, closing it removes the worktree.** In a git repo, Cmd+T or "+"
  runs `git worktree add --lock -b agent/<adjective-noun>` under `~/.tidewatch/<repo>-<hash>/<name>` on a
  background thread and starts the shell inside it. Closing asks "Keep branch" or "Delete branch"; a
  worktree with uncommitted changes also offers "Keep worktree" and an explicit "Discard" (plain remove
  is refused), and unmerged commits are called out before a branch delete. In a folder that is not a git
  repo the window is in the Other group: Cmd+T opens a plain shell, no worktree. The main tab stays a
  plain shell in the real checkout. The repo chip shows the repo name. On startup a background job runs
  `git worktree repair` for stored worktrees whose repo moved and counts leftovers (worktree folders with
  no open tab), shown as "N leftover" with a path list on hover. Real-git findings (git 2.55.0) are in
  PLAN.md: a failed `add` can leave its branch or whole worktree behind, so add rolls both back, and
  `rev-list --all` hides unmerged commits while a worktree is checked out. Numbers: 83 tests passing
  (45 app, 22 core unit, 16 core round-trip against throwaway repos), up from 22. Runnable check on a
  throwaway repo and temp home: the worktree and `agent/*` branch appeared in `git worktree list`
  (locked); closing with "Delete branch" left no folder, no worktree entry and no branch; two earlier
  worktrees showed as "2 leftover". Open: no repo switching (one repo per window), no UI to remove
  leftovers, worktrees stay on disk when the window quits, `worktrees.toml` is read-modify-write
  without a lock when two windows run, Tidewatch tabs are not restored across launches yet.
- **Decisions confirmed one by one.** D1 to D13 reviewed with the owner. Changed on review: D4 (comments are notes to
  the agent and PR comments), D6 (MCP only), D7 (`pushInsteadOf` guard, Tidewatch delivers the push and PR), D10
  (Linux too, Ubuntu CI job added). Added: D12 (Reader view), D13 (house-style file). Verified live: Claude
  `--append-system-prompt-file`; Codex 0.162.1 flags from `--help`.
- **Phase 3: first running app.** `tidewatch [path]` opens a window for the git repo with a
  pinned main shell tab, Cmd+T / "+" for more shell tabs, stdusk's tab strip and terminal,
  and placeholder History and Diff panels (layout B, 45% / 55%). `TIDEWATCH_SCREENSHOT`
  harness works. Numbers: 22 tests passing (18 app, 4 core). Stdusk crates are path
  dependencies on the Phase 1 worktree. CI is not verified (path dependencies need the
  sibling checkout there).

## 2026-10-05

- Repo created. Concept mockup saved to `docs/concept.html`. Decided so far: name Tidewatch,
  its own repo, agent worktrees in `~/.tidewatch/<repo>/<tab>`, layout B with focus mode,
  stdusk's terminal engine shared through a library crate in the stdusk repo.
- Plan written (`PLAN.md`, 11 decisions, Phases 0 to 10) from three research reports in
  `docs/research/`. Agent rules ported from stdusk and Kelp into `.agents/rules/`, plus
  `workflow.md` for the plan and ledger protocol. Concept corrected: Codex `--full-auto` is
  deprecated, Copilot `--allow-tool write` confirmed valid.
